# Changelog

## 2026-10-18 — Editor upgrade: layout, scripting, reusable content, durable saves, lighter site

- Editor source is split into `editor/src/modules/` (assembled by `build/build-editor.js`).
- Layout: Hierarchy docked left, grouped Objects palette (legacy demo items in their own group), View menu for diagnostics, editor-only ground grid, collapsible Inspector cards with help behind "?" and Up/Down/Remove behind "⋯". Start Blank opens a playable scene (ground, player start, third-person player).
- Script component: per-object code with onStart/onUpdate/onTriggerEnter/onTriggerExit/onInteract/onClick/onStop, a `ctx` API, a highlighted code editor with templates and API reference, and a Script Console with object names and line numbers. Runs only in Play under the existing trust gate.
- Reusable content: linked named Materials (saving updates every linked object; hand edits make one unique), Update Prefab From This rebuilds every other instance while keeping per-instance overrides, and assets get default colliders on drop and Scatter (trees at the trunk, ground cover walk-through).
- Durable saves: projects, previous revisions, version history and the Prefab library live in IndexedDB with automatic migration from localStorage, cross-tab sync and a persistent-storage request. Several large projects now save where the second used to fail.
- Scene navigation: right-drag to look, hold it with W/A/S/D/Q/E to fly (Shift faster, wheel sets fly speed), middle-drag pan, Alt-drag orbit, Alt+right-drag zoom, distance-proportional wheel zoom from 0.5 m to 600 m with a far plane that follows, and F to frame the selection. Games keep the engine's default orbit limits (new opt-in `Camera.setOrbitOptions`).
- Surface snapping: dragged objects and drops from the Objects palette or Content Library rest on whatever is under the cursor (models by their triangles, cubes and ramps by their oriented boxes, terrain by its heightfield) instead of the Y = 0 plane; End drops the selection onto the surface below. Toggle in View ▾ → Snap to surfaces.
- Authoring picks use the real mesh/box under the cursor instead of an oversized bounding sphere, so a crate on a table selects the crate (Play keeps the generous gameplay pick).
- Copy / Cut / Paste (Ctrl+C / X / V, also in Ctrl+K): copies the selection with its children, behaviors and hierarchy; pastes under the mouse (resting on surfaces) or beside the original; works across levels, projects and tabs. Models, Textures, Sprites and Audio travel with the objects, and an id that means a different asset in the target project is renamed instead of reused.
- Drag assets into the scene: model files dropped from the desktop onto the viewport are imported (through the usual review) and placed where dropped, resting on the surface; images dropped onto an object become its texture; Texture library rows drag onto objects to apply them.
- Play From Here (▶ Here button, Shift+P, Ctrl+K): starts the level being edited with the player on the surface under the view (or the mouse) facing the editor's direction, skipping the Start Screen; respawns return there and Stop restores everything. P now plays, as the Play tooltip always claimed.
- Multi-object editing: with several objects selected the Inspector's new "Edit all" card sets Position / Rotation / Scale (blank when values differ), Color and Solid on every object at once, aligns (X/Y/Z low side, centre, high side), spreads evenly on X/Z, randomizes turn and size, and drops everything to the surface; each change is one Undo step and children follow their parents.
- Undo / Redo keep the selection: objects that still exist stay selected instead of everything being deselected.
- Animation timeline: the Property Animation editor docks at the bottom so the scene stays usable, with a dopesheet (track rows, keyframe diamonds, playhead). Drag the playhead, pose the object in the scene, press Key; drag diamonds to retime, Delete removes a key (never the object). Previously the editor covered the scene and Add Keyframe restored the authored pose before capturing.
- Scene links: every object dropdown in Behaviors, Graph nodes and Sequences has a 🎯 button: click it, then click the target in the scene. With one object selected the viewport draws arrows to the objects its logic targets (labelled with the actions) and dashed arrows from objects that target it, including off-screen ones; toggle in View ▾ → Logic links.
- Hierarchy tools: type icons on every row, rename in place (double-click a name or F2), double-click to frame an object, a right-click menu (Rename, Focus, Duplicate, Copy, Paste, Unparent, Delete), and the selected row scrolls into view when you pick objects in the scene.
- Drag-to-scrub number fields in the Inspector and animation tracks: press and drag left/right (Shift faster, Alt finer); a click still types; each scrub is one Undo step.
- Thumbnails for your own imported Models (Assets tab) and saved Prefabs, drawn in the browser with the same software renderer as the Content Library pictures (textured, lit, transparent background); drawn a few at a time when idle and cached.
- Script editor autocomplete: suggestions with one-line descriptions after ctx., ctx.entity., ctx.input., ctx.game., ctx.player. and vectors (including variables assigned from them), lifecycle hooks after "function " (inserted with their body), Ctrl+Space on demand; Enter keeps indentation.
- Move gizmo: a yellow XZ plane square drags an object along the ground in one motion at its own height; hold Ctrl while dragging to flip snapping for the moment (never saved).
- Corner snapping: hold V while dragging and the object's nearest bounding-box corner locks onto a nearby object's corner (within 16 px on screen), with a marker; walls, floors and crates line up edge to edge.
- Workflow tools: Copy Logic / Paste Logic (Hierarchy right-click, Ctrl+Shift+C/V) copies Behaviors, Script and Object Variables onto the selection, retargeting references to the source onto each receiver and never overwriting a script; Select Similar picks every object with the same model, prefab or base name; Shift+1…9 saves the scene view and 1…9 returns to it (per project and level).
- Hosted game pages share one cached, content-hashed engine/editor runtime: average page 7.6 MB → 1.8 MB, site 215 MB → 127 MB, upload ZIP 84 MB → 61 MB. Exports remain single-file.

## 2026-10-18 — Real-game controls and collision repair

- Third-person camera no longer spins. Movement was camera-relative while the chase camera followed the character's facing, so holding A or D ran the player in circles. The controller now owns the camera yaw: strafing goes straight, forward-diagonal input steers gently (1.6 rad/s), the character turns toward travel at a bounded 12 rad/s instead of snapping, and authored camera bearings are still respected. Added hold-right-mouse-drag and gamepad right-stick camera orbit (no Pointer Lock, so On Click behaviors keep working) and a "Rotate Camera" row in the in-game Controls screen.
- Low ledges are climbable again. Step-up required the player's centre to already be over the ledge, but the wall push kept it one radius outside, so any solid up to STEP_HEIGHT acted as a wall. It now tests the player's footprint; tall walls still block.
- Added Inspector "Fit Box to Trunk/Base" (trees, lamp posts, statues block at the base, not the canopy). Applied it to 296 trees, including Scatter-generated ones, plus 7 floor-standing pipes across 16 example games, which players previously walked straight through.
- Added `tools/playtest-collision-sweep.js` + `tools/run-collision-sweep.js`: a headless real-game random-walk sweep over all 28 public games that reports solid-collider penetration and walk-through visible objects. Before this pass: 0 solid penetrations across 28 games; walk-through props were the remaining failures.
- Editor deep links (`editor/?game=<id>`) open straight into the editor with the sample loaded, no spurious "unsaved changes" prompt for the untouched starter, and the tab title follows the opened project. Leaving an untouched starter/sample no longer triggers the browser's leave-site warning.
- Game pages embed compact project JSON (site 297 MB → 213 MB). `tools/package-hostinger.ps1` now runs on Windows PowerShell 5.1 and writes '/' ZIP entry names.

## Phase 046 — One-Way Platform 2D

- Added a first-class singleton One-Way Platform 2D component for Character response on enabled Static Body + Box objects, including invisible support, validation, Inspector readiness, selected top-edge affordance, and solid fallback for unsupported rotation. Ordinary Kinematic Body response is unchanged.
- Advanced Tilesets v4→v5 with Tile-owned `collision.oneWay` for Full Cell and Custom Rectangle collision. The Tile Palette toggle and preview, sparse Layer query, Terrain/Auto-Tile resolution, Save/Load, Prefab, Bundle, and both export tiers preserve semantics without per-cell objects.
- Analytic previous-position/top-crossing logic passes upward and sideways, catches downward/high-speed/diagonal landings, maintains grounded on top, and excludes ceiling/wall/wall-slide contact. Qualification: 44 focused tests, 500,000 seeded moves, 100,000 Tile-query comparisons, 20 browser cycles, full integrated regression, 28/28 public builds, and an exact-final 30-minute active soak with 161,030 ticks passed. Final verdict: `PHASE_046_ONE_WAY_PLATFORMS_READY`. Phase 047 remains unimplemented.

## Phase 045 — Character Collision 2D and Tilemap response

- Added a versioned Character Collision 2D component for an enabled Kinematic Body plus ordinary Box/Circle Collider. It reuses the Phase 043 conservative mover, reports transient grounded/ceiling/wall contact, and optionally clamps world-down wall sliding without changing authored velocity.
- Added direct sparse Tilemap collision queries for Full Cell and Custom Rectangle shapes, including rotated/scaled/mirrored and multilayer maps. Tile Layers now author `collisionEnabled` independently of visibility and edit locking; no per-Tile scene objects or Bodies are created.
- Qualified 36 focused checks, 500,000 seeded movement cases, 100,000 transformed Tile-query comparisons, a 10,240-cell performance fixture, 20 isolated browser cycles, standalone Editable/Playable collision execution, the unchanged 28-game public boundary, and an exact-final 30-minute active soak with 162,850 Character moves. Final verdict: `PHASE_045_CHARACTER_COLLISION_WALL_SLIDE_READY`. General Dynamic physics, controller input/jump, one-way platforms, slopes, carry, filtering, and collision events remain deferred; Phase 046 remains untouched.

## Phase 044 — Dynamic Body utility gate

- Completed a qualification-only gravity/mutable-velocity prototype over the Phase 043 Static collision service before any product schema change. Analytic free fall, Static contact, 60-second rest, Trigger ordering, parent conversion, thin walls, and bounded performance passed.
- Qualified 15 focused checks, 500,595 seeded movement cases, 240,000 resting ticks, 20/20 isolated browser cycles, full integrated regression, and 28/28 public game builds. Disclosed 6,065 fail-closed high-speed/grazing sweep caps with zero recurring rest caps.
- Rejected canonical Dynamic promotion: the examples provide only two materially distinct simpler no-code workflows, while absent Dynamic/Dynamic, Dynamic/Kinematic, and Tilemap response would make the label misleading. Body remains v1 Static/Kinematic; final verdict `PHASE_044_DYNAMIC_BODY_NOT_JUSTIFIED`. Phase 045 is untouched.

## Phase 043 — Static/Kinematic Body 2D

- Added a versioned singleton Body 2D component with Static/Kinematic roles, world-XY velocity, searchable Add Component and Inspector authoring, validation, Save/Load, Prefabs, Undo, and Editable/Playable export support.
- Added conservative Kinematic-versus-Static Box/Circle movement, thin-wall anti-tunneling, deterministic X-then-Y sliding, parent-transform conversion, and post-motion Trigger lifecycle ordering. Static objects never solver-move, and requested velocity survives contact.
- Kept the scope explicit: no Dynamic, gravity, forces, mass, friction/restitution, Kinematic/Kinematic response, filtering, character controller, Tilemap collision consumption, or collision-contact events. The internal dogfood project does not enter the 28-game public catalog.
- Qualified 24 focused checks, 1,939 Phase 011–043 and 2,301 integrated assertions, 500,607 movement fuzz cases, 20/20 isolated browser cycles, 28/28 public game builds, and a 30.0034-minute active browser soak with 1,361,600 ticks, bounded/reclaimed heap, and zero page/console/network errors. Final verdict: `PHASE_043_STATIC_KINEMATIC_BODIES_READY`; Phase 044 remains untouched.

## Phase 042 — Trigger Collider 2D

- Added a separate versioned Trigger Collider 2D component with independent Box/Circle geometry, retained subshape values, visible-control authoring, selected-only dashed/diamond-handle gizmos, Fit to Visual, validation, persistence, Prefabs, and Editable/Playable export support.
- Added deterministic object-level Enter/Stay/Exit lifecycle over ordinary Phase 041 colliders, including multi-shape deduplication, self/Trigger-only exclusion, stable frozen payloads, Behavior/Action routing, callback-safe snapshots, live-disable Exit, silent deletion cleanup, and Play/Level resets.
- Reused Phase 041 world-shape/AABB/narrow overlap functions; added no Body, response, velocity, gravity, filtering, Tilemap collision consumption, or global physics debug system.
- Qualified 73 focused checks, 500,000 transitions over 20 seeds, callback mutation, a 100,000-pair median of 19.535 ms, 20/20 isolated browser cycles, exact Editable/Playable lifecycle, and the unchanged 28-game public boundary. A 30.0004-minute active soak completed 2,500,950 ticks with 163,312 Enter, 615,902 Stay, 163,307 Exit, zero final pairs, bounded/reclaimed heap, and no page, console, network, persistence, restart, Prefab, or export failure. Final verdict: `PHASE_042_TRIGGER_COLLIDER_READY`; Phase 043 remains untouched.

## Phase 040 — 10K Tilemap Performance and Epoch 04 Qualification

- Replaced per-cell Tilemap draw submission with revision-invalidated, transient consecutive render-state batches that preserve authored order, reuse buffers across camera motion, stay below Uint16 limits, and release GPU resources on replacement/deletion/destruction.
- Qualified a 10,240-cell, four-Layer world containing Manual, Auto-Tile, weighted Terrain, Auto-Terrain, mixed filtering and collision metadata, 100 Spawn Markers, 75 ordinary Prefab objects, and a reusable 1,000-cell Chunk.
- Reduced the canonical mixed-filter world from about 10,240 Tilemap draws to three; sustained editor and Playable p99 frame time is approximately 7.1 ms on the recorded AMD/Edge hardware environment.
- Completed a corrected 60.32-minute active browser soak with 26,894 mutations, 119 save/load + Chunk + Bundle cycles, stable cache memory, zero semantic mismatches/crashes/browser errors, 30/30 focused checks, 250,000 cache-fuzz mutations, 2,121 integrated assertions, and 28/28 public game builds.
- Closed Phase 040 as `PHASE_040_TILEMAP_10K_PERFORMANCE_READY` and Epoch 04 as `EPOCH_04_TILEMAPS_GRIDS_2D_WORLDS_QUALIFIED`. Isolated Windows/Chromium RAF gaps remain disclosed as an `ENVIRONMENT_LIMITED` maximum-stall sub-gate. Phase 041 was not started.

## Phase 036 — Terrain and Biome Tile Brushes

- Added Tileset-v4 Weighted and Connected Auto-Tile Terrain Brushes with stable local identity, signed Seeds, bounded weights, canonical FNV-1a coordinate resolution, semantic cells, validation, repair, recompute, and deterministic Editable/Playable export policy.
- Added Terrain Palette authoring plus single/square/circle radius 1-8 footprints, exact hover previews, interpolated batch paint/erase, active-Layer isolation, connected-neighbor recompute, and one Undo record per drag.
- Passed 102 focused checks, 1,480 Phase 011-036 checks, 1,842 integrated checks, 750,000 fuzz operations, real-browser dogfood, isolated export boots, 28/28 site builds, and final packaging with zero engine-byte delta.
- Preserved the initial `PHASE_036_BLOCKED` 60.025-second attempt, then closed the endurance gate with one fresh 21,639.369-second continuous campaign: 37 heartbeats, 14 isolated browser/Editable/Playable cycles, zero semantic failures or crashes, and green post-run regression/build/site gates. Final verdict is `PHASE_036_TERRAIN_BRUSHES_READY`; Phase 037 was not implemented.

## Phase 033 — Multi-Layer Tilemaps

- Advanced Tilemaps to version 2 with deterministic Phase-032 migration, stable local Layer IDs, bottom-to-top per-layer sparse cells, canonical layered Save/Load, and conservative all-layer export dependencies.
- Added a topmost-first Layer panel with active selection, Add, rename, reorder, visibility, edit lock, duplicate, delete, Clear Layer, Clear All Layers, and snapshot Undo/Redo.
- Made Paint/Erase and stroke history Layer-ID-aware while preserving one Tilemap scene object, one shared Tileset/Transform/sort slot, contiguous Sprite-pass composition, Orthographic/Perspective picking, and the 28-game public boundary.

## Phase 031 — Tileset asset and Tile Palette foundation

- Added first-class versioned Tileset assets whose stable local Tiles reference existing Sprite Assets; Texture payloads and atlas rectangles remain owned by Texture/Sprite Asset records.
- Added a dedicated searchable, keyboard-operable Tile Palette with pixel-correct thumbnails, bulk Sprite Asset intake, stable selection, immediate rename, reorder, duplicate, remove, broken-reference repair, and card-size state.
- Extended validation, usage warnings, navigation, Undo/Redo snapshots, Save/Load, Editable export, dependency closure, and Playable pruning without adding a Tilemap/Grid scene object or runtime tile renderer.
- Qualified a visible-control 4x4 atlas workflow, two independent Tilesets, deliberate break/repair, Save/reload, real Editable/Playable exports, direct-Sprite dependency overlap, 1,000-Tile lookup/serialization, and the unchanged 28-game public boundary. Tilemaps remains honestly graded D.

## Phase 030 — Sprite starters and Epoch 03 qualification

- Added three discoverable, local, deep-independent normal-project Sprite starters: 2D Sprite Scene, Animated Character, and Parallax Hybrid Scene; Start Blank remains first.
- Qualified starter validation, Save/Load, Editable/Playable export, offline closure, removability, and source immutability through 67 focused checks and fresh-origin browser runs.
- Built the internal `Phase 030 Sprite Dogfood` through visible controls, exercised Orthographic Camera, sorting, parallax, animation clips/states/events/direction, Nine-Slice, Pixel/Smooth, Prefabs, and hybrid layering, then verified break/repair, Save/reload, isolated exports, and public-catalog exclusion.
- Closed Epoch 03 at 1,335/1,335 integrated and 973/973 Phase 011–030 focused checks with 28/28 public games. Sprites / 2D remains B, Animation C, Tilemaps D; Phase 031 was not implemented.

## Phase 029 — Explicit hybrid scene layering

- Split the historical transparent 3D pass into deterministic Behind Sprites and In Front of Sprites bands around one contiguous Sprite pass; both bands depth-test, disable depth writes, sort farthest-first, and use stable Object ID ties.
- Added the bounded `spritePassRelation` authoring field for transparent Meshes and Billboards with historical fallback, opaque alpha-roundtrip preservation, structured validation, Inspector summaries, and Project help.
- Qualified save/load, duplicate, prefab/update, level storage, perspective/orthographic camera switching, Editable/Playable exports, and a 1,750-object minimum stress matrix. Added 50 focused checks while retaining Sprites / 2D B and Animation C; Phase 030 was not started.

## Phase 022 — Sprite Animation Editor

- Advanced Sprite Animation Clips to v2 with explicit `once`/`loop` playback, lossless v1-to-Once migration, efficient modulo catch-up across large deltas, and safe one-frame/empty-loop behavior.
- Replaced detailed vertical frame forms with one dedicated visual editor: atlas-aware thumbnails, broken slots, typed Sprite selection, millisecond timing, Add/Remove/Duplicate, native drag reorder, Left/Right fallback, and derived frame/duration summaries.
- Added an isolated checkerboard preview with Play, Pause, Stop/reset, scrubbing, frame-click seek, current-frame indication, source-rect transparency, and per-frame Smooth/Pixel treatment, all on the shared runtime timing helpers.
- Qualified Save/Load, validation repair, animated Sprite Prefabs, real Editable and Playable export boots, 100-frame editor timing, and 100 independent Loop instances with 61 focused Phase 022 checks. Browser dogfood also caught and repaired a scrubber repaint-order bug. Animation remains C and Sprites / 2D remains B; Phase 023 was not started.

## Phase 019 — Sprite Asset placement workflow

- Made every normal Sprite Asset library row a thresholded pointer drag source with cropped-region ghost feedback, a viewport drop indicator, Escape/outside cancellation, and click-to-inspect preservation.
- Added a typed stable-ID placement payload and routed drag, row Place, and command-palette Place Sprite through the existing perspective ground-ray, spatial snap, Sprite instantiation, selection, and single-snapshot Undo/Redo paths.
- New instances receive natural full/atlas-region size, asset pivot, white/opaque/visible appearance, Default/0 sorting, unique Object IDs, and collision-safe asset-derived names while source/region/filtering remain live-linked and size/pivot remain overrides.
- Added 39 Phase 019 checks, normal-editor blank/atlas/Pixel/Prefab/Save/Load/Level/export dogfood, contracts, characterization, and retained the honest Sprites / 2D grade B without adding later 2D systems.

## Phase 018 — Sprite atlases and sub-rect assets

- Advanced Sprite Assets to v3 with optional top-left-origin pixel source regions, strict bounds validation, full-image migration, region-relative natural size/pivot, and one authoritative WebGL UV conversion.
- Added cropped previews, numeric and drag/resize Source Region editing, Use Full Image, and a deterministic regular-grid slicer with optional margins/spacing and collision-safe bulk naming.
- Preserved one Texture payload across independent Sprite Assets, duplicate editing, Prefabs, pruning, Save/Load, editor Play, and both export tiers; added a bounded half-texel Smooth treatment and documented extreme-minification mip bleed.
- Added 33 Phase 018 checks, a reproducible 4×4 atlas fixture, normal-editor dogfood, contracts, characterization, and retained the honest Sprites / 2D grade B without adding animation.

## Phase 017 — Sprite material controls

- Added per–Sprite Asset Smooth and Pixel / Nearest sampling without duplicating source Texture bytes or leaking sampler state between assets sharing one Texture.
- Added bounded per-instance Sprite opacity that composes with source image alpha through the existing standard-alpha Sprite pass.
- Versioned and migrated Sprite Asset and Sprite Renderer records with appearance-preserving defaults; direct Texture, Mesh, and Billboard users retain Smooth sampling.
- Added 30 Phase 017 checks, normal-editor Save/Load/Play/export dogfood, checked-in pixel-art proof, filtering and presentation contracts, and retained the honest Sprites / 2D grade B.

## Phase 016 — Sprite prefab, serialization, and portability hardening

- Preserved linked-prefab identity and instance parameter overrides on Sprite records, closing a Save/Load and export-pruning gap.
- Extended structured project validation into prefab member components, including missing Sprite Assets, Textures, Sorting Layers, and newer unsupported Sprite Renderer versions.
- Added prefab-aware repair context and focus behavior without introducing a Sprite-specific serializer or changing generic prefab override semantics.
- Qualified deterministic migration, duplication, cross-level reuse, dependency closure, editable/playable export, and a 100-instance reuse case in `tests/phase-016-sprite-prefab-serialization.test.js`.

All notable changes to Digital Opus Engine will be documented in this file.

## [Unreleased]

### Phase 013 — Named Sprite Sorting Layers

- Added a project-owned Sprite Sorting Layer registry with stable IDs, explicit contiguous order, a protected Default layer, rename-safe references, deterministic reordering, and guarded deletion with an exact reference count and explicit reassignment to Default.
- Advanced Sprite Renderer to schema version 3 with typed `sortingLayerRef` and integer `orderInLayer`; legacy version 1/2 `layer` values migrate to Default plus Order in Layer without changing their relative order.
- Runtime Sprite stacking is now exactly project layer order, then Order in Layer, then persisted Object ID. Transform Z remains spatial and is not an ordering key; transparent Sprite depth-write behavior is unchanged.
- Added 38 Phase 013 characterization checks, a 300-Sprite randomized-insertion determinism stress test, normal-editor browser dogfood, migration documentation, and capability/friction evidence. Sprites / 2D remains grade B; Phase 014 was not started.

### Phase 012 — First-Class Sprite Asset

- Registered reusable Sprite Assets through the Phase 003 asset registry. Each versioned record owns a stable Texture reference, default pivot, and Pixels Per Unit while the Texture remains the sole owner of image bytes and GPU decode state.
- Added Texture → Create Sprite and composed Import as Sprite workflows, registry-derived asset type filtering, local previews, Sprite metadata inspection, natural-size calculation, duplication, placement, missing-source repair, and dependency-aware deletion warnings.
- Sprite Renderer now prefers a stable Sprite Asset reference while preserving Phase 011 direct-Texture records, explicit per-instance Width/Height, conversion, Save/Load, Prefabs, validation, and shared Editable/Playable export.
- Added 36 Phase 012 characterization checks and normal-editor browser dogfood. Sprites / 2D remains grade B; Phase 013 sorting work was not started.

### Digital Opus Engine 1.0.0-rc.1 (human acceptance pending — not deployed)

- Packaged the 28-game public library as RC1 with 4 Featured and 24 Samples.
- Hardened controls with corrected mouse-look axes, persistent sensitivity, Invert Mouse Y, and pointer-lock release/reacquire handling.
- Hardened collision with movement sweeps, wall sliding, and coverage for ramps, steps, crouch, terrain, and moving platforms.
- Made Hovercraft controllers respect authored movement tuning.
- Built and clean-room tested the exact flat-root RC ZIP; subjective human acceptance is still pending.
- This is not a Production Launch. Nothing was deployed, DNS was not changed, and Wave 10 was not started.

### Featured Promotion + Final Rehabilitation (ready for human review -- not deployed, not Wave 10)

- Installed the user-supplied ring-and-wave artwork as the canonical Digital Opus app icon across the editor, development demo, Engine home, and Games/Examples hubs. Site builders now package the same original PNG at `assets/branding/digital-opus-icon.png`; one-file game exports deliberately remove the editor-relative icon link to preserve standalone portability.
- Promoted **Arsenal Run, Power Grid, Forest Range, and Moving Temple** only after rendered review and native-input start-to-win evidence; Foglight, Signal Relay, and Silent Sector remain honest public Samples.
- Expanded the original deterministic showcase library from 36 to 63 meshes, 6 to 27 textures, and 16 to 24 audio clips. The 27/21/8 additions cover deliberate fantasy, facility, combat, environment and sound variants without external-license risk.
- Added a bounded Player Visual adapter for Hovercraft: the existing controller body remains authoritative for physics, an authored static or skinned mesh follows it, and the old primitive render remains the migration-free fallback. Survival Arena 2 and 3 now win and restart under native input and graduate to Sample; the original Survival Arena stays Internal after reaching only 3/4 orbs.
- Valley Run now has visible route landmarks and a verified normal-input ridge victory/Restart, graduating to Sample. Opus Arena remains Internal because an ordinary-input eight-kill victory is still unproven.
- Added `public-showcase-review-v2/` with actual before/current standalone frames, editorial scores, local play links and SHA-256 screenshot provenance. Final catalog: **28 total / 4 Featured / 22 Samples / 2 Internal**.
- All content promotion tooling is idempotent; a repeated How To Play suffix found during final regression was fixed at its source and normalized.
- No deployment, production ZIP, DNS change, or change to `games.digitalopusadsolutions.com` occurred. Wave 10 remains not started.

### Public Showcase Recovery (closed out -- see PUBLIC-SHOWCASE-RECOVERY-REPORT.md; a quarantine + bug-fix pass, NOT a launch, NOT showcase-ready, NOT Wave 10)

**Supersedes the "Engine Hostinger Upload Package Prepared" entry directly below: that package is now marked `INTERNAL_DIAGNOSTICS_ONLY` (see `release/INTERNAL_DIAGNOSTICS_ONLY.md`) and must not be uploaded.** A visual review of the 7 "Featured" games found them not to be public-showcase quality (sparse/primitive geometry, a visible skeletal test-rig, empty rooms, a wrong on-screen title). 31/31 tests passing proves technical operation, not presentation quality.

- **All 28 catalog entries quarantined to `visibility: internal`** -- `games/catalog.json` now carries `purpose` (`mechanics-sample` or `regression`) and `visibility` (`internal` or `public`) per entry. None stayed public merely for having had `featured: true`. **Public showcase count: 0.**
- **Public build now genuinely filters by visibility** -- `buildGamesSite()`'s new `onlyVisibility` option means `build-engine-site.js`'s public Engine home and Examples Hub only ever show `visibility: public` entries (currently zero), while internal regression tooling (`build-games.js`, the dev editor) is unaffected. Zero public games renders as a restrained "Playable showcase in development" notice -- never an empty grid, never a redirect to the internal catalog.
- **Real bug found and fixed: First-Person's own collision body was visibly re-rendered every frame**, filling a large portion of the lower viewport in every First-Person game (confirmed in Signal Relay, Arsenal Run, Power Grid). Root cause: `teardownPlayerVisual()` (called every frame for any controller without a skinned visual) unconditionally restored `player.entity.visible = true`, silently overwriting First-Person's own spawn-time "never render your own body" convention. Fixed by exempting `controllerType === 'firstperson'` from that restore -- Third-Person/Top-Down/Hovercraft are unaffected.
- **Real bug found and fixed: a hardcoded Crate Run controls hint leaked onto unrelated games** -- the on-screen "WASD / Arrows to drive... Collect crates..." reminder was static HTML shown on every Hovercraft-controller game (e.g. Survival Arena 2.0/3, both about surviving hunter waves, not collecting crates). Replaced with a new per-project `controlsHint` field (Start Screen config, same blank-hides-it convention as the existing How To Play/Credits fields), moved to the one shared spawn choke point so any controller can use it. Default blank -- an unauthored game now shows nothing, never another game's instructions.
- **Real bug found and fixed: `relic-frontier.json`'s own on-screen Start Screen title said "Relic Runner 2"** while its catalog/meta title said "Relic Frontier" -- a real player would see the wrong title. Fixed both the level name and Start Screen title to "Relic Frontier". This rename alone does not make the project public-quality (it remains internal, still has the geometry/asset issues Section 1 of the report describes).
- **Two new build-time public-qualification checks added** to `build-games-site.js`: an identity-consistency check (catalog/meta/Start-Screen/HTML titles must agree) and a test-fixture heuristic (rejects any public-bound skinned mesh asset under 24 vertices -- sized to catch the exact two-joint bend/ribbon rig pattern already found live in Relic Frontier's own "IronSentry" asset). Both only block `visibility: public` builds; internal regression fixtures are unaffected.
- **Asset inventory taken, flagship rebuild correctly not attempted** -- no coherent Player character, Guardian creature, environment kit, texture/material kit, or audio kit exists locally (only a handful of isolated test-fixture-grade files). Per this pass's own explicit instruction, no primitive-box "character", no second two-joint ribbon, and no unlicensed downloaded assets were used to fake a rebuild. `SHOWCASE-ASSET-REQUEST.md` lists exactly what's needed.
- **Final verdict: `PUBLIC_SHOWCASE_BLOCKED_ASSETS_REQUIRED`** -- a correct, honest result, not a failure. Nothing was deployed; no DNS was touched; Wave 10 was not started.

### Engine Hostinger Upload Package Prepared (closed out -- see DIGITAL-OPUS-ENGINE-UPLOAD-READY-REPORT.md; release-engineering pass, NOT a deployment, NOT Wave 10 -- **superseded, see the entry above: this package is now INTERNAL_DIAGNOSTICS_ONLY**)

- **Real bug found and fixed: falling off the edge of any level was unrecoverable** -- a user-requested logic check across all 28 games found that every jump-enabled controller (Top-Down w/ jump, Third-Person, First-Person) had zero recovery from clearing the edge of placed floor geometry. None of the 28 catalog games place an explicit `terrain` object, so `currentTerrainItem()` returns null and there is no fallback ground height once a player clears the last box collider; `applyMapBoundary` (the existing radial containment) only ever nudges horizontal position, never Y. A scan of all 28 projects found only 5 (Foglight, Blacksite, Relic Frontier, Relic Runner 2, Valley Run) had ANY checkpoint/respawn wiring at all, and that was authored per-hazard, not a general void catch -- the other 23 had no recovery whatsoever. **Fixed once**, at the single per-frame choke point every controller already funnels through (`updatePlayerController` in `editor/src/01-editor.js`): falling more than 30 units below the last spawn/checkpoint now automatically returns the player to it, reusing the exact same recovery the existing `Respawn At Checkpoint` behavior-graph action already used for hazards -- not a per-level authoring fix requiring 23 projects to be individually retrofitted with a manual void-trigger volume. A hovercraft controller never drops in Y, so this never fires for it; it is scoped to the controllers that actually have gravity. All 28 games and the packaged editor were rebuilt with this fix baked in; the 31-test suite still passes unchanged (the fix lives in the editor/game runtime, not the engine bundle under test).
- **Engine home page now shows all 28 games, not just the 7 Featured** -- since every game was already built and qualified, `build-engine-site.js`'s home-page card list was changed from `featuredBuilt` to the full `built` set (Featured sorted first, badge now conditional instead of hardcoded). The "See All N Examples" CTA was reworded to "Open the Examples Hub" since the home page no longer hides any game behind it.
- **Every card now gets a generated title-card thumbnail, not just Featured ones** -- once all 28 games rendered on one page, the 7 image cards next to 21 bare-text cards read as visually inconsistent/broken, not like a deliberate design. `build-games-site.js` now generates a `titleCardSVG` thumbnail for every game; the 7 Featured entries keep their hand-picked `FEATURED_PALETTE` colors unchanged, and the other 21 get a new deterministic per-id fallback gradient (a stable hash of the game's own id to an HSL hue) instead of one flat, repeated default color -- no hand-picked palette entries to maintain as the catalog grows. Applies to both the Engine home page and the standalone Examples Hub/Digital Opus Games site, since both read from the same `built` list this generator produces.
- **Local, uploadable release package produced** -- `release/digital-opus-engine-web.zip` (SHA-256 in `release/SHA256SUMS.txt`), a flat-root ZIP of the already-qualified `dist/engine-site/` package with no wrapping folder, so extracting it puts `index.html` directly at a web document root.
- **Clean-room tested as the actual upload payload, not the source tree** -- the ZIP itself (not `dist/engine-site/` directly) was extracted into an unrelated temporary directory and served fresh; `/`, `/editor/`, `/games/`, `/games/foglight.html`, and `/editor/?game=foglight` all verified working with zero console errors.
- **Manual deployment docs added** -- `release/HOSTINGER-DEPLOYMENT-GUIDE.md` (step-by-step upload/extract/SSL/DNS instructions for `engine.digitalopusadsolutions.com` only) and `release/POST-DEPLOY-VERIFICATION.md` (post-upload checklist, including a mandatory re-check that the existing, separate `games.digitalopusadsolutions.com` portfolio is untouched).
- **Nothing deployed, no DNS/hosting mutated** -- this environment has no Hostinger/DNS/FTP credentials; `engine.digitalopusadsolutions.com` was only read-only checked (no existing DNS record found) and `games.digitalopusadsolutions.com` was only read-only re-confirmed unchanged. The actual upload remains a manual step for the user.

### Digital Opus Engine Public Home (closed out -- see DIGITAL-OPUS-ENGINE-PUBLIC-HOME-REPORT.md; a product/distribution pass, NOT Wave 10, NOT deployed)

- **Public Engine home page prepared locally** -- a new landing page (`build/templates/engine-home.html`) explaining Digital Opus Engine to a first-time visitor: hero, five value-proposition pillars, four grounded feature groups (Build Worlds / Build Gameplay / Characters & Combat / Presentation), a 3-step "How It Works", and a Featured-examples section rendered entirely from `games/catalog.json` (no hardcoded title list). Explicitly distinguishes itself from the separate, already-live Digital Opus Games portfolio, with a clear footer link and no catalog merge.
- **The real editor, packaged, not forked** -- `node build/build-engine-site.js` copies `editor/index.html`/`editor/src/01-editor.js` byte-for-byte alongside the built engine bundle and `build/export-template.js`, in a folder shaped like the repository's own layout (so the editor's existing hardcoded relative fetches resolve with zero rewriting). One editor implementation, packaged for a different location -- not a second one.
- **Engine examples hub reused, not duplicated** -- `build/build-games-site.js`'s own game-site generator was refactored into an exported `buildGamesSite()` function, now called by both the standalone "Digital Opus Games"-style CLI build (unchanged output) and the new Engine site's own `games/` subpage (different branding copy, same generator, same `composeExportHTML`).
- **Real bug found and fixed**: packaging the editor exposed a genuine, previously-latent conflict -- the editor's own Games tab fetches `../games/catalog.json` and reads `entry.file` to know which raw project JSON to load, but a PUBLIC catalog.json (the kind `buildGamesSite()` generates for a games hub) shapes that same field as the compiled `<id>.html` filename instead, for its own card links. Packaging the editor and a public games hub side-by-side put the public-shaped catalog at the exact path the editor's Games tab reads, and "Open in Editor" failed. Fixed at the root: the editor's `openCatalogGame` now fetches `<id>.json` (built from the catalog's own stable id) instead of trusting `entry.file`, which is correct under either catalog shape.
- **Optional deep link added**: `editor/?game=<id>` (a ~10-line addition reusing the existing `openCatalogGame`, not a router) lets an Engine example card's own "Open in Editor" jump straight to that sample instead of a blank editor.
- **Verified via all four required clean-room journeys**, from a genuinely separate directory served fresh (not the dev tree): Home → Open Editor → Crate Run visible; Home → Play Example Games → Foglight → Start; Editor → Games → Foglight → Open in Editor → move an object → Play (the object stayed moved); a real file-input Texture import; and a real Playable Export from the packaged editor, opened in an isolated realm, confirming the edited object and zero network requests. Subpath hosting (`/test/engine/`) also verified working, since every internal link in the package is relative.
- **Not deployed anywhere.** `games.digitalopusadsolutions.com` (the separate, real 43-game Digital Opus Games portfolio) was not touched. `PROPOSED_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET: engine.digitalopusadsolutions.com` -- a recommendation only, not created, not authorized.

### Digital Opus Games Public Hub (closed out -- see DIGITAL-OPUS-GAMES-PUBLIC-HUB-REPORT.md; a website/product-distribution pass, NOT Wave 10)

- **Deployment-ready public games site** -- `node build/build-games-site.js` generates `dist/games-site/`, a complete, self-contained static site (no repository-relative paths, no server runtime) suitable for direct upload to a static host: a calm, player-facing hub (hero, Featured section, filtered "More Games"/"Experimental" grid, About section), 28 standalone playable builds, a stripped public `catalog.json`, and a deployment README. Not deployed anywhere this pass -- output stays local.
- **One export implementation, reused a third time** -- the site's own game builds go through the exact same `composeExportHTML` (`build/export-template.js`) the editor's Playable Export button and `build/build-games.js` already use. No forked export logic.
- **One page template, not a buried string** -- the site's HTML lives in `build/templates/games-site.html` (placeholder tokens filled in by the build script), not a 500-line template string inside `build-games-site.js`.
- **Public terminology cleanup** -- the catalog's own genre/description text for the 7 "technical" games previously read "Technical Sample" and "kept for engine regression coverage" -- internal-facing language that would have leaked straight onto player-facing cards. Rewritten to calm, player-facing wording ("First-Person Experiment", "A short first-person interactive experiment.") in `games/catalog.json` itself, so the fix applies everywhere the catalog is read from (editor Games tab, both generated sites), not just the new site's own template.

Rather than build a more elaborate capture pipeline to work around a text-relay limitation, Featured games ship with deterministic generated gradient/title-card SVGs instead (`THUMBNAILS_DEFERRED_WITH_REASON` for real screenshots specifically); Sample/Experimental cards use no image, per this pass's own stated fallback order.
- **Verified deployment-ready in practice, not just by inspection**: the entire `dist/games-site/` folder was copied to a clean, unrelated directory and served from a fresh static server with zero repository paths involved -- launcher, filters, and Play all worked identically. One Featured build (`foglight.html`) was additionally copied ALONE (no `index.html`, no `catalog.json`, no `assets/`) into an empty folder and still played correctly end-to-end with zero network requests.
- **Build repeatability confirmed** -- running the site build twice produced byte-identical output except for a single harmless `generatedAt` timestamp field in the public catalog metadata; no duplicate, growing, or stale entries.

### Game Catalog & Playable Builds (closed out -- see GAME-CATALOG-AND-PLAYABLE-BUILDS-REPORT.md; a product/distribution workflow pass, NOT a capability wave)

- **Built-in Games catalog, right in the editor** -- a new "Games" tab (left workspace, alongside Objects/Assets/Prefabs) turns the whole `games/*.json` backlog into one-click cards: title, genre, controller, a short player-facing description, estimated playtime, and a Featured badge where earned. **Play** loads the bundled project through the exact same `loadProjectFull` normalization/asset-decoding/prefab-merge every manual JSON import already uses and enters Play immediately; **Open in Editor** loads the same project and stays in Edit mode for full modification/Save As/re-export. Zero file-picker interaction either way.
- **One shared catalog metadata source** -- `games/catalog.json` (28 entries: 7 Featured, 14 Sample, 7 Technical) drives the editor's own Games tab, the static launcher, and the build-time playable-HTML generator. No hand-duplicated game lists across three places.
- **Persistent standalone Playable builds** -- `node build/build-games.js` generates `dist/games/<id>.html` for every catalog entry via the SAME `buildExportHTML`-equivalent pipeline the editor's own Playable Export button already uses (see below) -- not a second, forked export implementation. Each build is the same kind of genuinely self-contained file already proven by two dogfood passes: no adjacent assets, no network dependency, portable as a single copied `.html` file.
- **One export implementation, not two** -- the actual HTML-templating step inside `buildExportHTML` (engine + editor + project -> one page) was factored into a new, dependency-free shared module, `build/export-template.js` (`composeExportHTML`), loadable both as a browser global (the editor embeds it as a script tag, and re-embeds its own source into every export so "Edit This Game -> modify -> re-export" keeps working from inside an already-exported page) and as a plain Node `require()` (the build script). Project serialization itself never moved -- only the templating step that already came after it.
- **Static launcher gallery** -- `dist/games/index.html`, a backend-free, framework-free static page with Featured/Sample/Technical filtering and plain `<a href="<id>.html">` links (no `fetch()` needed just to launch a game, so `file://` double-click access stays simple). Opens and plays the full catalog with zero editor involvement.
- **Cross-project load isolation, verified live** -- every catalog `Open`/`Play` is a fresh fetch + fresh `loadProjectFull`, never a cached/reused parsed object; rapidly loading Foglight, mutating an object, switching to Signal Relay, and reopening Foglight was confirmed to reload it pristine (the exact contamination pattern prior dogfood passes have found bugs in before).
- **Unsaved-work protection** -- the editor's own undo history (already pushed before essentially every meaningful edit) is reused as the "has this project been touched" signal; opening a different catalog game while it's non-empty asks for confirmation first, same as any editor session should.
- **Real bug found and fixed**: the Games panel's own catalog-loading init call was, like the rest of `editor/src/01-editor.js`, embedded wholesale into every Playable/Editable Export -- meaning every exported/played game made an unexpected `../games/catalog.json` network request the instant it loaded, purely by accident of how exports embed the full editor source. Fixed by gating the Games panel's init behind the same `window.__OPUS_EMBEDDED_PROJECT__` flag every export already sets before its embedded editor script runs -- the Games panel is now correctly dev-editor-only, and a played/opened export makes zero unexpected network requests (confirmed live: `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` returns `[]` for a fresh Foglight/Signal Relay/Arsenal Run build).
- **Editable sources preserved, manual import unaffected** -- `games/*.json` remain the canonical editable projects; **Project → Import Project JSON** is untouched and still works exactly as before. The catalog is an additional first-class access path, not a replacement.

### Post-Wave-9 Product Dogfood #2 (closed out -- see POST-WAVE-9-DOGFOOD-02-REPORT.md; a second real game, deliberately a different genre/controller than Signal Relay -- NOT a capability wave)

- **New game: Foglight** (`games/foglight.json`) -- a ~15-minute Third-Person atmospheric traversal/exploration game, deliberately exercising a different slice of the engine than Signal Relay's First-Person stealth build: real jump-based platforming across two stepping-stone sections, a locked Gate gated on a real Item Definition (the Vale Key), one parameterized Foglight Prefab (three per-instance tinted beacons to light), a real animated/skinned enemy (the Mist Guardian -- an original 2-joint glTF creature with a "Lurch" clip) patrolling a real authored Path and breaking into a chase near the player, a Scatter Layer of moss groundcover, Save/Continue, and a real Win (reach the Sanctuary) and Lose (health depletion) state.
- **Real imported assets, through the real Import UI, all the way to a clean-room export** -- one original skinned glTF model (the Mist Guardian, real `JOINTS_0`/`WEIGHTS_0`/skin/animation data, round-tripped through the engine's own `Opus.Mesh.fromSkinnedGLTF`/`toSkinnedArrays`), one original procedural texture ("moss-stone"), one original two-tone WAV chime ("beacon-ignite") -- distinct assets from Signal Relay's own, each pushed through the real Import Model/Texture/Audio UI and verified fully self-contained in an isolated clean-room export with zero dependency on the editor's session, cache, or dev server.
- **Five stock one-click palette presets (Collectible, Health Pickup, Damage Hazard, Checkpoint, Enemy) were invisible in Play** -- each placed its sole visible mesh with `isTrigger: true`, which `preparePlacedForPlay` unconditionally hides; none of the five actually needed `isTrigger` for their own enter/exit/stay overlap detection to work. Found reaching for the Enemy preset as a starting point for the Mist Guardian; fixed by removing `isTrigger` from all five (their own `destroyObject`/damage-`cooldown` logic already gave them the correct one-shot/repeat behavior regardless).
- **A HUD element's inline offset style silently defeated every anchor except top-left** -- `renderHudLayer` always emitted an inline `left:0px;top:0px` (or whatever offsetX/offsetY happened to be), which as an inline style always wins over a `.hud-anchor-*` CSS class's own `right`/`bottom`/`transform`-based positioning; every non-top-left-anchored HUD element in every game has been collapsing to the viewport's literal top-left corner. Found live: an Objective Text element explicitly anchored `top-right` rendered directly overlapping a `top-left` Health Bar. Fixed by switching to `margin-left`/`margin-top` (which never overrides a class's own positioning) and only emitting them at all when an author has actually set a nonzero offset.
- **A Playable/Editable Export's browser-tab `<title>` fell back to a hardcoded other game's name** -- `buildExportHTML` fell back to the literal string `'Digital Opus — Crate Run'` whenever the easy-to-miss Project Meta title field was left empty, even though the in-page Start Screen correctly showed the real game's own title throughout. Fixed to fall back to the Start Screen's own title (which essentially every real game already sets) before finally falling back to a neutral, non-demo-specific name.
- **Full 28-game catalog regression** -- all 28 shipped games (27 existing + Foglight) loaded, entered, ticked, and exited Play with zero new console errors; the Crate Run fresh-boot anchor re-confirmed unaffected by the export-title fix (still correctly shows its own real name).
- **Deliberately not built this pass** (no concrete blocking need from the game itself): no new renderer features, no reopening of the closed Dynamic Physics/GPU Instancing/ONNX gates, no touch-control parity work (Foglight's own controls didn't require it), no Node Graph runtime-semantics change (this pass's own two graphs were authored cleanly on the first pass with the gate-safety lesson already applied, and the one real friction point found -- Objective progress not auto-completing -- turned out to be a `completeObjective` action I simply forgot to add, an authoring mistake, not evidence toward changing engine behavior).

### Post-Wave-9 Product Dogfood (closed out -- see POST-WAVE-9-DOGFOOD-REPORT.md; deliberately NOT a capability wave -- one real game built through the editor UI, real friction fixed at the root, nothing speculative added)

- **New game: Signal Relay** (`games/signal-relay.json`) -- a ~15-minute stealth/objective prototype built end-to-end through the real editor UI: restore three Relay Consoles while avoiding two patrol/alert/investigate/attack State Machine drones, then extract. Main Menu/Start Screen, a Sidearm with muzzle/impact effects and a tracer, three real HUD elements, Save/Continue, a real Win state (Extraction trigger) and a real Lose path (health-triggered), one linked Prefab with a Color Parameter (three per-instance override colors), a Scatter Layer of decorative clutter painted with the real Brush tool.
- **Real imported assets, through the real Import UI, all the way to a clean-room export** -- one original glTF model (a procedurally-generated "Relay Console" prop, no external/copyrighted source), one original texture, one original WAV chime, each pushed through the actual Import Model/Texture/Audio file-input UI, placed via the real Asset Library, and verified still fully self-contained (zero network requests, real decoded AudioBuffer, real applied texture) in an isolated `<iframe srcdoc>` clean-room realm with no access to the editor's own session, cache, or dev server.
- **Update From Prefab discarded an instance's own Name/Tags/Variables** -- `swapItemForPrefabInstance` now preserves them across a refresh; found placing and Updating three uniquely-named/uniquely-tagged linked instances.
- **An ordinary linked-Prefab placement (no Spawner/AI/weapon reference) was silently dropped from every Save and Export** -- `scanObjectRecord` now actually adds `sourcePrefabName` to the dependency set it already scans params against; found when three plainly-placed console instances vanished from a fresh Save.
- **A Node Graph node with two gate edges silently honored only the first one** -- `runGraph` now collects and ANDs every gate edge targeting a node, instead of `.find()`-ing just one; found live when an objective requiring "all 3 consoles powered" instead completed at 2, and a Security Gate gated the same way nearly opened early too.
- **A fresh Stop→Play reset every piece of global runtime state except an object's own `variables`** -- `enterPlayMode` now also restores each object's `variables` to an authored-time snapshot (`_authoredVariables`) on every fresh Play, matching the existing reset already applied to `__gameState`/`__worldState`/equipment/etc.; found repeating the mandated Stop→Play repeatability check.
- **A Wander AI's live runtime target (`wanderTarget`/`wanderTimer`) was being baked directly into the saved project file** on any Save that happened after that AI had ticked even once -- `serializeCubeRecord` (and, defensively, the record-load path) now strip these runtime-only fields via a new `sanitizeAiStatesForSave` helper, contradicting the code's own long-standing comment that claimed this already happened. Found live as a load-time crash (`ai.wanderTarget.sub is not a function`); an existing catalog file (`forest-range.json`) was independently found already polluted on disk and cleaned the same way.
- **Full 27-game catalog regression** -- all 27 shipped games (26 existing + Signal Relay) loaded, entered, ticked, and exited Play with zero new console errors; the five Wander-using games (`arsenal-run`/`forest-range`/`relic-frontier`/`signal-relay`/`silent-sector`) specifically re-verified ticking live for several real seconds after the wander-pollution fix; the Crate Run fresh-boot anchor (no project loaded first) re-confirmed clean.
- **Deliberately not built this pass** (no concrete blocking need from the game itself): no new renderer features (PBR/post-processing/volumetric/GI/reflections/rigid-body solver), no reopening of the closed Dynamic Physics/GPU Instancing/ONNX/Signal/Status/Suspicion gates, no touch-control parity work (Signal Relay's own controls didn't require it), no change to the intentional per-node-gate-re-evaluation behavior itself (API-FRICTION #72) despite this pass independently re-discovering that exact authoring trap.

### Capability Expansion Wave 9 (closed out -- see CAPABILITY-WAVE-9-REPORT.md)

- **Weapons 2.0 -- Ammo/Reload/Fire Modes/Spread/Recoil/Switching/HUD (Priority A)** -- a genuine weapon RUNTIME state layer, deliberately separate from the Weapon Definition (`currentPlayerSettings.weapon`/an Equipment Def's own `.weapon`) so authored data is never mutated by play. `weapon.ammoMode: 'infinite'|'magazine'` (Infinite is the unconditional default -- every pre-Wave-9 weapon keeps firing exactly as before); Magazine mode adds Magazine Size/Starting Magazine/Reserve Ammo, a real simulation-time Reload (`Reload` Input Action, default `KeyR`, same author-default/player-override architecture every other action already uses; partial-fill-only, never discards remainder), Semi (unconditional default, `wasPressed`) vs Automatic (`isDown`, paced by the weapon's own Cooldown in simulation time) fire modes, bounded cone Spread (Hitscan and Projectile identically), and a small additive (never state-corrupting) First-Person Recoil that decays back to zero every frame. Weapon **switching** is a real Primary/Secondary toggle (`Switch Weapon`, default `KeyV`) over Equipment slots -- each slot keeps its own independent magazine/reserve for the session (switching away and back never refills), stops an in-progress reload on the outgoing slot, and only the active slot's visual is ever shown. Ammo HUD via three new binding tokens (`{ammo}`/`{reserveAmmo}`/`{weaponName}`), reusing the exact same HUD label mechanism every other counter already binds through rather than a new dedicated element type. A bounded CSS-only crosshair (spread-reactive gap width) and Player Damage Feedback (a short red screen flash + a small Camera Shake) round out combat feel.
  - **Real bug found and fixed live**: `syncPlayerVisual` (called every real frame for any controller without a skinned body -- First-Person and Hovercraft) called the FULL `teardownPlayerEquipment()` unconditionally every frame, silently wiping which item was equipped in every slot for the entire duration of any First-Person session -- meaning Equipment Weapon mode (Primary/Secondary switching) was architecturally impossible in First-Person from the moment Wave 7 introduced it, undiscovered until Weapons 2.0's ammo/switching was the first thing to actually read `playerEquipment` back a frame later instead of only synchronously right after Equip. Fixed by splitting visual-entity cleanup (`teardownPlayerEquipmentVisualsOnly`, safe to call every frame) from the full slot-clearing teardown (now only called at genuine session boundaries). Reproduced and re-verified live: repeated `syncPlayerVisual()` calls no longer clear `playerEquipment`, and `{ammo}`/`{weaponName}` HUD tokens correctly track a live Primary/Secondary switch afterward.
  - Dogfooded live in a real loaded game (Iron Arena, reconfigured to Magazine ammo): 6 successive shots correctly took a 5-round magazine to 0 and then correctly blocked further fire (no negative ammo, Empty Sound played, no cooldown-bypass spam); Reload correctly moved exactly `min(size - magazine, reserve)` rounds and left the remainder in reserve; firing DURING an active reload was correctly blocked with zero ammo change; switching Primary/Secondary correctly gave the incoming weapon its own independent magazine and preserved the outgoing weapon's exact remaining count across the switch.
- **AI Perception / Stealth (Priority B)** -- a bounded Perception layer on the AI State Machine (`ai.perception = {visionDistance, fovDegrees, requiresLOS, hearingRadius}`, defaulted lazily so every pre-Wave-9 State Machine keeps behaving exactly as before until a perception condition is actually authored). Real Vision Field (`canSeePlayer`/`cannotSeePlayer`): distance, then angle inside the authored FOV cone using the AI's own REAL current orientation (`entity.forward` -- no second heading model), then optionally Line Of Sight (reusing the exact existing `hasLineOfSightToPlayer` ray). Real deterministic Hearing: a shared `Emit Noise` action (also automatically callable from weapon fire via `weapon.emitNoiseOnFire`/`noiseRadius`) broadcasts a noise event immediately to every placed AI within both the noise's own radius and that listener's own Hearing Radius -- no persistent event queue, no audio analysis. A new `heardNoiseRecently` transition condition and a new `investigate` movement type (moves toward the last heard position via direct movement, no new pathfinder, turning to face it) complete Patrol -> Alert -> Investigate -> Patrol. Suspicion/gradual awareness deliberately NOT built -- these three conditions already covered the required guard-encounter dogfood cleanly; see the Wave 9 report's honest scope accounting.
  - Dogfooded live with a real placed State Machine guard (Patrol/Alert/Investigate states) against the actual current Player: facing away from the Player at close range correctly read `canSeePlayer: false`; turning to face the Player correctly transitioned Patrol -> Alert on the very next tick; turning back away correctly transitioned Alert -> Investigate (`cannotSeePlayer`); a real `emitNoiseEvent` call at the Player's position was correctly received (`_lastHeardNoisePos`/`_lastHeardNoiseAt` set) and the Investigate state correctly turned the guard to face the heard position each tick, exactly matching Section 40's required "hides behind wall -> LOS lost -> searches" flow (re-detecting the Player once turned to face where the sound came from is the CORRECT emergent behavior, not a test artifact -- confirmed by inspecting state directly, not assumed).
- **Camera Shake (Priority C)** -- a real reusable primitive: `Camera.shakeOffset` (new, additive-only, `null` by default) layered on top of whatever mode/authored settings the `eye` getter already computes, never mutating `target`/`_chaseEye`/`fixedOptions` themselves, so an active shake never corrupts any authored Camera setting. Simulation-time driven (freezes correctly on pause), linear falloff, a fresh trigger replaces rather than stacks with an in-flight one. New shared `Camera Shake` action (Behaviors/Node Graph/Animation Events/Sequences alike) plus a per-weapon optional `fireCameraShake` and the new Player Damage Feedback hook.
- **Audio volume-ramp API + Audio Zone crossfade (Priority G)** -- the real engine-level gap Wave 8's own report named: `AudioEngine.playBuffer(...)`'s returned handle gained `setVolume(value, rampSeconds)`, real Web Audio gain automation (`cancelScheduledValues`/`setValueAtTime`/`linearRampToValueAtTime`), not a manual timer, correctly replacing (not stacking with) an in-flight ramp. Audio Zone gained `Fade Duration` (0 keeps the exact Wave 8 hard-switch, so no existing Zone changes behavior) and `Priority` (highest active zone's ambience wins; a lower-priority zone entered while a stronger one plays is a geometric no-op; leaving the currently-audible zone falls back to the level's own persistent Ambient Audio). Known bounded gap, documented rather than solved with a full N-deep zone stack: true 3+-zone overlap stacking is deferred -- see the Wave 9 report.
- **Weapon dependency-collector regression (Priority A/Section 105)** -- the new Empty Sound asset reference (Fixed Weapon and per-Equipment-Item) flows through the SAME generic `collectProjectDependencies()` Player Settings scan Wave 8 built, not a new feature-specific scanner; Audio Zone's own `audioAssetId` field already flowed through the Wave 8 declarative ACTION_TYPES field-bucket scan with zero new code needed.
- **Full catalog smoke regression** -- all 24 shipped games (`games/*.json`) loaded, entered, and exited Play through the real `loadProjectFull`/`enterPlayMode`/`exitPlayMode` functions with zero console errors after every Wave 9 change above landed.

### Capability Expansion Wave 9B (closed out -- finished Wave 9's own bounded gaps; see CAPABILITY-WAVE-9-REPORT.md)

- **`games/arsenal-run.json`** -- the required First-Person shooter Wave 9 never shipped as a finished game. Two genuinely different Equipment weapons (Sidearm: Semi, 10-round magazine, low recoil/spread; Rifle: Automatic, 28-round magazine, faster fire rate, stronger recoil/spread), granted and auto-equipped on Start, three ranged State-Machine guards (Perception-driven Patrol/Chase/Attack, `EnemyBolt` bundled projectile prefab), a level-spanning "stay"-trigger Lose watcher (Player Health <= 0), and an Extraction Win trigger. Fully live-dogfooded: 6/6 ammo-to-empty-and-blocked, partial reload math, mid-reload fire block, independent per-slot ammo across a real Sidearm/Rifle switch, real Hitscan damage landing on a placed guard, Win and Lose both fired via real trigger-volume/state-machine paths (not called directly), and a genuine Save mid-session (partial Sidearm 7/30, partial Rifle 23/84, Rifle active) round-tripped EXACTLY through a fresh session's Load. Verified in a real clean-room `<iframe srcdoc>` Playable Export: Start Screen dismissed, Player spawned, both weapons equipped, and a real shot fired taking the magazine from 10 to 9, entirely inside the isolated realm.
  - **Real bug found and fixed live**: `enterPlayMode()` (and `switchToLevelDuringPlay()`) ran `runStartBehaviors()` BEFORE `spawnPlayer()` -- so ANY On Start behavior that touches the Player (Equip Item chief among them) saw `player === null` for the entire duration of every Start behavior, on every genuine fresh Stop->Play cycle, and silently no-oped. Undiscovered until now because nothing before Arsenal Run's own "grant + equip both weapons on Start" pattern ever called Equip Item from Start (Wave 7's Equipment dogfood only ever equipped via a runtime Interact, always well after spawn). Fixed by spawning the Player BEFORE Start behaviors run, in both call sites. Reproduced with a genuinely nulled `player` (not a stale leftover reference, which is what had been masking this bug in ad-hoc console testing) and re-verified fixed.
- **`games/silent-sector.json`** -- the required stealth/infiltration game, the full AI Perception dogfood Wave 9 proved live but never shipped. Three FOV/hearing-driven State-Machine guards (Patrol -> Alert -> Investigate -> Chase-equivalent -> a `caught` state whose Entry Action is Lose), a facility with a real LOS-blocking wall placed specifically to prove FOV Case C, an objective chain (Reach Security Wing -> Steal the Data Core -> Reach Extraction, gated on actually holding the Data item), and Win/Lose both real. Live-dogfooded all three required FOV cases directly against `aiCanSeePlayer` (Case A: close behind, not seen; Case B: in front, clear LOS, seen; Case C: geometrically inside the FOV cone but LOS-blocked by a real wall, not seen), hearing (a noise emitted away from an unseeing guard correctly set `_lastHeardNoisePos` and drove Patrol -> Investigate), the full three-objective Win chain end-to-end via real trigger volumes and a real Interact, and Lose via a guard closing to catch range in Alert. Clean-room `<iframe srcdoc>` export verified: Start Screen dismissed, Player spawned, all three guards present, objective active, inside a genuinely isolated realm.
- **Movement-generated noise (Player Settings)** -- `movementNoiseEnabled` (opt-in; every pre-Wave-9B project is a complete no-op) plus `walkNoiseRadius`/`sprintNoiseRadius`/`crouchNoiseRadius`, emitted through the exact same `emitNoiseEvent` AI hearing already reads, on the SAME bounded per-footstep interval cadence the existing footstep-audio system already established (never once-per-frame). Verified live: crouch-radius noise reached a listener 1.2 units away but not one 5 units away; sprint-radius noise reached one 15 units away -- confirmed `crouch < walk < sprint` exactly as specified.
- **Audio Zone overlap-stacking fix (Sections 37-40)** -- replaced Wave 9's single "current winner" scalar with a real active-zone membership `Map` (`activeAudioZones`), recomputed on every Enter/Exit (`recomputeAudioZoneWinner`). This is the actual fix for the bounded gap Wave 9 shipped and documented: exiting the current highest-priority zone while a second, lower-priority zone is STILL entered now correctly reveals that second zone's ambience, instead of falling straight through to level ambient. A winner that doesn't change (entering/leaving a zone that was never going to win) is still a genuine no-op, no fade restarted. Verified live with a synthetic A(pri 1)/B(pri 2) scenario: enter A -> A audible; enter B -> B audible; exit B -> A audible again (the fix); exit A -> level ambient; re-enter B then a lower C -> B stays audible (no-op confirmed).
- **Full catalog + both new games smoke regression** -- all 26 games (24 existing + `arsenal-run.json` + `silent-sector.json`) loaded, entered, and exited Play with zero console errors. Targeted deep checks: Iron Arena's Fixed Weapon still fires 30 unthrottled shots with `ammoMode` correctly `undefined` (Infinite, unaffected by Weapons 2.0); Relic Frontier's State-Machine Guard still ticks its original `distanceToPlayerAtMost` transitions correctly with `perception` correctly `undefined` (no forced default behavior change for a State Machine that never authored perception).
- **Honest scope accounting**: Scatter Layer UI/Brush, Camera Path authoring, Fog Zones, Prefab Parameter asset types (Mesh/Texture/Audio), Collections, ADS, Suspicion, and weapon muzzle/impact effects were NOT built this pass -- see `CAPABILITY-WAVE-9-REPORT.md` for the full accounting and reasoning.

### Capability Expansion Wave 9C (closed out -- finished the world-authoring/cinematic side of Wave 9; see CAPABILITY-WAVE-9-REPORT.md)

- **Scatter Layers -- real saved authoring surface + Brush (Priority A)** -- `level.scatterLayers = [{id, name, seed, sources:[{prefabName,weight}], scaleMin, scaleMax, randomYaw, minSpacing, maxSlope, brushRadius, density}]`, a persisted level-scoped definition (mirrors `objectiveDefs`'/`sequences`' own sync pattern) that CONFIGURES the existing Wave 8 weighted `performScatter` machinery -- no second Scatter implementation. A real editor workspace (Add/Rename/Delete/Select Layer, weighted Source list, all the numeric fields) plus a genuine Paint/Erase Brush on real Pointer Events (mirroring the existing Sculpt/Terrain-Paint gesture shape): one continuous drag = one Undo step (`scatterFillRegion`, the pushUndo-free core split out of `performScatter` for exactly this), a bounded per-dab cadence (not once-per-pointermove-event), and Erase strictly filtered to the selected Layer's own `scatterLayerId`-owned objects. Scatter Layer prefab sources flow through the existing generic dependency collector -- no `usedScatterPrefabNames()`.
  - **Real bug found and fixed live**: `scatterLayerId` (Wave 8's own ownership tag) was never added to the cube-record Save/Load whitelist (`serializeCubeRecord`/`instantiateCubeRecord`) -- every Scatter Layer's ownership was silently dropped by Save/Export since Wave 8 introduced it, undiscovered because nothing before this Wave's own Forest Range Save->Reload dogfood ever actually reloaded a saved project and checked. Fixed by adding it to both the serialize and instantiate whitelists. Reproduced (a fresh reload of a real painted Forest Range showed 0 owned objects) and re-verified fixed (28 owned objects survived a genuine reload) live.
  - **A second, smaller pre-existing gap fixed in passing**: Duplicate Level cloned `objectiveDefs` into its own local variable but never actually included it in the new level's pushed object, silently dropping every Objective Def on Duplicate Level since Wave 7. Fixed alongside the new Scatter Layer id-remapping this same edit added.
  - **Forest Range dogfooded through the REAL UI**, not helper calls: created a "Forest" Layer via real button clicks, added 3 weighted Prefab sources (Pine 45/Oak 35/Rock 20) via real dropdown/input events, painted via genuine dispatched `PointerEvent` drag gestures (56 objects across 3 strokes), erased a corridor with a real Erase-mode drag (36 removed, the pre-existing 426 hand-placed objects and terrain untouched), painted a denser grove, Saved, and reloaded fresh -- Layer metadata and per-object ownership both verified intact (28 objects, matching the post-ownership-fix repaint). Clean-room Playable Export verified in an isolated realm (483 total objects, 28 layer-owned).
- **Camera Path (Priority B)** -- the authoring/runtime bridge between three already-existing systems (Path/Spline storage, `samplePathPosition`, and the "repurpose Camera's `fixed` mode as an interpolation vehicle" trick Camera Blend already established) -- no second spline implementation, no second camera architecture. A shared `Play Camera Path` action (Path, Duration, Loop, Look Mode -- Along Path/Look At Object/Fixed Target, Return To Previous Camera) reachable from Behaviors/Node Graph/**Sequence** with zero Sequence-specific code (`Object.entries(ACTION_TYPES)` already populates the Sequence step dropdown generically). Cleaned up on Stop/Restart/Switch Level alongside Camera Blend's own existing cleanup. Live-verified: real position/orientation tracking along an actual 6-point Forest Range path; a real "Return To Previous Camera" restoring a previously-applied Camera object's exact settings after completion; a 50-point synthetic spiral path run through 3 full loops (300 ticks) with zero NaN and no unexplained drift.
- **Silent Sector cinematic upgrade** -- a real `IntroFlyover` 6-point Path plus an `IntroCinematic` Sequence (Freeze Player -> Play Camera Path -> Delay(same duration) -> Unfreeze Player, the bounded "author knows the Duration" completion signal, no async Sequence machinery), fired from Start. Live-verified: correctly frozen and mid-flight at the halfway point, correctly unfrozen with the path finished once both the path's own duration and the matching Delay elapse; the FP controller's own per-frame camera write naturally retakes `fixed` mode once unfrozen, needing no explicit "restore camera" step. Verified in a clean-room export: the intro auto-fires correctly inside an isolated realm.
- **Fog Zones (Priority C) + reusable zone-membership pattern** -- reuses the exact `Map`-based active-zone-membership shape the Wave 9B Audio Zone fix established (highest-priority-wins, exiting the top zone reveals the next-highest still-active one, never straight to base) for `skyHorizon`/`fogDensity` specifically -- sun direction/shadows/panorama are never touched. A new `Set Fog Zone` action (Fog Color, Fog Density, Blend Duration, Priority). The pattern is mirrored, not literally unified into one author-facing "Environment Zone" object (Section 44's own "internal reuse matters more than forced unification" -- the UI stayed clearer as two separate actions). Live-verified with the identical A/B/C overlap sequence already proven for Audio: exiting a higher-priority zone correctly reveals a still-active lower one.
- **Power Grid environment upgrade** -- a real nested-overlap pair (`GeneratorRoomZone` priority 1 containing `GeneratorCoreZone` priority 2) plus a separate `CoolantAreaZone`, all Audio+Fog together. Live-verified through REAL trigger volumes in the actual game: entering the Room settles fog density to 0.09, entering the nested Core settles it to 0.16, and leaving the Core correctly falls back to the Room's own 0.09 (not level base 0.035) -- the overlap fix proven end-to-end in a real game, not just a synthetic test. Puzzle logic untouched; clean-room export re-verified (objective still activates correctly).
- **Weapon/switch torture (bounded scale)** -- 300 Semi-fire attempts through real ammo/reload state consumed exactly 40 rounds (Sidearm's full 10+30 economy) with zero negative ammo; 300 Automatic-fire attempts (fresh session) consumed exactly 112 rounds (Rifle's full 28+84 economy) with zero negative ammo; 100 weapon switches returned to the correct starting slot with stable object count (14, no leaks), stable `playerEquipment`, and each slot's own independent runtime state intact.
- **Full catalog regression** -- all 26 games (including the two new ones) load/enter/exit Play with zero console errors from a genuinely fresh page load; Crate Run's fresh-boot invariants (21 crates, 5 boost pads, Hovercraft, terrain, water) re-confirmed directly (not by name-string guessing -- via the same `behaviors`-based classification `itemLabel` itself uses).
- **Honest scope accounting**: Prefab Parameter Mesh/Texture/Audio asset types, weapon muzzle/impact effects, formal 1,000/2,000-shot and 30-guard and 1,000-object-Scatter torture at the LITERAL specified scale, dedicated touch/gamepad verification sessions, and new focused Node-side unit tests were NOT done this pass -- see `CAPABILITY-WAVE-9-REPORT.md`.

### Capability Expansion Wave 9D (closed out -- final closure pass; see CAPABILITY-WAVE-9-REPORT.md)

- **Prefab Parameter Mesh/Texture/Audio Asset types (Priority A)** -- three new parameter types (`meshAsset`/`textureAsset`/`audioAsset`), each with a real Asset Library dropdown selector (both for a parameter's own Default and an instance's Override -- never a raw typed id) and a centralized live-swap helper (`applyMeshAssetToItem`/`applyTextureAssetToItem`, reusing `getAssetMeshHandle`/`applyTextureToEntity` -- no GPU resource duplication; a skinned-mesh swap is refused cleanly rather than corrupting a rig). All three flow through the existing generic dependency collector with zero new scanners -- the `meshAsset`/`textureAsset`/`audioAsset` collector cases already existed from an earlier pass but were unreachable until this Wave's authoring UI made the types creatable. Dogfooded with a 20-variant "Supply Crate" prefab (Mesh/Texture/Audio/Color/Scale params, half the instances overridden): all three reference sources (parameter default, instance override, Spawner per-wave override) correctly discovered by the collector; Save/Load round-trip confirmed exact.
  - **Real bug found and fixed live**: `cloneBehaviors`'s fallback branch was a SHALLOW `{ ...b }`, leaving every non-legacy action's own `params` object as one SHARED reference across every instance ever cloned from the same source (prefab spawn, Duplicate, Array, Scatter). Harmless until something mutated `params` in place after cloning -- the new `soundAsset` Prefab Parameter binding is the first thing that does, and setting one instance's audio override silently overwrote every sibling's params too. The exact same bug CLASS Wave 8 already fixed once for a shared mutable `color` array; a different field, same root cause, same fix shape (per-instance shallow copy).
  - **A second real bug found and fixed**: a Spawner's own per-wave `paramOverrides` were never actually scanned by the dependency collector's Spawner branch -- the `scanPrefabParams` helper needed for it already existed (its own comment explicitly anticipated "per-Spawner-wave" reuse) but the Spawner branch had never been wired to call it. Fixed with a one-line addition reusing that existing helper.
- **Weapon muzzle/impact effects + Tracer (Priority B)** -- reuses the existing Particle Emitter (burst mode, the same primitive Hovercraft boost exhaust already uses) and `app.audio.playBuffer` -- no new VFX graph. Muzzle effects fire from the weapon's own real resolved origin (Socket if configured, else Player root -- never a second guessed origin) on every actual shot, never on an empty/reload/cooldown-blocked attempt.
