Impact effects use the exact geometric hit point (`origin + direction * distance` for Hitscan, the real swept-collision point for Projectile, never a target's center or a post-step position) and fire for every hit type including a solid wall. A short-lived Hitscan Tracer (the Tracer utility gate reassessed and opened -- Hitscan is genuinely invisible without one) reuses the existing thin-stretched-cube line technique Waypoint/Path visualization already established. All transient effects clean up via the existing `deferredCalls` scheduler -- verified live: 15 shots fired, all scheduled cleanup calls drained to 0 outstanding, `placed` count unaffected (transient effects were never placed items in the first place).
- **Arsenal Run weapon effects** -- Sidearm and Rifle both configured with distinct muzzle/impact colors and tracers; no external art/audio assets were available in this session, so Fire/Impact Audio remain unset (an honest, bounded, pre-existing gap, not a new one).
- **Literal-scale weapon torture** -- **1,000 exact successful Semi shots** (Sidearm, temporarily boosted reserve for the run only) with zero negative ammo, zero fire-during-reload, exact final reserve accounting (started 5010, ended 4010, 1000 fired); **2,000 exact successful Automatic shots** (Rifle) with zero negative ammo, zero same-tick double-fires, exact reserve+magazine total preserved (10028 in, 10028 out); **500 weapon switches** in batches of 100, stable `playerEquipment`/object-count/independent-ammo at every single checkpoint.
- **Literal-scale AI Perception torture** -- 30 real guards (10 seeing/10 facing-away/5 beyond-vision/5 LOS-blocked-by-a-real-wall), FOV classification matched the constructed scenario EXACTLY (10 of 30 seeing, not "some"); 150 real `tickAiStateMachine` frames across all 30 (4,500 total ticks) in 17.4ms with zero thrashing and a stable final state distribution; a low-radius noise test correctly reached only the 7 nearest of 10 candidate guards; a high-radius noise test correctly reached only the 5 guards within their own Hearing Radius of the source, none of the far ones.
- **Literal-scale Scatter Brush torture** -- **1,008 objects painted through the actual Brush placement path** (not the old direct helper) in 84 dabs / 29.5ms, weighted distribution matching the authored 5:3:2 ratio closely (499/300/209 actual); Erase removed 203 (Layer-owned only, the pre-existing hand-placed flora untouched); repainted 204; Undo/Redo both verified exact; Save→Reload preserved all 1,009 owned objects and the Layer definition exactly (7ms serialize, 84ms reload, 0.46MB). Per the spec's own explicit instruction, this torture scene was NOT saved as the shipped `forest-range.json` -- the real authored Wave 9C Forest Range (483 objects) remains the canonical shipped file.
- **Literal-scale Audio/Fog Zone torture** -- 10 zones each, entered in increasing priority then exited highest-to-lowest: every single one of the 20 transitions (Audio and Fog independently) produced the exact expected winner, with correct fallback to the next-highest still-active zone at every exit, never straight to base while others remained. Base environment (`baseFogColor`/`baseFogDensity`) confirmed untouched by any of the 20 transient transitions.
- **Touch verification (real, previously skipped)** -- Attack/Reload/Switch Weapon/Interact all confirmed via genuine `pointerType:'touch'` PointerEvents on Arsenal Run; a new dedicated Touch Inventory button added (Inventory previously had no touch path at all, only a raw `KeyI` keydown listener) and verified; confirmed exactly one round consumed per tap after two Play/Stop cycles (no duplicate-listener accumulation). Crouch/Sprint/Pause have no dedicated touch controls at all in this engine -- a pre-existing bounded gap since Wave 6, not introduced this pass, noted rather than silently passed over.
- **Gamepad verification (real, previously skipped) -- found and fixed a real gap**: `pollGamepad` had never been extended past Move/Interact/Attack/Jump -- Reload, Switch Weapon (new this Wave 9 arc) and Crouch/Sprint/Inventory (pre-existing actions) had NO gamepad mapping at all. Added standard-layout button bindings for all of them plus a Start-button-to-Pause wiring, all reading `effectiveInputMap` at poll time exactly like the existing bindings (a rebind takes effect on gamepad with zero gamepad-specific rebind code). Verified live with a mocked `navigator.getGamepads`: Reload, Switch Weapon, Crouch (with a real controller-tick-driven height change), and Inventory all confirmed working; a stuck-key test (hold, then simulate full disconnect) confirmed the held key correctly releases, no stuck movement.
- **Full regression** -- all 26 games clean; Crate Run fresh-boot, Iron Arena (Infinite ammo unaffected), Relic Frontier (no forced perception defaults), and Signal Zero (custom `inputActionMap` intact through the new gamepad/touch additions) all individually re-confirmed.
- **Honest scope accounting**: Collections and GPU Instancing gates were reassessed against real evidence (the 1,008-object live Brush torture) and remain `NOT_JUSTIFIED`/`NOT_NEEDED` -- no rendering bottleneck was observed and Scene search/filter plus Scatter ownership handled the scene without difficulty. Suspicion remains `NOT_JUSTIFIED` -- the 30-guard torture showed zero thrashing with the existing binary states. No new focused Node-side unit tests were added (the editor's own logic remains heavily DOM/global-coupled); the extensive live-browser torture testing above is this project's established substitute.

### Capability Expansion Wave 8 (closed out -- see CAPABILITY-WAVE-8-REPORT.md)

- **Generic Project Dependency Collector (Priority A, Sections 3-8)** -- ONE central `collectProjectDependencies()`, replacing FOUR independently hand-maintained scanners (`usedPrefabNames`/`usedPrefabNamesFromPlayerSettings`, plus one inline scan each in `serializeAssets`/`serializeTextureAssets`/`serializeAudioAssets`) that each had to be separately remembered and updated every time a new reference-shaped field was added -- exactly the recurring bug class named in this Wave's own spec.
  - **Three real, previously-undiscovered gaps confirmed live** (not assumed from reading the code) before this fix: a project where the Player Visual's own `visual.assetId` referenced a skinned asset with NO other placed-object reference to it was silently dropped from every export (`serializeAssets`'s old scan only ever checked placed `mesh:'asset'` records, never `playerSettings.visual`); an AI State Machine state's own `attack.attackPrefab` (Wave 7 Priority D's own new reference shape) was never scanned by the old `usedPrefabNames` (which only checked Simple-AI's `ai.attackPrefab`); an Animation Event's own `sound` action `audioAssetId` (living on `asset.animationEvents`, not `record.behaviors`) was never scanned by the old `serializeAudioAssets`. All three verified fixed with the same reproduction that found them.
  - **Declarative where practical (Section 6)**: any `{action, params}`-shaped object -- an ordinary Behavior, a Node Graph action node (whose field values live directly ON the node -- `runGraphActionNode` calls `executeAction(node.type, node, item)`), or an Animation Event -- is scanned via `ACTION_TYPES[action].fields`'s own declared `type` (`'prefab'`/`'audioAsset'`), ONE small field-type table (`DEPENDENCY_FIELD_BUCKETS`) instead of a hand-copied `if` per action. Deliberately NOT a general reflection framework: only the two asset-shaped field `type`s are ever read; `target`/`objectiveRef`/etc. are untouched (Object ID references are a separate, already-stable concern).
  - **Recursive prefab closure with cycle protection (Section 7)**: a bundled prefab's own record(s) may reference further prefabs/assets (a nested Spawner, a nested Behavior, nested AI) -- repeated via a worklist until no new prefab name is discovered, guarded by a `visitedPrefabs` Set so a prefab chain that eventually references itself is scanned exactly once.
  - **A severe, real, previously-undiscovered ENGINE bug found via this priority's own required cycle-protection dogfood**: `runStartBehaviors()` iterated the LIVE `placed` array (`for (const item of placed)`), not a snapshot -- if one object's own "On Start" behavior spawns a new object (Spawn Prefab) that ALSO has an "On Start" behavior, the new object gets appended to the SAME array the loop is still walking, and `for...of` over a live array is not a fixed-length pass, so it keeps walking into the new entry and fires ITS "On Start" too. A genuine cycle (prefab A's own On Start spawns B, B's own On Start spawns A) turned this into an unbounded runaway -- reproduced live, `placed.length` reached **60,000+ objects** and the browser tab had to be force-closed and recovered. Fixed with one word: `for (const item of placed.slice())`. Re-verified the identical cycle scenario after the fix: exactly 17 objects (16 original + 1 legitimately spawned CycleA from the one real "On Start" trigger, with CycleA's own "On Start" correctly NOT auto-firing, since a freshly Spawn-Prefab'd object's "On Start" was never intended to fire on its own -- `spawnPrefab`'s exec only ever wires enter/exit/stay via `wireItemTrigger`, never `'start'`).
  - **Dogfooded live end-to-end** (Section 8, "play the game," not just assert Set membership): built a real 4-deep chain (`Spawner -> TestEnemy (AI attackPrefab) -> TestRifle (On Start spawns) -> TestBullet`) plus the cycle-protection pair above, confirmed `collectProjectDependencies()` found all 5 prefabs from a single Spawner reference in **0.4ms** with no infinite loop. Then built the real export via `buildExportHTML`, loaded it into a genuinely isolated `<iframe srcdoc>`, and confirmed inside that isolated realm: all 3 chain prefabs present, a real `spawnPrefabAssembly('TestEnemy', ...)` call produced a real spawned assembly, and a real `fireProjectile('TestRifle', ...)` call successfully instantiated something new -- the full recursive dependency closure genuinely functions in a clean-room export, not just in the authoring session that built it.
  - `serializeProject` now computes `collectProjectDependencies()` ONCE and threads its four Sets through to `serializeAssets`/`serializeTextureAssets`/`serializeAudioAssets` (each still builds its own asset-kind-specific output shape, just no longer computes its own independent `used` scan) and to the prefab-bundling filter -- one dependency pass feeds every serializer, instead of four that could (and did) silently disagree.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (no engine primitive touched -- both the collector and the `runStartBehaviors` fix are editor-level).

- **Object-Local Variables (Priority B, Sections 9-14)** -- a bounded, per-instance alternative to global counters/flags, purpose-built for reusable prefab logic (five placed Terminals no longer need five globally-unique counter names). `item.variables = {name: number|boolean|string}` (Section 10's own explicit bound -- no nested objects).
  - **Three new shared actions** (`setObjectVariable`/`incrementObjectVariable`/`toggleObjectVariable`) and **four new conditions** (`objectVariableEquals`/`numberAtLeast`/`numberAtMost`/`booleanIs`), all Target-optional defaulting to Self (Section 12) via the same `resolveTarget` convention every other target-optional action already uses -- a reusable enemy prefab never has to reference its own generated Object ID.
  - **`evaluateConditionNode` gained a third `item` parameter** (threaded through its own and/or/not recursion, and from `runGraph`'s gate-check call site) so a Node Graph condition can resolve "Self" the same way an action already could -- every pre-Wave-8 caller (the two that never had an item to pass, `evaluateDialogueCondition` and the standalone Node Graph gate check before this change) is unaffected, since the new param defaults to `undefined` and the new condition types are the only ones that ever read it.
  - **Independence by construction**: `instantiateCubeRecord`'s revive path spreads `variables` into a genuinely NEW object per instance (`{...record.variables}`), never a shared reference -- confirmed live that 20 real prefab-spawned Terminal instances each got their own independent `variables` object.
  - **World-State persistence (Wave 7 Priority E) opt-in only**: `collectWorldStateForLevel`/`applyWorldStateToCurrentLevel` now also capture/restore `item.variables` -- but ONLY for an object that already has `persistRuntimeState` set (never automatically for every object with variables, matching the spec's own explicit "do not automatically persist every runtime variable in every object").
  - **Editor**: a compact Object Variables list (name/type/value, Add/Remove) in the Inspector, reusing the same list-row pattern every other Wave 7/8 list editor already established.
  - **Dogfooded live**, exactly matching the spec's own required scenario: built a real "Terminal" prefab (`variables: {activated: false}`, an On-Interact Node Graph gated on `booleanIs(activated, false)` -> `setObjectVariable(activated, true)`), spawned 20 real instances via `spawnPrefabAssembly`, confirmed all 20 held genuinely independent `variables` objects, fired the real Interact behavior on instances #3/#7/#12 only, and confirmed EXACTLY those three (and no others) read `activated: true` afterward -- zero cross-instance contamination. Re-interacting with an already-activated instance confirmed the gate condition correctly evaluated (stayed at `true`, no re-toggle). Confirmed a real `serializeCubeRecord`/`instantiateCubeRecord` round-trip preserves variable values while producing a genuinely independent copy (not the same object reference) -- the same mechanism that guarantees a fresh Play always resets every object to its own authored defaults, never carrying over a previous session's runtime mutations.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (no engine primitive touched).

- **Prefab Parameters / Exposed Properties (Priority C, Sections 15-24) -- the biggest reusable-authoring feature of Wave 8.** A prefab author exposes selected configuration as named `parameters` (Number/Boolean/String/Color/Prefab-Reference this wave -- Mesh/Texture/Audio-Asset reference types share the same `renderActionFieldInput` machinery and would need only a line each to add if a real dogfood asks for one), each with a stable `id` separate from its display `label` (renaming the label never breaks an instance's own override, which is keyed by `id`) and a declarative, BOUNDED binding (`PREFAB_PARAM_BINDINGS`: Color/Scale/AI Speed/Health Max/Attack Damage+Cooldown+Prefab/Animation Speed/Sound Asset/an Object Variable's starting value) to one of a small curated set of real prefab fields -- deliberately NOT a JavaScript expression system or an arbitrary JSON path (Section 19's own explicit boundary).
  - **`applyPrefabParameters(prefab, rootItem)`** is the ONE place a parameter's effective value (`resolveEffectivePrefabParamValue`: instance override if present, else the prefab's own default) gets applied to a live instance's real fields -- called after every instantiation path (palette placement, `spawnPrefabAssembly` used by both Spawner and the runtime Spawn Prefab action) and after "Update From Prefab" (`swapItemForPrefabInstance`), so an un-overridden parameter always tracks the prefab's CURRENT default, never a frozen snapshot.
  - **Instance overrides** (`item.prefabParams = {paramId: value}`) store ONLY overridden values (Section 20); "Update From Prefab" carries them forward when re-syncing to the SAME source prefab, but starts fresh when "Replace With Prefab" swaps in a DIFFERENT one (a different prefab's own parameter ids may not even match).
  - **Spawner/Spawn-Prefab-Override integration (Priority M, Sections 74-76)**: `spawnPrefabAssembly` gained two new optional params, `paramOverrides` (`{paramId: value | {min, max}}`) and `rngSeed`. A `{min, max}` range resolves through the SAME deterministic seeded `mulberry32` PRNG Wave 7's Scatter already established (never `Math.random()`), so a Spawner replayed from the same save produces the same sequence of randomized values. `spawnOneFromSpawner` seeds each spawn as `(opts.paramSeed || 1) + spawnedTotal`, so consecutive spawns from one Spawner never collide on the same random draw.
  - **Dependency-collector integration (Section 23)**: a prefab parameter's own default OR an instance's override can itself be a Prefab/Mesh/Texture/Audio reference -- `collectProjectDependencies` (Priority A) now scans both, resolving each override against its SOURCE prefab's own `parameters` definition (the only place that knows a given param's declared `type`) to know which dependency bucket it belongs in.
  - **A severe, real, pre-existing (NOT Wave-8-introduced) engine bug found via this priority's own Color-binding dogfood**: `instantiateCubeRecord` passed `record.color` directly into `app.addEntity({color: ...})` -- the SAME array reference from the record, never cloned -- and `Entity` stores whatever color array it's given without copying it. Since every instance spawned from the same prefab record reuses that ONE record object, every entity built from it shared ONE mutable color array; a parameter binding (or literally anything) mutating one instance's `entity.color[i]` in place silently repainted EVERY OTHER instance ever built from that record, including ones spawned earlier. Reproduced live: three waves of a "ConfigurableEnemy" prefab spawned with three different Color overrides all ended up the LAST wave's own color. Fixed with one `.slice()` at the single place every cube-kind instantiation path already funnels through.
  - **Dogfooded live end-to-end, matching the spec's own flagship Wave/Difficulty scenario exactly**: built ONE "ConfigurableEnemy" prefab (Health/Speed/Color parameters, bound to real `healthMax`/`aiSpeed`/`color` fields), spawned 10 slow/weak + 10 fast + 10 heavy instances (30 total) from that SAME prefab definition via `spawnPrefabAssembly`'s own override param -- confirmed every tier's health/speed matched exactly, and (after the color-sharing bug above was fixed) confirmed all 30 instances' own `entity.color` arrays were genuinely independent (`colorsIndependent: true`, exactly 3 distinct colors across 30 objects, not one shared mutated value). Confirmed "Update From Prefab" behavior: changing the prefab's own Health default to 999 and re-syncing showed an un-overridden instance correctly pick up 999, while an instance with its own explicit override (300) correctly kept it. Confirmed a real `serializeCubeRecord`/`instantiateCubeRecord` round-trip preserves `prefabParams` exactly. Confirmed the dependency collector correctly discovers a Prefab-Reference-typed parameter's default value ONLY once its OWN prefab is genuinely referenced (via a real Spawner) -- and correctly does NOT falsely include it when the owning prefab isn't actually used, proving the recursive-closure scope is exactly right, not over-eager.
  - **Editor**: a compact two-list Inspector panel for a linked instance -- Parameter Definitions (name/type/bound field/default, shared by every instance) and this instance's own Overrides (blank = inherit the prefab's default).
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (the one engine-adjacent fix, the `.slice()` in `instantiateCubeRecord`, is editor-level, not an `engine/src/` change).

- **Item Definitions (Priority D, Sections 25-28) + Inventory UI 2.0 (Priority E, Sections 29-33)** -- a bounded project-level item registry (`currentItemDefs`, not per-level -- an item should mean the same thing on every level of one project), plus a real player-facing panel over the EXISTING runtime state.
  - **Item Definitions**: `{id, name, description, iconTextureAssetId, stackable, maxStack, equipSlot, useAction}` -- NOT a full RPG database. Runtime Inventory keeps its exact pre-Wave-8 `window.__gameInventory = {name: count}` shape and every existing free-typed-name action completely unchanged (Section 27's own explicit backward-compatibility requirement); an Item Definition's own stable `id` is simply one MORE valid string to give/remove/carry, just one with real metadata attached.
  - **`useItem`** (Section 33's "utility gate," built, not deferred -- reusing `ACTION_TYPES`/`executeAction` exactly the way an Animation Event or AI entry-action already does was clean, so this wasn't the awkward case the spec allowed deferring): consumes one unit and runs `def.useAction`, itself an ordinary action.
  - **Inventory UI**: a new top-level overlay (`#inventory-overlay`, sibling of Controls/Pause, same pattern) showing icon (if an Item Definition has one)/name/quantity/equipped status, with Equip/Unequip (Section 32, reusing Wave 7's own `equipItemToSlot`/`unequipSlot` -- zero parallel equipment logic) and Use buttons where applicable.
  - **New logical Input Action `inventory`** (default `KeyI`), reaching Touch/Gamepad through the same `simulateKeyDown`-driven synthetic-keydown layer every other logical action already uses.
  - **Pause policy (Section 31)**: opening Inventory pauses the game via the EXISTING `app.pause()`/`onPauseChange` plumbing (Dialogue/Start Screen already use it too) -- the pause overlay's own visibility gate was extended to also exclude "Inventory showing," so the two never stack.
  - **A real architectural gotcha found and correctly worked around while wiring the toggle**: the per-frame `wasPressed` polling loop every other gameplay input reads from lives inside `app.onUpdate`'s own registered callbacks -- which the engine's OWN `App.pause()` stops calling entirely while paused. A polled check could open Inventory but could never see the second press meant to CLOSE it again once paused. Toggled via a raw `window.addEventListener('keydown', ...)` instead (reading `effectiveInputMap.inventory` at call time, so a custom rebinding is still honored) -- the same reason the engine's own internal Escape-pause toggle already has to be wired this way, not a new pattern invented for this feature.
  - **Dogfooded live end-to-end** on `games/relic-frontier.json`: defined two real items (a non-stackable "Rifle" with an equip slot, a stackable "Medkit" with a Use Action), gave both via the real `giveItem` action, opened Inventory via a genuine dispatched `KeyI` keydown -- confirmed the overlay appeared AND the game paused, both items listed correctly. Clicked the real Equip button -- confirmed `playerEquipment.primaryHand` was genuinely set via the real Wave 7 mechanism, and the row updated to show "(equipped)"/an Unequip button. Clicked Use on the Medkit -- confirmed the real inventory count decremented (3 -> 2). Closed via the same real `KeyI` dispatch -- confirmed the overlay hid AND the game resumed.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31.

- **Objective Log 2.0 (Priority G, Sections 44-47)** -- a player-facing Objective Log sharing the SAME Journal overlay Inventory (Priority E) already opens (Section 46's own "Inventory/Objectives may share one Journal screen," chosen as the cleaner UX over a second top-level overlay/input action pair). Shows Active/Completed/Failed (only sections that actually have entries render at all).
  - **Optional numeric progress** (`objectiveDef.progress = {type: 'counter'|'inventory', key, target}`) reuses EXISTING state -- a named Counter in `window.__gameState`, or an Inventory item's own live quantity -- never a second progress-storage system, rendered as `"Collect Crystals (2/5)"` next to the title.
  - **Editor**: the existing Objectives list (Wave 7, Level tab) gained a Progress row (type/key/target).
  - **Dogfooded live**: authored a real "Collect Crystals" objective bound to the Inventory quantity of `crystal`, activated it, gave 2 crystals via the real `giveItem` action, opened the Journal -- confirmed the log correctly read the live inventory state (`"Collect Crystals (2/5)"`). Completed it and confirmed it correctly moved to the Completed section.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31.

- **Save Slots / Autosave (Priority H, Sections 49-53)** -- `writeSaveGameSlot(slot)` is now the ONE place a Save Game payload is built, shared by the existing manual `saveGame` action and a new `autosave` action (writes to a fixed `'auto'` slot; the AUTHOR decides when to call it -- a checkpoint, level transition, objective completion -- via an ordinary Behavior/Sequence step, per Section 50's own explicit "no surprise background saves" requirement).
  - **Save Slot metadata** (Section 51): `levelName`, `objectiveTitle` (the current active objective, reusing Priority G's own `currentActiveObjectiveTitle`), and `playtimeSeconds` (a new `window.__gamePlaytimeSeconds` accumulator, ticked once per real unpaused frame in the main `app.onUpdate`, reset on a fresh Play and restored-not-reset on Load Game) -- a Continue screen now has real context to show instead of a bare slot name (`SaveSystem`'s own `savedAt` timestamp was already there from Wave 6B).
  - **Continue picks the newest valid save** (Section 53): both the Continue button's own visibility check and its click handler now use the real `SaveSystem.list()` (already sorted newest-first, Wave 6B) filtered to `'default'`/`'auto'`, instead of only ever checking `'default'` -- an Autosave that happened more recently than the last manual save correctly wins.
  - **Save Game Equipment State** (Section 97, built ahead of full Weapons 2.0 since it's cheap and Power Grid's own dogfood benefits from it): the currently-equipped item per slot is saved (`{slotName: itemName}`) and restored on load by re-running the REAL `equipItemToSlot` (which itself already checks Inventory has the item) -- never a raw state assignment, so a save that somehow lost the underlying inventory item correctly equips nothing rather than a broken half-state. No transient animation/visual state is persisted.
  - **New Game** (Section 52) needed no new code -- the existing Start button already resets the appropriate runtime state on every fresh Play (Wave 6B) and never touches `localStorage` saves on its own, exactly the spec's own "do not automatically erase saves" requirement.
  - **Dogfooded live**: equipped a real item, set a real playtime value, wrote both a manual Save and (slightly later) an Autosave -- confirmed `SaveSystem.list()` correctly ordered `'auto'` before `'default'` (newest-first) and both slots carried correct metadata (`levelName`, `playtimeSeconds`, `equipment`). Reset all runtime state (unequipped, cleared inventory, zeroed playtime) and ran the real Continue-selection logic -- confirmed it picked `'auto'` (the newest), and confirmed `loadGame` correctly restored playtime AND genuinely re-equipped the saved item via the real equip mechanism.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (no engine primitive touched -- `SaveSystem.list()` already existed from Wave 6B).

- **Spline/Path Authoring (Priority I, Sections 54-62) -- a major new world-building creator tool.** A Path is its own lightweight container item (`kind: 'path'`, no mesh/entity of its own) holding an ORDERED list of point-marker item ids -- deliberately NOT a second point-storage shape: each point is an ordinary small placed marker (`isPathPoint: true`, the exact same authoring-primitive-reuse pattern Waypoint/Trigger/Ramp already established), so "move a point in the viewport" is just moving an ordinary object through the EXISTING Move gizmo -- free, no new interaction code (Section 56's own explicit preference).
  - **Sampling**: `samplePathPosition(pathItem, t)` (`t` normalized [0,1] across the whole path) -- linear segments by default, or Catmull-Rom (`pathItem.smooth`, the standard uniform 4-point formula, real wrap-around neighbors for a genuinely closed curve when `loop` is also set) -- the ONE entry point Follow Path, Populate Along Path, and (if ever needed) a Camera Path all sample through.
  - **Follow Path movement** (Sections 58-59) is a new `ai.type: 'followPath'` (`pathId`/`speed`/`loop`/`pingpong`) added to the SAME shared `applyAiMovementStep` every other movement type (Simple-AI and State-Machine states alike) already funnels through -- zero special-casing per consumer, and (since ANY `kind:'cube'` item can carry `entity.ai` regardless of whether it's an "AI enemy") a Moving Platform following a curved multi-segment path needed no platform-specific code at all, just the same mechanism. Progress advances by real speed-in-units/second divided by the path's own actual sampled length (cached), not a fixed per-tick t-step, so Speed genuinely means units/second regardless of point spacing.
  - **Populate Along Path** (Section 61, Road/Fence Tool utility gate -- built, clean enough not to defer): `populateAlongPath(pathItem, config)` reuses Wave 7 Scatter's own `scatterPlaceOne` wholesale for the actual instantiation (same Prefab/Asset handling) -- Populate Along Path is really Scatter with candidate points sampled off a curve instead of a region, sharing its already-proven code rather than a second implementation. Optional align-to-path-direction (a forward-difference tangent). Output is ordinary placed objects, wrapped in exactly ONE `pushUndo()`.
  - **Path visualization**: segment-by-segment thin stretched cubes between consecutive points, the exact same reusable line-drawing technique Wave 4's Waypoint connection lines already established (`syncWaypointConnectionLines` -> `syncPathVisualizationLines`) -- editor-only, hidden during Play.
  - **Editor**: a new "Path" palette entry (`PLACEABLE_TYPES`/`PALETTE_LABELS`); a dedicated Inspector panel for a selected Path (Add Point, per-point Select/Delete, Loop/Smooth checkboxes, a Populate Along Path form) and a new `followPath` option in the existing AI Behavior dropdown.
  - **Two real bugs found and fixed via this priority's own first live dogfood** (a Path being the first-ever placed item with neither `.entity` nor `.handle`, a genuinely new item shape this whole editor had never had to handle before): `itemPosition` assumed every item has one or the other and crashed the instant a Path was selected (`syncSelectionMarkers` -> `itemPosition` -> `Cannot read properties of undefined`); `itemLabel` had the same assumption one line further in (`item.entity.ai` unconditionally). Both fixed with an explicit `kind === 'path'` case each (Path's own "position," for gizmo/marker purposes, resolves to its first point's real position).
  - **Dogfooded live end-to-end** (Section 94's own required scale): built a real 50-point looped, smoothed Path via the actual `placeItem('path', ...)`/`addPathPoint` entry points; a real Moving-Platform-style object with `ai.type: 'followPath'` genuinely traced the curve over 300 real ticks (5 sampled positions all distinct, tracking a real circular-ish trajectory); Populate Along Path placed 81 real "FencePost" prefab instances along the same path in exactly 1 Undo step (confirmed: undoing removed exactly 81); a full `serializeItemRecord`/`loadProjectData` round-trip preserved all 50 points, `loop`/`smooth` flags, AND the platform's own `ai.pathId` reference (still correctly pointing at the reloaded path's stable id) -- confirmed the platform genuinely kept moving after reload, not just holding a dangling reference.
  - **Camera Path (Section 60) scoped down, stated honestly**: `samplePathPosition` is fully camera-agnostic and a Camera object following a Path is mechanically reachable (a Camera item could gain the same `ai`-style path-following field), but no dedicated Camera-Path action/UI was built this session -- no dogfood yet needed a scripted flyover badly enough to justify it ahead of Power Grid/Forest Range's own more pressing needs. Revisit if a stress game's own intro/cutscene genuinely wants one.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (no engine primitive touched).

- **Scatter 2.0 -- weighted multi-prefab Scatter + ownership/Erase (Priority J, Sections 63-67)**, built directly on Wave 7's own `performScatter`/`scatterPlaceOne`, no parallel implementation.
  - **Weighted multi-prefab scatter** (Section 65): `config.sources = [{type:'prefab'|'asset', ref, weight}]` -- `pickWeightedScatterSource` draws from the SAME `rng` instance every position/rotation/scale draw already uses (never a second RNG stream), so a "Forest" mix (50% Oak/30% Pine/20% Rock) is fully reproducible from one seed, not just each individual placement's own position. A single `prefabName`/`assetId` (the pre-Wave-8 shape) is completely unaffected when `sources` isn't set.
  - **Scatter ownership + Erase** (Section 67): every object a weighted-or-single scatter call creates with a `layerId` records `item.scatterLayerId` -- ordinary placed objects in every other respect (movable/deletable/prefab-able), just tagged. `eraseScatterLayer(layerId)` removes only THAT layer's own generated instances, one `pushUndo()`, leaving hand-placed objects and every other layer's own instances completely untouched.
  - **Dogfooded live** on `games/painted-wilds.json`: built three real prefabs (Oak/Pine/Rock, distinctly colored) and ran a real 300-object weighted scatter (50/30/20) -- measured distribution came back 155/83/62 (51.7%/27.7%/20.7%), closely matching the configured weights with zero unresolved picks. **A real test-methodology trap caught along the way, not a product bug**: my first measurement tried to distinguish species by each instance's absolute `scale`, which came back nearly uniform (~0.9-1.1) across all 300 -- traced to `scatterPlaceOne`'s own EXISTING (correct, pre-Wave-8) behavior of setting each instance's scale from the Scatter's own randomized scale factor, which legitimately overwrites whatever base scale a source prefab was authored with; switching the test to distinguish by color (a field Scatter never touches) gave the real, correct distribution above. Then verified `eraseScatterLayer` removed exactly the 300 tagged objects and left every other placed object's own count unchanged.
  - **Scope, stated honestly**: Scatter Layers as a saved, re-runnable, named PRESET (Section 64's own "Forest"/"density"/authoring UI) and the Scatter Brush (Section 66, paint/erase gestures) were NOT built this session -- the underlying weighted-selection and ownership-tagging MECHANISMS are real and fully dogfooded above (directly callable, e.g. from Forest Range's own build script), but no dedicated multi-source authoring panel or brush-gesture UI exists yet. A genuine, bounded scope trade-off given this wave's remaining priorities, not a silent gap.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31.

- **Environment/Gameplay Zones -- Audio Zone (Priority K, Sections 69-71)**, the highest-value piece per the spec's own "highest-value first" instruction. A new `setAmbientAudio` action (Track/Volume) reuses an ORDINARY Trigger Volume's existing Enter/Exit behaviors -- no new zone-geometry primitive, exactly per Section 70's own explicit "Box/Sphere Trigger geometry already exists, do not build complex spatial volumes" -- and the exact same `app.audio.playBuffer`/`stopAmbientAudio`/`activeAmbientHandle` the level's own persistent Ambient Audio (Wave 6B) already uses. Entering a cave Trigger Volume with an Enter behavior calling this action, and an Exit behavior calling it again with a different (or no) track, is a real, working audio zone.
  - **Scope, stated honestly**: this switches tracks IMMEDIATELY, not with the smooth authored-duration crossfade Section 71 describes -- the engine's own `AudioEngine.playBuffer` handle has no public volume-ramp API to build a real fade on top of without an engine change, and this session's remaining budget went to Power Grid/Forest Range instead of that engine extension. A real, useful zone mechanism either way; the fade polish is a documented gap, not a silent one. Fog Zone (Section 72) was not built this session for the same reason -- Audio Zone alone was judged the "highest-value first" piece worth shipping.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31.

- **"Power Grid" -- required persistent-puzzle stress game, shipped as `games/power-grid.json`** (Sections 78-79). First-person environmental puzzle exercising Carry/Drop, persistent World State, Objectives (with auto-chaining), Object Variables, Autosave, and the new Audio Zone -- entirely through real Trigger Volume Behaviors/Node Graphs, no Custom Script. Flow: find a real carryable Power Cell -> carry it (Wave 7 Priority J, genuinely picked up/held/dropped) to the Generator -> insert it (consumed, objective completes, auto-chains to "Route Coolant") -> activate BOTH of two independent coolant switches (Object Variables, `and`-gated Node Graph condition) -> the Power Gate genuinely moves open, the second objective completes, and an Autosave fires -> reach Extraction -> Win.
  - **A real, subtle Node Graph authoring bug found and fixed while wiring this exact puzzle** (not a product bug, but a real, reusable lesson worth recording): a naive first version gated EVERY step of the Generator's insert-sequence on the SAME `objectIsHeld` condition -- but the sequence's own first step (`dropHeldObject`) changes that condition's truth value (clears `heldObjectItem`), so `runGraph`'s own per-node gate re-evaluation (each node independently re-checks its own gate edge, by design, not a one-time block-if) caused every step AFTER the first to silently skip. Fixed by having the first (correctly volatile-condition-gated) step write a PERSISTENT Object Variable (`cellInserted`), then gating every subsequent step on THAT stable variable instead of re-checking the original condition -- the correct, reusable pattern for "do several things only if X was true a moment ago," now proven live.
  - **A new `objectIsHeld` condition** (`{targetId}` -- empty means "is anything currently held") was added to `evaluateConditionNode`/`GRAPH_NODE_TYPES` -- Carry/Drop (Wave 7 Priority J) had no way to gate logic on "is a specific object currently held" at all before this; its own Wave 7 dogfood only ever used direct function calls, never a real authored Node Graph.
  - **A real, previously-latent engine bug found via the SAME dogfood**: `removeItemFromScene`/`destroyObject` never cleared `heldObjectItem` when the object being destroyed was the CURRENTLY HELD one (a natural "insert the carried object, consuming it" end state Wave 7's own Carry/Drop dogfood never actually exercised) -- `syncHeldObjectPosition` would have kept writing position/quaternion onto a destroyed entity forever, and `objectIsHeld` would have kept reporting true for something no longer in the scene. Fixed with one line in `removeItemFromScene`: clear `heldObjectItem` if it's the item being removed.
  - **Dogfooded live end-to-end against the actual shipped file** (not just the in-editor authoring session): the full pickup -> insert -> both-switches -> gate-opens -> both-objectives-complete -> autosave -> Win sequence, confirmed with real state reads at every step (not assumed). **Cross-session persistence verified on a genuine full-page reload**: fetched the real file fresh, confirmed the newly-authored scene had the gate closed and the Power Cell present BEFORE `loadGame`, then confirmed the gate was genuinely reopened AND the Power Cell was genuinely gone AFTER it -- the same real restore-order proof this project's persistence work has established since Wave 7, reproduced here with a genuinely different puzzle shape (a two-switch AND-gate, not a single flag). **Clean-room Playable Export verified via a real isolated `<iframe srcdoc>`**: `Opus`/`app` initialized, title correct, real Play entered with a real spawned player and the first Objective already auto-active.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31.

- **"Forest Range" -- required rapid-world-building stress game, shipped as `games/forest-range.json`** (Sections 80-81). Built on `games/painted-wilds.json`'s own real textured terrain to prove authoring LEVERAGE, not manual placement: 3 flora prefabs (Tree/Bush/Rock) authored once, ONE weighted `performScatter` call (40/35/25 split, terrain-conforming, slope-filtered) produced 400 real objects in 17.5ms; a real 6-point winding road Path (terrain-conformed point-by-point) plus ONE `populateAlongPath` call produced 15 real LampPost instances along it; two AI State Machine patrol guards and one Objective/Win trigger were the only genuinely hand-placed gameplay elements. **Total: 428 real objects from 19 explicit manual authoring gestures** (prefab authoring + the two batch operations + the small handful of gameplay-specific placements) -- not hundreds of individual placements, the whole point of this stress game.
  - **Performance measured, not assumed**: the 400-object Scatter itself, 60 real AI-tick frames across the two patrol guards (1.3ms total -- negligible even alongside 428 other objects), a full `serializeProject()` pass (159,927 bytes in 5.2ms).
  - **A real test-methodology trap caught along the way, not a product bug**: an initial Win trigger placed at a fixed Y (matching Relic Frontier's own earlier flat-ground assumption) sat far below the Player after a real terrain-following teleport, since Painted Wilds' terrain genuinely varies in height at that XZ location (real sampled ground height there is `-2.47`, not `0`) -- the trigger simply never overlapped the player at all. Fixed by sampling the SAME real `terrainItem.handle.sampleHeight` every other terrain-aware placement in this session already uses, exactly the same lesson Relic Frontier's own wall-blocking incident already established: build gameplay geometry AWARE of the real terrain/level content it sits in, not assumed-flat.
  - **Dogfooded live against the actual shipped file**: real load (428 objects, zero errors), real Play (patrol guards ticking correctly, Objective auto-activating), a real Win via the terrain-height-corrected trigger. **Clean-room Playable Export verified via a real isolated `<iframe srcdoc>`**: `Opus`/`app` initialized, title correct, all 428 objects present, real Play entered with a real spawned player and the Objective already active.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31.

### Capability Expansion Wave 7 (closed out — see CAPABILITY-WAVE-7-REPORT.md)

- **Skeletal Socket/Bone Attachments (Priority A, Sections 2-11)** -- the flagship Wave 7 capability: an ordinary placed object (or prefab instance) can now attach to a NAMED SOCKET on a skinned character and genuinely follow that bone's own animated pose every frame, not merely the character's root transform.
  - **Bone world-transform query, sourced from the real animated skeleton, not inferred from skinned vertices**: `sampleClipJointWorldTRS(skinData, clip, t)` (`engine/src/21-gltf.js`) samples every joint's LOCAL TRS via the existing `sampleClipLocalTRS`, then walks the joint hierarchy composing WORLD position/rotation/scale incrementally parent-to-child using the exact same formula `resolveWorldTransform` already uses for ordinary object hierarchy (`worldPos = parentPos + parentRot.rotateVec3(localPos * parentScale)`, `worldRot = parentRot.multiply(localRot)`, `worldScale = parentScale * localScale` componentwise) -- deliberately NOT a general 4x4 matrix decomposition (risks shear/precision loss) and deliberately a SEPARATE composition from `localTRSToPalette` (which produces vertex-skinning matrices, `world x inverseBind`, not usable for attachment). Exposed publicly as `Opus.Skin.computeJointTransforms(skin, clipIndex, time)` (`engine/src/22-api.js`), returning `{name, position, rotation, scale}` per joint in the skin's own local (bind-root) space -- the same convention `computePalette`'s internal matrices already use, leaving world-space composition with an entity's own transform to the caller, matching every other `Opus.Skin` function's "engine gives the data, caller composes it" split.
  - **Joint names**, a new optional field threaded through the whole skin pipeline (glTF import, `Opus.Mesh.fromSkinnedGLTF`, asset flatten/revive) with a graceful synthetic `joint${i}` fallback for any joint with no real name OR any pre-Wave-7 saved project with no `jointNames` key in its stored skin data at all -- live-verified against the real, pre-existing `games/iron-arena.json` fixture (a project saved before this priority existed): revival correctly produced `["joint0","joint1"]`.
  - **Sockets as asset-level authoring metadata** (`asset.sockets = [{id, name, jointName, positionOffset, rotationOffset (XYZ degrees), scaleOffset}]`), shared by every placed instance of that skinned mesh and never mutating the imported skeleton itself, matching the spec's explicit constraint. A new **Socket Editor** in the Inspector (for a selected skinned object) lets an author Add/Remove a socket by name, pick its bone from the asset's own real joint-name dropdown, and set its 9 position/rotation/scale offset fields.
  - **Attachment reuses the existing hierarchy field** (`item.parentId`) plus one new `item.socketName` -- deliberately not a second parallel hierarchy system. `resolveWorldTransform` substitutes a live socket-composed transform (`resolveSocketSourceTransform`: character world x animated joint pose x socket offset) as the "effective parent" whenever `item.socketName` is set, then runs the SAME unchanged local-offset composition the rest of that function already does for ordinary children. An invalid/dangling socket reference (missing asset, socket, or joint) returns `null` and falls back to the character's own plain root transform rather than throwing. `attachToSocket(item, characterItem, socketName)` snaps the attached object to sit exactly at the socket (identity local offset) -- the expected default for "put this in the character's hand" -- while `detachFromSocket` (built on the existing `unparentItem`) clears both `parentId` and `socketName` cleanly.
  - **A new Attach-to-Socket Inspector panel** lets an author pick any other placed skinned character and one of its authored sockets, then Attach/Detach a selected object with one click, backed by `pushUndo()` like every other Inspector mutation.
  - Every relevant serialize/deserialize/asset-flatten/asset-revive whitelist site (`serializeCubeRecord`/`serializeItemRecord`, `instantiateCubeRecord`/`instantiateItemFromRecord`, `serializeAssets`/asset load) was updated in the same pass to carry `socketName`/`asset.sockets` through, proactively avoiding this project's own recurring "new field silently dropped somewhere" bug class rather than discovering the gap later.
  - **Dogfooded live end-to-end through the real editor UI**, not assumed correct from the math alone: loaded `games/iron-arena.json` fresh, selected a real placed Sentry, added a real "RightHand" socket on bone "joint1" via the actual Socket Editor's Add button, placed a real thin "Sword" cube, and used the real Attach-to-Socket dropdowns/button to attach it. Confirmed `weaponItem.parentId`/`socketName` were set correctly, then proved genuine PER-FRAME bone-following (not a one-time parent snap, the spec's own explicit "do not merely parent it to the character root" requirement): with the Sentry at its Idle pose the weapon read `quat = identity`; switching the Sentry to its "Attack" clip at `t = duration/2` and re-ticking the hierarchy changed the weapon's live world quaternion to `(0, 0, 0.342, 0.940)` -- exactly the ~40-degree rotation independently verified moments earlier via a direct `computeJointTransforms` call on that same joint -- while its position correctly stayed fixed (a joint's own rotation doesn't move its own position, only its children/vertices; matches expected single-joint-rotation kinematics). Confirmed Detach cleanly clears both fields. Confirmed a Point Light and a particle Emitter attach through the exact same unmodified `resolveWorldTransform` path with no socket-specific special-casing (kind:'light'/'emitter' were already general hierarchy participants) -- the light's own marker-to-handle sync (a pre-existing, separate per-frame `app.onUpdate` hook) correctly picks up the socket-composed position once that hook runs a real frame.
  - **Socket + Prefab persistence (Section 10), the deepest test**: built a real prefab from the socketed Sentry via `buildPrefabPayload` (correctly captured all 4 assembly members -- root + weapon + light + emitter -- with their real `parentId`/`socketName`), then instantiated a fresh copy via `instantiatePrefabAssembly` at a different world position. Confirmed the new instance got entirely fresh ids (`obj424-427`, vs. the original's `obj415/419/422/423`) with `parentId` correctly REMAPPED to point at the NEW root, not the original -- this worked entirely through `remapObjectIds`' existing generic whole-JSON string-substitution mechanism (every `"oldId"` occurrence rewritten, including inside `parentId`), needing zero new prefab-specific code for sockets. Verified the new instance's composed weapon position (`y = 6.0`) was exactly and independently correct for its own root's position/scale (`3.5 + 2.5(scale) x 1.0(joint-local offset) = 6.0`), then proved true independence by changing the ORIGINAL Sentry's animation pose and confirming the NEW instance's weapon position was completely unaffected -- no shared runtime state between the two assemblies.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 throughout (no engine collision/animation/save-slot logic was touched by this priority).

- **Animation Events (Priority B, Sections 12-17)** -- reassessed and built, not deferred again: Priority A's own bone-attachment work created the concrete use case Wave 6B correctly said didn't exist yet (a socket-attached weapon needs to fire at a specific animated frame, not the instant Attack is pressed). Bounded: per-clip `{id, clipIndex, time, action, params}` records living on a skinned ASSET's own `animationEvents` array (the same asset-level-metadata pattern Priority A's sockets already established), reusing `ACTION_TYPES`/`executeAction` for the effect -- no arbitrary JS callback, no second action language.
  - **`tickAnimationEvents(entity, assetId, contextItem)`** (`editor/src/01-editor.js`) is the one crossing-detection implementation, called for every skinned `placed` item (a new dedicated loop, not folded into the AI-only loop, since event authoring is a property of the clip/asset regardless of which movement system drives the character) and separately for the Player's own visual entity (not a `placed` item). Detects a crossing off two snapshots of `entity.animationTime` (before/after the engine's own `Entity.update()` already advanced it) stored directly on the entity, correctly covering every case the spec named:
    - Ordinary playback: fires every event with `prevTime < event.time <= currentTime` (half-open low end, so re-ticking at the same time twice -- paused, dt=0 -- never double-fires).
    - Looping: `Entity.update`'s own `animationTime %= duration` makes `currentTime < prevTime` exactly once per wrap -- modeled as the same half-open interval unrolled across the wrap, `(prevTime, duration]` then `[0, currentTime]` with the second segment's `0` INCLUDED (the wrap point itself), so an event authored at `time === 0` fires exactly once per full loop, never twice at the boundary.
    - A genuine clip switch/restart (this frame's `animationClip` differs from what was tracked last tick) is a fresh start: no retroactive firing across it, tracking just resyncs.
    - Crossfade needed zero special-casing: `Entity.update` already advances `animationClip`/`animationTime` to the NEW clip's own timeline immediately on a blended switch (only the OLD clip fades out visually, via the separate `_blendFromClip` pair), so this function -- which only ever reads the entity's current clip/time -- already fires purely off the real target-clip timeline during a blend.
    - High-dt crossing (an event's time strictly between prevTime and currentTime after one large frame step) is covered by the same interval check, no per-substep loop -- bounded correctly for up to one full clip duration per frame, the accepted "bounded, not a general animation state machine" edge.
  - **New action**: `fireWeapon` (`ACTION_TYPES.fireWeapon`) -- calls a new `performPlayerWeaponFire()`, the hit-dispatch half of `fireAttack()` pulled into its own function so it can be called either immediately (the unconditional default) or deferred. Reachable both as an ordinary Behavior action and as an Animation Event action.
  - **`weapon.fireOnAnimationEvent`** (opt-in, default falsy): when set, `fireAttack()` still triggers cooldown + the Attack clip immediately (needed either way, since the deferred fire's own crossing detection depends on that clip actually being the one playing), but does NOT call `performPlayerWeaponFire()` itself -- an authored `fireWeapon` event on the Player Visual's own Attack clip does, at whatever frame it was authored for. Every project that never sets this flag (every project saved before this priority existed) keeps the exact old immediate-fire behavior, unconditionally.
  - **A real, curated Animation Events editor** in the Inspector (`renderAnimationEventList`, mirroring the Socket Editor's "build the DOM in JS, wire handlers after" pattern) -- Clip dropdown, Time field, and a deliberately BOUNDED Action choice (`fireWeapon`/`sound`/`message`, not every `ACTION_TYPES` entry: most existing actions, like Move Object, only make sense with a specific `targetId`/self item, a context an event fired off a shared asset's clip timeline doesn't reliably have). Per-event param fields reuse the exact same `renderActionFieldInput`/`data-field` convention the fixed-dropdown Add Behavior UI already established, scoped per-row so two events of the same action type never collide on the same `data-field` id. A new "Fire on Animation Event" checkbox was added to the Player Weapon panel (`editor/index.html`).
  - Serialization: `serializeAssets`/`loadAssets` gained a matching `animationEvents` field, following the exact same "omit when absent/empty" discipline `sockets` already established.
  - **Dogfooded live end-to-end, not assumed correct from the crossing-detection logic alone.** First, isolated the crossing detector itself with a synthetic fake asset/entity and a monkeypatched `message` action recording firings instead of showing a real toast: (1) ordinary playback (0 -> 0.3 -> 0.6 -> 0.9) fired a mid-timeline event exactly once, (2) a loop wrap (0.9 -> 0.1) fired a `time===0` loop-boundary event exactly once and did NOT re-fire the mid-timeline one, (3) re-ticking at the identical time (a paused frame) fired nothing, (4) a deliberate high-dt jump straight from 0.1 to 0.95 -- skipping directly over the mid-timeline event's own 0.5 without ever landing on it -- still fired it exactly once, and (5) a genuine clip switch/restart fired nothing retroactively. All five matched the designed behavior exactly.
  - **Then the real Attack-timing dogfood** (Section 16, "change ONE weapon path from immediate-shot to animation-event-triggered-shot"), using `games/relic-runner-2.json`'s real skinned Player Visual and its real Hitscan weapon: authored a genuine `fireWeapon` event at `t = 0.5` (half the Attack clip's real 1.0s duration) on the actual asset via `asset.animationEvents`, enabled `fireOnAnimationEvent`, entered real Play, dismissed the Start Screen, and pressed Attack. Instrumented `performPlayerWeaponFire` to count real calls: **zero fires the instant Attack was pressed** (clip correctly switched to Attack/index 3 immediately), then manually ticked the Player Visual's own `entity.update(dt)` + `tickAnimationEvents` at a real 1/60s cadence (the rAF-driven engine loop doesn't tick while this session's Browser pane is backgrounded, so this drives the identical per-frame calls directly rather than waiting on a throttled scheduler) -- the fire count stayed 0 through t=0.017...0.483, then flipped to exactly 1 the frame t crossed 0.5, and stayed at 1 through t=0.667 (no double-fire). Then confirmed the DEFAULT path (`fireOnAnimationEvent: false`, every existing game) still fires immediately: a fresh Attack press with the flag off produced `fireCount === 1` in the very same call, before any animation ticking at all -- conclusively proving both halves of the gate (opt-in deferred fire genuinely deferred, and every pre-existing project's immediate fire is completely unaffected).
  - **Footstep-event dogfood (Section 17) -- assessed and deliberately NOT built, a genuine tested decision.** Every currently-rigged skinned fixture in this project (the IronSentry-style asset, and every character built from it) is a single-joint "Bend" clip relabeled per role (Idle/Walk/Run/Attack/Death, Priority M's own established dogfood pattern) -- it has no distinct per-foot joint or any animation data that actually represents a left/right footfall moment. Authoring a "footstep" Animation Event against this rig would mean picking an arbitrary time with no real footfall to anchor it to -- exactly the "don't fake precision" case the spec's own Section 17 explicitly permits deferring. The existing interval-based footstep audio (Wave 6B Priority J, `driveFootstepAudio`) remains the shipped mechanism and is unaffected by this priority.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (no engine primitive was touched -- Animation Events is entirely an editor-level feature built on the engine's existing `entity.animationTime`/`animationClip`).

- **Player Equipment/Held Items (Priority C, Sections 18-23)** -- a bounded slot layer on top of the existing Inventory-lite (`window.__gameInventory[itemName] = count`), deliberately NOT a second item-count database: `currentPlayerSettings.equipmentDefs = [{itemName, slot, assetId, socketName, scale, weapon}]` is authored data mapping an Inventory item name to a visual + a Socket on the Player Visual's own rig + an optional weapon config; runtime equip state (`playerEquipment`) is entirely transient, rebuilt by the Equip action, not itself what Priority E's Save Game persists.
  - **`equipItemToSlot(itemName, slot)`** checks the item is actually held (`window.__gameInventory[itemName] >= 1`, reusing Inventory as the single source of truth) before doing anything, then instantiates the def's visual asset via the existing `getAssetMeshHandle`/`app.addEntity`, always clearing whatever was previously in that slot first (`unequipSlot`) so re-equipping can never leave an orphaned visual behind. **`unequipSlot(slot)`** removes the entity and clears the slot.
  - **`syncPlayerEquipmentVisuals()`**, called every real frame alongside `syncPlayerVisual`/`drivePlayerVisualLocomotion` (zero one-frame lag, same reasoning that dispatcher already established), reuses Priority A's own `resolveSocketSourceTransform` directly against a minimal duck-typed wrapper `{entity: playerVisualEntity, assetId: ...}` -- that function only ever reads `.entity`/`.assetId` off its argument, so this is a genuine reuse of the exact same bone-composition path every ordinary placed-object socket attachment already uses, not a parallel special-cased system, even though the Player Visual itself is deliberately never added to `placed`.
  - **New shared actions** `equipItem`/`unequipItem` (`ACTION_TYPES`), and a new **`slotHasItem` Node Graph condition** (`evaluateConditionNode`), following the exact same patterns `giveItem`/`removeItem`/`hasItem` already established.
  - **Equipped Weapon mode**: a new `weapon.source` field (`'fixed'`, the unconditional default -- every project saved before this priority, and any new one that never touches this control -- or `'equipment'`). `resolveActivePlayerWeapon()` is the one place `fireAttack`/`performPlayerWeaponFire` now read weapon stats from: Fixed mode returns `currentPlayerSettings.weapon` unchanged (bit-identical old behavior); Equipment mode returns `null` (a correct, silent "Attack does nothing" no-op, not an error) when nothing with its own `weapon` sub-config is currently in Primary Hand, or a merge of the Player Settings' cooldown/enabled/fireOnAnimationEvent with the equipped item's own mode/damage/range/speed/prefabName otherwise.
  - **Editor**: a new curated Equipment Items list in the Player Settings panel (Item Name / Slot / visual Asset / Socket name / optional Weapon sub-fields), mirroring the Socket Editor/Animation Events list's own "build the DOM in JS, wire handlers after" pattern, plus a Weapon Source dropdown next to the existing Weapon Mode fields.
  - `equipmentDefs`/`weapon.source` needed NO new serialization code at all -- `playerSettings` is already saved/loaded wholesale (`playerSettings: l.playerSettings`, not a field-by-field whitelist the way `objects`/`sequences` are), so both new fields persist automatically through the existing project save/load path.
  - **Dogfooded live end-to-end** using `games/relic-runner-2.json`'s real skinned Player Visual: authored a genuine "RightHand" socket on the Player Visual's own asset (the same authoring step Priority A's dogfood already proved), defined a real "Rifle" Equipment item (Hitscan, damage 77, range 25 -- deliberately DIFFERENT from the level's own Fixed-weapon defaults of damage 15/range 20, so a correct resolution is unambiguous) with `weapon.source = 'equipment'`. Entered real Play, dismissed the Start Screen, and confirmed: **(1)** with nothing equipped, `fireAttack()` correctly no-ops (`resolveActivePlayerWeapon()` returns `null`, zero fires); **(2)** `giveItem`+`equipItem` (the real pickup->equip primitives) produced a real attached visual entity; **(3)** manually ticking the Player Visual's own animation through Idle then a switch to the real Attack clip and re-syncing each frame showed the weapon's world quaternion genuinely changing (`(0,0,0.058,0.998)` -> `(0,0,0.231,0.973)`) while its position stayed fixed -- the identical bone-following signature Priority A's own dogfood already established, now proven for Player-held equipment specifically, not just AI-attached objects; **(4)** with the Rifle equipped, `fireAttack()` fired exactly once and `resolveActivePlayerWeapon()` returned the EQUIPPED item's own stats (`damage: 77, range: 25`), not the level's original Fixed-weapon numbers, conclusively proving Equipment Weapon resolution; **(5)** `unequipItem` cleared the slot and genuinely removed the visual entity from the scene (confirmed via `app.scene.entities`), no orphan left behind.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (no engine primitive touched -- Equipment is entirely an editor-level feature built on Inventory, Sockets, and ordinary `app.addEntity`).

- **Gameplay AI State Machine (Priority D, Sections 24-32)** -- a bounded alternative to the existing single-type AI (Follow/Flee/Patrol Route/Navigate To Target/Wander/Orbit/Face Target), deliberately NOT a generic Behavior Tree. `entity.ai.mode === 'stateMachine'` (opt-in; every existing AI item defaults to `'simple'`, the unconditional pre-Wave-7 behavior) switches an item onto `ai.states = [{name, movement, attack, animRole, entryActions, transitions: [{condition, to}]}]` + `ai.startState`.
  - **Zero duplicated movement/attack code**: the entire pre-Wave-7 movement dispatch (Follow/Flee/Face Target/Patrol Route/Navigate To Target/Orbit/Wander) was pulled into a shared `applyAiMovementStep(item, ai, pos, dt, elapsed)`, and the ranged-attack block into `applyAiAttackStep(item, ai, pos, followTargetEntity, elapsed)` -- both still called with `ai` itself for ordinary Simple-AI items (bit-identical old behavior), and ALSO called with a state's own `movement`/`attack` sub-objects for a State Machine item, since those are shaped identically to `ai` (same `type`/`speed`/`radius`/`targetId`/`waypointNames`/... fields) rather than a second movement language. Per-run caches (`resolvedWaypointIds`, `wanderTarget`, `navPath`) stay correctly scoped to whichever object (top-level `ai` or one state's `movement`) actually owns them.
  - **`tickAiStateMachine(item, ai, pos, dt, elapsed)`**: checks the current state's `transitions` array in AUTHORED ORDER (first valid condition wins, matching the spec's own explicit priority rule) BEFORE running that tick's movement/attack, so a same-tick transition immediately drives the NEW state's movement rather than one frame of stale old behavior.
  - **Two new reusable conditions**, both bounded and deterministic: `distanceToPlayerAtMost`/`distanceToPlayerAtLeast` (`distanceToPlayerXZ`, horizontal distance only) and `lineOfSightToPlayer`/`noLineOfSightToPlayer` (`hasLineOfSightToPlayer` -- a straight ray from the AI's position to the Player, blocked by any SOLID box collider crossing it first, reusing the exact same `Opus.Physics.rayAABB` primitive `fireHitscan` already uses against solids -- boolean only, no vision cones/perception simulation). Plus `healthAtMost` (self health, reusing the existing per-object Health system) and `timeInStateAtLeast` (built-in hysteresis/cooldown primitive -- how long the AI has been in its CURRENT state, tracked via `item._aiStateEnteredAt`).
  - **`enterAiState`**: sets the state's own `animRole` once on entry (explicit per-state animation, not the Simple-AI locomotion driver's inferred-from-movement-delta Idle/Walk/Run -- a State Machine character skips that inference entirely) and runs any `entryActions` (reusing the SAME curated `ANIMATION_EVENT_ACTIONS` set Priority B's Animation Events editor established, for authoring consistency).
  - **Editor**: a compact list/card editor (`renderAiStateList`, per the spec's own explicit preference over a node canvas for a first version) -- an "AI Mode: State Machine" checkbox reveals a state list, each card editing Name/Movement Type+Speed+Radius+Waypoints/Anim Role/optional Attack sub-fields/an ordered Transitions list (condition type + value + destination state)/an ordered Entry Actions list, plus a "Start" button to designate the entry state.
  - **Serialization**: `mode`/`states`/`startState` added to the `ai` field whitelist in `serializeCubeRecord` -- proactively, in the SAME change that introduces them, following this project's own "finding #48" discipline (a comment already sitting right above this exact whitelist, documenting an earlier bug where `targetId`/`waypointNames` were silently dropped by omission) rather than discovering the gap later. Runtime-only fields (`_currentState`, per-state movement caches) are deliberately NOT persisted -- a fresh Play/Load always starts every AI at `startState` again. `instantiateCubeRecord`'s revive path already spreads `{...record.ai}` wholesale, so no separate whitelist was needed there.
  - **Dogfooded live end-to-end**, not assumed correct from the transition logic alone, using a real guard built on `games/iron-arena.json`'s own Sentry fixture with a genuine 5-state machine (Patrol -> Alert -> Chase -> Attack -> Flee, matching the spec's own Section 32 worked example exactly): **(1)** player far away -> state stayed `patrol`; **(2)** player came within 15 units -> transitioned to `alert`, then to `chase` after the authored 0.3s `timeInStateAtLeast` hold; **(3)** with the player held STATIONARY, Chase's real `follow` movement (the same code Simple-AI Follow uses, genuinely closing distance frame-by-frame, not teleported) drove the guard into its own authored Attack range (`dist: 3.90 <= 4`), correctly transitioning to `attack`; **(4)** dropping the guard's health to 15 (below the authored `healthAtMost: 20` threshold) transitioned it to `flee`, and 30 further real ticks of Flee's own `flee` movement genuinely increased separation (`4.03 -> 8.03`), not just a state-label change with no actual behavior. **Line of Sight** verified separately: placed a real solid wall directly between guard and player -- `hasLineOfSightToPlayer` correctly returned `false`; removed the wall -- correctly returned `true`. **Serialization round-trip** verified through the real `serializeCubeRecord`/revive-spread path: a 2-state machine with an entry action survived intact (state names, mode, and the entry action's own params all matched exactly). **UI** verified by selecting a real state-machine-configured item and calling the real `renderInspector()` -- the state list rendered real card HTML (5937 characters for 2 states) with the mode checkbox correctly reflecting `entity.ai.mode`, with zero console errors.
  - **Scoping note**: the state-card UI exposes Movement Type/Speed/Radius/Waypoints -- the fields needed for the required guard dogfood (Wander/Follow/Flee/Patrol Route) -- but not every Simple-AI type's more specialized fields (Orbit's implicit center, Navigate To Target's own target picker); those types remain fully FUNCTIONAL in a state's `movement` config (the runtime is generic), just not yet exposed as dedicated UI controls in this first version. A genuine, bounded scoping choice, not an oversight -- extend the card UI if a future stress game's dogfood actually needs one of those types mid-state-machine.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (no engine primitive touched -- the State Machine is entirely an editor-level feature built on existing movement/attack/health/collision primitives).

- **World-State Save Game 2.0 (Priority E, Sections 33-41)** -- persists ONLY deliberately-marked runtime state, never a blind snapshot of the whole live scene. New per-object author setting `item.persistRuntimeState` (OFF by default -- every existing object, old or new, that never sets it is completely unaffected) opts an object into having `{destroyed, visible, position, rotation, health?}` tracked, keyed by its own stable id, per level: `worldState[levelId][objectId]`.
  - **`markPersistentDestruction(item)`**, called from the ONE place any destruction funnels through (`removeItemFromScene`, gated on `playMode` so an author deleting something in the EDITOR itself is never mistaken for a runtime destruction), records a persistent object's destroyed id into `window.__destroyedPersistentIds[levelId]` at the moment it actually happens -- not inferred retroactively by diffing against a prior snapshot, which would miss an object destroyed before its first-ever Save.
  - **`collectWorldStateForLevel(levelId)`**/**`captureCurrentLevelWorldState()`**: the active level's own live persistent objects are read straight off `placed`; every OTHER level's own slice is covered entirely by its own destroyed-id set plus whatever `window.__worldState` already accumulated the last time THAT level was itself active -- `placed` only ever holds the currently-loaded level's objects, so there is nothing else to read for a level that isn't loaded right now.
  - **`applyWorldStateToCurrentLevel()`**: restore order matches the spec's own Section 37 exactly -- called only AFTER `switchToLevelDuringPlay` has already rebuilt the authored scene (never before the objects it targets exist), removes anything recorded `destroyed:true` (via the same shared `removeItemFromScene`), and otherwise overwrites visible/position/rotation/health on the live, freshly-rebuilt object matching that id. A record whose object doesn't resolve is always a silent no-op.
  - **Save format versioning** (Section 38-ish): `saveFormatVersion: 2` added to the Save Game payload -- a save written by a pre-Wave-7 game (no `worldState` key at all) still loads correctly, since `applyWorldStateToCurrentLevel` is a no-op with nothing to apply and every other field reads exactly as before. `saveGame`/`loadGame` also now carry `inventory` and `objectives` (Priority F, below) through the same payload, alongside the pre-existing `gameState`/`flags`/`checkpoint`/`playerPosition`.
  - **Spawner persistence**: deliberately NOT built this wave -- the spec explicitly allows deferring it as bounded/lower-priority than object persistence, and no dogfood surfaced a concrete need for it yet (every stress scenario built so far persists ordinary placed objects and doors, not Spawner completion state). Revisit if a future stress game's dogfood actually needs it.
  - **Dogfooded live end-to-end across a genuinely fresh browser session** (a real full page navigate between the Save and the Load, a completely new JS realm with all in-memory editor state gone -- only `localStorage`, the real underlying store `Opus.SaveSystem` writes to, carries anything across), covering BOTH required scenarios: marked a real crate object and a real door object `persistRuntimeState`, entered real Play, genuinely destroyed the crate via `removeItemFromScene` (the same call any real Destroy Object behavior makes) and moved the door to an "opened" position, then `saveGame`. After the full page reload: loaded the SAME project file fresh, confirmed the crate was present and the door was back at its ORIGINAL authored position (0, 1.5, 15) -- proving the authored scene genuinely rebuilds first, not skipped -- THEN called `loadGame` and confirmed the crate was gone (`!findItemById(...)`, matching persisted `destroyed:true`) and the door was back at its saved opened position (0, 4.5, 15), not its authored one. Both the destroyed-object and persistent-door-transform dogfoods the spec requires passed with real cross-session evidence, not an in-memory simulation.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (no engine primitive touched -- built entirely on the existing `Opus.SaveSystem`, `removeItemFromScene`, and per-object Health).

- **Objectives/Quest-lite System (Priority F, Sections 42-46)** -- a small structured Objective system on top of the pre-existing Objective Text/flags/counters/inventory, deliberately NOT an RPG quest database. `currentObjectiveDefs = [{id, title, description, nextObjective}]` is per-level authored data (same global-mirror-synced-into-`levels[currentLevelIndex]` pattern `currentSequences` already established, including matching `applyLevelObjectiveDefs`/serialize/normalize/Duplicate-Level wiring); runtime state is `window.__gameObjectives[id] = 'inactive'|'active'|'complete'|'failed'`, the same plain-named-state shape Inventory/flags/counters already use.
  - **Three new actions** `activateObjective`/`completeObjective`/`failObjective`, and **two new conditions** `objectiveIsComplete`/`objectiveIsActive` (`evaluateConditionNode`), all following patterns this project already established elsewhere (`giveItem`/`removeItem`/`hasItem`). `completeObjective` honors a SIMPLE `nextObjective` chain (auto-activates the next id) -- deliberately bounded, no branching logic, matching the spec's own explicit limit on how far chaining should go.
  - **HUD auto-display**: an Objective Text HUD element with NO authored text now auto-shows the current active objective's own title (`currentActiveObjectiveTitle()`, the first def whose state is `'active'` in authored order) -- leaving the field empty IS the binding, matching the spec's own "auto-displays... when bound appropriately." `resolveHudBindText` also resolves an explicit `{activeObjective}` token anywhere in custom HUD text, for an author who wants it embedded in a longer string.
  - **Persistence**: `saveGame`/`loadGame` now also carry `inventory` and `objectives` through the same Save Game payload Priority E's World State already extended (`saveFormatVersion: 2`) -- Objectives ride the exact same versioned format, not a second save mechanism.
  - **Editor**: a compact Objectives list editor (`renderObjectiveDefPanel`) added to the Level tab right below Sequences -- Title/Description/an optional Next-Objective dropdown per row, the same list-row pattern every other Wave 7 editor list already established. A new `objectiveRef` field type was added to the shared `renderActionFieldInput` (reused by the three new actions' own param forms).
  - **Dogfooded live**: authored two real objectives ("Find the weapon" -> auto-chains to -> "Defeat the guard") plus a real Objective Text HUD element with empty text, entered real Play, called `activateObjective('obj_find')` (HUD correctly read "Find the weapon"), then `completeObjective('obj_find')` (chain correctly auto-activated `obj_defeat`, HUD correctly flipped to "Defeat the guard" with no further action needed). Confirmed both new conditions (`objectiveIsComplete`/`objectiveIsActive`) evaluate correctly against that exact state. Confirmed persistence end-to-end through the real `saveGame`/`loadGame` actions: cleared `window.__gameObjectives` (simulating a fresh load with nothing in memory) and confirmed `loadGame` correctly restored `{obj_find: 'complete', obj_defeat: 'active'}` from the saved slot.
  - `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31.

- **Rapid World Building -- Scatter Placement (Priority G, Sections 47-54) and Array/Duplicate Pattern Tool (Priority H, Sections 55-56)** -- authoring-speed tools whose OUTPUT is always ordinary placed objects, never a second scene representation (movable/deletable/editable/prefab-able/groupable for free, exactly the spec's own explicit requirement for both).
  - **`performScatter(config)`**: source is a Prefab (priority) or a plain Asset; region is Circle (uniform-AREA-density sampling via `sqrt(rng()) * radius`, not center-biased) or Box; deterministic seeded placement via a small `mulberry32` PRNG (same seed -> byte-identical output, verified); naive bounded rejection-sampling for min-spacing (not a true Poisson-disc solver, an accepted bounded simplification); optional terrain-conform (samples the first Terrain's real `sampleHeight`) with an optional Max Slope filter (`sampleNormal`'s own `.y` converted to a real degree angle via `acos`, skipping any candidate point steeper than authored) -- reusing the exact same terrain query primitives the sculpt brushes already established, not a second height/slope computation. Wrapped in exactly ONE `pushUndo()` for the whole operation. **A real multi-member-vs-single-object prefab-shape bug was caught and fixed BEFORE this landed** (not assumed correct): `scatterPlaceOne` initially assumed every prefab has `.records`/`rootId` (Priority K's multi-member shape) and crashed instantly on a real single-object prefab (`prefab.record` only, no `.records` at all) -- fixed by branching on `prefab.records && prefab.records.length > 0` the exact same way `swapItemForPrefabInstance` already does, and separately caught that the multi-member branch never called `addPlaced()` on its own new items at all (would have silently placed nothing into the live scene) -- fixed in the same pass.
  - **`performArrayDuplicate(sourceItem, config)`**: Linear (count/spacing along X or Z) or Grid (countX/countZ/spacingX/spacingZ), reusing a new `duplicateOneItemAt` primitive built on the exact same `serializeCubeRecord`/`instantiateCubeRecord` round-trip Save/Load already trusts to faithfully carry every field, rather than hand-copying fields the way the existing `duplicateSelected` does inline -- so Array output can never silently diverge from what a real save/load already proves correct. Circular mode deliberately NOT built: Linear/Grid alone already covered both required dogfood cases (a fence line, a grid of columns) with zero friction, and a third mode with no concrete need yet would be complexity ahead of an actual use case.
