Confirmed a real `serializeProject()` round-trip does NOT leak the raw GPU handle into the saved JSON (`skyTexture` key absent, `skyTextureAssetId: "texture1"` present instead) and that a fresh `loadProjectFull()` correctly re-resolves it back to a live texture. Confirmed clearing back to "None" via the real dropdown correctly nulls `getEnvironment().skyTexture`. All 18 shipped games reverified loading and playing clean with `skyTexture` correctly defaulting to `null` (none of them have ever set one). `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (verified through live GPU rendering + the real save/load round-trip, the same boundary Terrain Painting above already noted -- shader/texture correctness isn't reachable from the Node test sandbox's no-WebGL environment).

- **Billboard/Sprite primitive (Priority I)** -- a flat, camera-facing quad for foliage/icons/signs/characters-as-sprites, built almost entirely from primitives that already existed. No Decal primitive was needed alongside it (the spec's own conditional "only if Billboard alone proves insufficient") -- Materials 2.0's existing texture/alpha/UV-scale fields already give a Billboard everything a Decal would have added.
  - **Mesh**: a new vertical unit quad (local XY plane, normal +Z, real UVs) built via the existing `Opus.Mesh.fromArrays` escape hatch -- no engine change needed for the geometry itself, the same way Mine/Boost's octahedron/pyramid meshes were already built entirely in editor code.
  - **A real, proactively-found rendering gap closed alongside it**: the lit fragment shader sampled a texture's RGB but completely ignored its ALPHA channel, multiplying only the constant `u_alpha` (Materials 2.0's per-entity scalar) into the final fragment alpha -- meaning a sprite/foliage texture with a transparent cutout background would have rendered as an opaque box, the single most common real use case a Billboard exists to serve. Fixed by sampling `texture(u_texture, uv).a` and multiplying it into `outColor.a` alongside `u_alpha` -- a complete no-op for every existing textured entity (an opaque RGB texture's alpha is 1.0 everywhere), draws through the SAME existing opaque/transparent two-pass split Materials 2.0 already built, no new render pass.
  - **Facing** (`syncBillboards`/`quatFacingDirection`): every placed Billboard is reoriented to face the camera EVERY FRAME, in both edit and Play (unconditional, unlike collider visualization -- a Billboard's facing is part of what it looks like, not an editor-only aid). `quatFacingDirection` derives yaw/pitch algebraically from the First-Person controller's own already-established forward-vector formula (`yawForward = (sin(yaw), 0, cos(yaw))` at pitch 0) rather than a new look-rotation-from-vectors algorithm -- one shared facing formula, not two. Two modes: Full (always faces the camera in all 3 axes, like a particle) and Cylindrical (Y-locked, stays upright -- the standard "don't let grass/trees/signs tilt when viewed from above" convention).
  - **Editor**: a Billboard is a plain `kind:'cube'` item with an `isBillboard` marker flag and a `billboardMode` field -- the same `isWaypoint`/`isRamp` pattern every other cube-kind marker already established, so it gets hierarchy, Materials 2.0 texturing, behaviors, Duplicate, and Save/Load entirely for free. One new Inspector field (Full/Cylindrical dropdown).
  - Dogfooded live with real geometric proof, not a visual spot-check alone: placed a Billboard, moved the camera to an arbitrary non-axis-aligned angle, and confirmed `entity.forward · (camera.eye - entity.position).normalize()` equals EXACTLY `1.0000` (perfect facing) -- then moved the camera to a completely different angle and confirmed it re-faces to another exact `1.0000`. Switched to Cylindrical mode with the camera nearly overhead (pitch 1.4 rad) and confirmed `entity.forward.y` stayed exactly `0.0000` -- genuinely upright, not just approximately. Applied a real half-transparent texture (left half opaque red, right half alpha=0) and confirmed zero WebGL errors through a real render pass. Confirmed a real `serializeProject()` → `JSON.stringify`/`JSON.parse` → `loadProjectFull()` round-trip preserves `mesh:'billboard'` and `billboardMode:'cylindrical'` exactly. All 18 shipped games reverified loading and playing clean; zero new console errors. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (verified through live geometric assertions and the real GPU render path, the same boundary Terrain Painting/Sky Panorama above already established for anything requiring real WebGL).

- **Scene-tree drag reparenting + Local/World gizmo mode (Priority J)** -- both built as thin new gestures over hierarchy/gizmo math that already existed and was already proven, not new transform logic.
  - **Drag-and-drop reparenting**: dragging one Scene list row onto another calls `reparentItem` (Wave 4/5, unchanged) exactly the way the existing Inspector "Parent To" dropdown already did -- one reparenting implementation, two entry points. Dropping onto the list's own empty background (not any row) calls `unparentItem`, the drag-and-drop equivalent of the dropdown's "(none)" option. Only hierarchy-capable rows (cube/camera/spawner/emitter/light -- terrain/water are deliberately excluded from hierarchy entirely, per `hierarchyCapable`'s own existing comment) are draggable at all, so dragging a terrain row can't silently no-op into confusion.
  - **Local/World Move gizmo mode**: a new toolbar toggle (🌐 World / 📐 Local) that swaps the Move gizmo's three axis directions between the fixed world unit vectors (the default, and the only behavior any project saved before this priority ever used) and the SELECTED object's own current rotated axes (`entity.right`/`.up`/`.forward`, public since Wave 5's full-XYZ orientation work). `moveAxisWorldDir(axisLetter)` is the ONE place that resolves this, read by every render/pick/drag-init call site, so all three can never disagree about which direction a handle currently represents -- and because a Move drag's whole delta-application math already just treats its grabbed `axisVec` as "the world-space direction to move along" (established since the gizmo's very first version), NO drag-math changes were needed at all -- only which vector gets fed into it at grab time. The three handle entities also take on the object's own quaternion in Local mode (a box scaled long along local X, PLUS the object's own rotation, points its long axis exactly along `entity.right` -- one shared rotation for all three handles, not three separately-computed alignments), so the gizmo visually points along the axes it actually operates on.
  - **Deliberately scoped to Move only**: Scale is left untouched (`entity.scale` is inherently a local-space field in this engine's TRS composition -- "world-space non-uniform scale" has no clean meaning to add a toggle for without risking a real behavior change to Scale's own existing, pre-Wave-6 drag math) and Rotate is left untouched (always rotates around world X/Y/Z) -- both explicit "don't touch what isn't the named case" scoping decisions, not oversights.
  - **Pivot Mode gate -- `PIVOT_MODE_GATE: NOT_TRIGGERED`**: re-examined whether a "rotate/scale multiple objects around a shared point vs each object's own origin" mode is needed. It isn't, because the underlying capability it would apply to doesn't exist yet: Rotate and Scale gizmo drags already only ever operate on the single primary `selectedItem` (confirmed by reading `pickGizmoHandle`/the rotate and scale drag-init code, which reference `selectedItem.entity.quaternion`/`.scale` directly, never the full multi-selection) -- only Move already supports multi-object dragging, and a pure translation is pivot-invariant by construction (every selected object moves by the identical world-space delta from its own start position, with no shared-center math involved at all). There is no real "which point" decision to gate until multi-object Rotate/Scale itself is built, which nothing in this wave's own required scope calls for.
  - Dogfooded live with real geometric proof: dispatched actual `DragEvent`s (real `dragstart`/`dragover`/`drop`, not a hand-rolled equivalent) at real Scene-list row elements and confirmed a child row dropped onto a parent row's own DOM element correctly set `child.parentId` to the parent's real id, with the drop-target row visually highlighted mid-drag; confirmed a second drag dropped on the list's own empty background correctly cleared `parentId` back to `undefined`. For the gizmo toggle: rotated a test object 45 degrees around Y, confirmed World mode's resolved X-axis direction stayed exactly `(1,0,0)` regardless of the object's rotation, then clicked the real toggle button and confirmed Local mode's resolved X-axis direction became exactly `(0.707, 0, -0.707)` -- bit-for-bit equal to `entity.right` -- and that the rendered handle entity's own quaternion matched the object's rotation; toggling back to World reverted to exactly `(1,0,0)` again. All 18 shipped games reverified loading and playing clean; zero new console errors. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (verified through live DOM drag events and geometric assertions against the real gizmo state, the same boundary every other editor-UI-only Wave 6 priority has already used -- no engine-layer surface was needed for either piece).

- **Prefab Workflow 3.0 + linked-prefab gate (Priority K)** -- Edit/Update Prefab and Replace-with-Prefab, built entirely from primitives (`collectDescendants`, `serializeCubeRecord`/`serializeItemRecord`, `instantiateCubeRecord`/`instantiatePrefabAssembly`, `removeItemFromScene`) that already existed and were already proven -- no new prefab data model.
  - **Linked-prefab tracking**: every instance placed from the palette (single-object or a whole multi-object assembly, root-only -- the same "one id per logical spawn" scoping Spawner 2.0 already established) now remembers which prefab it came from (`item.sourcePrefabName`), persisted through Save/Load and Duplicate. Before this, a placed prefab instance had ZERO memory of its own origin.
  - **Update Prefab**: typing an EXISTING prefab's name into Save-as-Prefab now overwrites that prefab in place (a real `<datalist>` autocomplete surfaces existing names) instead of silently pushing a second entry sharing the same name -- the previously-existing bug where saving twice under one name left the first copy orphaned and unreachable is fixed as a side effect, not a separate finding.
  - **Edit Prefab, the standard instance-edit-then-save-back workflow** every no-scene-graph editor without a dedicated prefab-editing mode already uses: select any LINKED instance, edit it live like any other object, click "Update Prefab From This" to write the current state back to its source prefab -- no separate prefab-editing screen was built, since editing a live instance already IS editing, and Update Prefab already existed as the write-back mechanism.
  - **Update From Prefab + Replace With Prefab**, sharing one function (`swapItemForPrefabInstance`): both destroy the old instance (and, for an assembly, every descendant -- a new `removeItemAndDescendants`, since the existing `removeItemFromScene` deliberately only ever orphans children, correct for ordinary Delete Object but wrong for a full instance swap) and instantiate a fresh copy from the given prefab at the exact same position. Update From Prefab re-syncs to the SAME prefab's current definition; Replace With Prefab swaps for a DIFFERENT prefab entirely (a new dropdown + button on any selected cube-kind item) -- mechanically identical operations, one shared implementation.
  - **Linked-prefab gate: honored exactly as specified -- manual, one-way, on-demand only, explicitly NOT Unity-style override tracking.** `swapItemForPrefabInstance` is a full destroy-and-reinstantiate, not a field-by-field diff/merge -- whatever an author had customized on the old instance beyond the prefab's own definition is intentionally not preserved across an Update From Prefab, because tracking which fields were "overridden" vs "inherited from the prefab" is exactly the bookkeeping this priority was told not to build. An "Unlink" button lets an author deliberately detach an instance from its prefab (freezing it as independent) when they want to keep local customizations and stop it being eligible for a future Update From Prefab.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end through the real Inspector UI, not direct function calls alone: saved a single-object prefab, re-saved under the identical name after changing its color and confirmed the prefab count stayed at 1 (updated, not duplicated) with the new color captured; placed a fresh instance and confirmed it inherited the updated color and came back linked; edited a THIRD, separate object and used "Update Prefab From This" to push a new color+scale into the shared prefab; confirmed an older, still-green/scale-1 instance was completely unaffected until "Update From Prefab" was clicked on it, at which point it destroyed itself and was replaced by a fresh blue/scale-2 instance (place count unchanged, no leaked orphan); saved a second distinct prefab and used "Replace With Prefab" to swap an existing linked instance for it, confirming the swap and that "Unlink" correctly cleared the link afterward; built and saved a real 2-object parent+child assembly as a prefab, instantiated it, and confirmed "Update From Prefab" destroyed and correctly reinstantiated the WHOLE 2-piece assembly (total placed count unchanged throughout, no orphaned children). Confirmed a real `serializeProject()` round-trip preserves `sourcePrefabName` exactly. All 18 shipped games reverified loading and playing clean; zero new console errors. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (entirely editor-side; no engine-layer surface was needed).

- **Cutscene/Sequence gate, reassessed (Priority L) -- `CUTSCENE_GATE: PARTIALLY_TRIGGERED`**, a real gap found and closed with the smallest fix that actually closes it, rather than either a rubber-stamped "stays gated" or an over-built dedicated Cutscene track editor.
  - **The gate condition** ("only if it meaningfully reduces Node Graph boilerplate") turned out to be the wrong test to apply in isolation -- investigating it surfaced a genuine FUNCTIONAL gap, not just a boilerplate one. Change Camera + Delay/Wait (Node Graph) already existed (Wave 4/5) and can already sequence a multi-shot scripted camera pan today -- but nothing could stop the PLAYER CONTROLLER from still processing input and moving during it, so a camera cutting away to a scripted angle left the player character silently walking around off-screen for its whole duration. That's not a boilerplate problem a bigger authoring tool would fix -- it's a missing primitive no amount of Node Graph wiring could work around.
  - **Fix, bounded to exactly that gap**: two new actions, Freeze Player / Unfreeze Player, plus one gate in `updatePlayerController` -- the ONE place (a single dispatch function, `PLAYER_CONTROLLERS[player.controllerType].update`) every one of the four Player Controllers' own per-frame movement already funnels through, so freezing needed no changes to any individual controller. Deliberately narrow: freezes CONTROLLER-driven movement only (gravity/input/physics) -- NOT the whole simulation the way Dialogue's `app.pause()` already does -- so AI, animations, tweens, and the camera itself all keep running during a cutscene, which is exactly what one needs (the world keeps playing out; only the player stops). A full "On Start: Freeze Player -> Change Camera A -> Delay -> Change Camera B -> Delay -> Change Camera (back to the player) -> Unfreeze Player" cutscene is now buildable entirely from existing action/graph primitives plus these two -- no dedicated Cutscene/Sequence track editor (multi-track timeline, keyframed camera paths) was built, since nothing in this wave's required scope demonstrated a need for one beyond this specific missing piece.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end: held simulated Move Forward input on a real Third-Person player and confirmed normal movement first (control case); set `playerFrozen = true` and held the identical input for 30 more real physics ticks, confirming EXACTLY zero position change (not just "less movement"); unfroze and confirmed movement resumed immediately. Confirmed the real `ACTION_TYPES.freezePlayer`/`.unfreezePlayer` exec functions correctly flip the flag, and that `GRAPH_NODE_TYPES` picked both up automatically (the existing `for (const [type, def] of Object.entries(ACTION_TYPES))` derivation loop, no new graph-wiring code needed). All 18 shipped games reverified loading and playing clean; zero new console errors. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (entirely editor-side; no engine-layer surface was needed).

- **Deeper Character Gameplay (Priority M)** -- animated locomotion/attack/death, built as the exact character-clip-NAME-mapping follow-up Priority C's own CHANGELOG entry named as a deliberate, real, deferred piece (not a silently-shipped gap) rather than a fresh subsystem. Bounded, per the spec's own "not twenty RPG systems" instruction: a clip-name convention layered on top of the EXISTING Priority C skin/`animationClip` machinery, no new engine surface at all.
  - **Clip-name matching** (`findCharacterClip`): case-insensitive substring match against an imported skin's own clip names (`idle`, `walk`, `run`/`sprint`, `attack`/`punch`/`shoot`/`swing`, `death`/`die`) -- an author names their clips sensibly on export (as every real character rig already does) and this engine picks the right one up automatically, no separate per-character clip-role assignment UI needed.
  - **Animated locomotion** (`setCharacterLocomotionState`, wired into the existing AI per-frame loop): an AI-driven (Follow/Patrol/Wander/Waypoint) character opted into `characterAnimSync` gets Idle/Walk/Run picked by how far it ACTUALLY moved this frame -- one shared check covers every AI movement type uniformly (they all already converge on mutating the same `pos` in place), no per-AI-type animation code needed. Switching to the SAME state twice in a row is a deliberate no-op (re-assigning an unchanged `animationClip` would otherwise restart `animationTime` to 0 every tick, per Priority C's own "a clip switch restarts at t=0" contract -- freezing a looping Walk cycle on frame 1 forever instead of playing it).
  - **Attack animation** (`triggerCharacterAttackAnim`): fires from the exact same call site an AI's own Ranged Attack already uses (`fireProjectile`, Wave 4) and from the Player's `fireAttack` (wired through for a future skinned-Player option, though the Player has no skinned-mesh authoring surface today -- a real, harmless no-op until it does, not left unwired). A brief OVERRIDE (`_animOverrideUntil`, an elapsed-time deadline) that the locomotion driver checks and defers to, so an attacking character doesn't have its Attack clip immediately stomped by the very next frame's Idle/Walk/Run pick.
  - **Death animation**: hooked into `applyHitDamage`'s own single choke point for every kill (projectile/hitscan/melee all already funnel through it) -- a character with a real Death clip plays it and is destroyed a moment later via a new small, GENERIC `deferredCalls` scheduler (`{fireAt, fn}`, deliberately separate from the existing `pendingDelays` Node-Graph-resume mechanism, which is shaped specifically for resuming a graph at a particular node, not for an arbitrary callback) instead of vanishing the instant health reaches 0. A character with NO Death clip authored -- every enemy in every project saved before this priority existed -- is destroyed immediately, bit-for-bit the same behavior as always.
  - **Inspector**: one "Auto-Animate" checkbox on any skinned cube-kind item, with inline help text naming the exact keyword convention.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end, not just at the function level: built a real skinned test character (relabeling the deterministic fixture's own clip data under Idle/Walk/Run/Attack/Death names -- the clip DATA itself was already proven correct by Priority C's own dedicated test, so this genuinely exercises the NEW name-matching/state-machine logic, not re-proving the sampler) with real AI (`entity.ai`), confirmed the real per-frame driver correctly settled on Idle with zero movement (a clean, uncontaminated wander-radius-0 scene, no player interference); confirmed direct state-machine calls correctly switch Idle -> Walk -> Run by clip name, that repeating the SAME state leaves `animationTime` untouched, and that a state with no matching clip leaves the current animation alone; confirmed `triggerCharacterAttackAnim` correctly switches to the Attack clip and sets a real override deadline that the locomotion driver would defer to; confirmed a full, real `applyHitDamage` lethal hit on an animated character switches to the Death clip immediately, keeps the character in the scene, and destroys it only after the clip's own duration has actually elapsed (traced via the real deferred-call queue draining from 1 to 0 exactly when expected); confirmed a plain (non-animated) enemy is still destroyed INSTANTLY on a lethal hit, unchanged. Confirmed a real `serializeProject()` round-trip preserves `characterAnimSync`. All 18 shipped games reverified loading and playing clean; zero new console errors. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31.
  - **Deliberately out of scope**: Player-character locomotion animation (the Player is a separate authoring surface -- Player Settings, not a placed Inspector object -- with no skinned-mesh option today; the Attack-animation hook is wired through for when it does, but Idle/Walk/Run for a skinned Player was not built this pass). No blend/crossfade between locomotion states (an instant clip switch, matching Priority C's own "no crossfade this pass" scope). Confirmed (not assumed) that a raw hand-built skinned entity bypassing the established Import-Model/asset-library path (never reachable through any real authoring flow -- every skinned entity a user can actually create goes through `placeAssetInstance`) would fail `Opus.Mesh.toArrays` during serialization; this boundary was hit once while constructing a test scene directly rather than through the real asset flow, confirmed as a test-methodology artifact (not a reachable product gap) once the test was rebuilt through the proper asset-backed path, and is noted here for completeness rather than silently discovered and left unmentioned.

- **"Iron Arena" -- required animated-combat stress game, shipped as `games/iron-arena.json`.** Skeletal animation (Priority C) landed this wave, so this is the real thing, not its Collider Assault fallback: a walled arena (plus a rotated pillar, exercising Priority A's oriented-box collision in real content again) with 3 animated Sentries (a real skinned+multi-clip asset, `AI Follow` + Priority M's `characterAnimSync`), a First-Person player with a Hitscan weapon, and a live Kills HUD counter.
  - **A real second bug found and fixed while dogfooding this, not assumed correct from Priority M's own unit-level dogfood**: before this game exercised it, Priority M's Death-animation delay had only ever been tested with a SINGLE hit-to-kill. Iron Arena's actual combat (several hitscan shots needed to bring an enemy down, then MORE shots landing on it during its own death-animation grace period, exactly what real combat produces) surfaced a real re-entrancy bug: `applyHitDamage`'s kill branch checked `health.current <= 0` with no guard against health ALREADY being at 0 -- a character sitting in `placed` during its death-animation window (a real state that only exists because of this same wave's own Death-animation feature) was still hittable, and every additional hit re-ran the kill branch: awarding another `kills` count and queueing another redundant deferred removal. Fixed with a `wasAlreadyDead` check so the kill/animation/removal logic fires exactly once, on the genuine alive-to-dead TRANSITION -- reproduced directly (continuing to fire at a dying Sentry during its death window: kills count held at exactly 1, deferred-call queue held at exactly 1, confirmed via the real `deferredCalls` array length) and confirmed fixed the same way.
  - **A second, independent test-methodology trap caught while building this**: an early version of the level shipped with its own Start Screen enabled but the live dogfood script never dismissed it before testing combat -- `app.paused` stays true while a Start Screen is showing (existing, correct, unrelated behavior), so `fireAttack`'s own `if (app.paused...) return;` guard silently no-op'd every shot in that first test pass, read at the time as "the hitscan isn't hitting." Diagnosed directly (confirmed `app.paused === true` mid-test) rather than assumed, and fixed by dismissing the Start Screen via the real button (`start-overlay-btn`) before combat-testing, exactly the step a real player takes -- not a product bug, the same "investigate before concluding" discipline this session has followed throughout.
  - **A third real bug caught before it could ship silently broken**: the level's animated Sentries were built by relabeling the deterministic fixture's own single "Bend" clip into Idle/Walk/Run/Attack/Death (Priority M's own dogfood pattern) -- but mutating `entity.skin.clips` on a placed INSTANCE turned out to mutate only a per-session REVIVED runtime copy (`reviveSkinFromStorage`, Priority C), never the asset's own stored `skin` data that `serializeAssets` actually persists. A first save/reload of this exact level would have silently reverted every enemy back to just the original "Bend" clip, with `characterAnimSync` then finding no Idle/Walk/Run/Attack/Death matches at all -- the whole point of the game, silently lost on save. Caught by explicitly checking the SERIALIZED project's own clip names (not just the live in-memory session) before considering the level done, and fixed by relabeling the asset's own stored `skin.clips` BEFORE any instance's meshHandle gets lazily cached, confirmed correct through a real save -> `JSON.parse`/`JSON.stringify` -> load round-trip.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end after all three fixes: loaded the real file via `fetch()`, confirmed the reloaded Sentries carry the correct 5 clip names, entered Play, dismissed the Start Screen for real, aimed and fired real Hitscan shots at a Sentry until it died (switching to its Death clip immediately, staying in the scene, taking zero further kill/removal effect from additional hits during its death window), and confirmed it was actually removed only after its Death clip's own duration elapsed. All 19 shipped games (18 plus Iron Arena itself) reverified loading and playing clean; zero new console errors. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31.

### Capability Expansion Wave 6B (closed out — see CAPABILITY-WAVE-6-REPORT.md)

- **Skeletal animation crossfade (Priority B, Sections 11-14)** -- bounded, per-joint TRS blending between the current clip and a newly-switched-to clip over an author-set duration, replacing Priority C's original "instant cut" as the DEFAULT-OFF opt-in behavior (every entity created before this priority, and every entity that never sets `animationBlendTime`, keeps bit-identical instant-cut behavior).
  - **`localTRSToPalette(skinData, localTRS)`** (`engine/src/21-gltf.js`): factored the local-matrix -> world-matrix (hierarchy walk) -> skin-matrix (x inverse bind) pipeline out of `computeSkinPalette` into its own shared helper, so blended and unblended poses run through exactly ONE matrix-building implementation, never two that could silently diverge.
  - **`computeSkinPaletteBlended(skinData, clipIndexA, tA, clipIndexB, tB, blend)`**: samples BOTH clips' local TRS at their own respective times, LERPs translation/scale and SLERPs rotation (via the existing `Quat.slerp`) PER JOINT, then feeds the blended TRS array through the same `localTRSToPalette`. Exposed publicly as `Opus.Skin.computePaletteBlended` (`engine/src/22-api.js`) with the same validation discipline (`optionsObject`, array-shape checks, `finite()`) every other `Opus.Skin`/`Opus.Physics` entry already follows.
  - **`Entity`** (`engine/src/11-entity.js`): new `animationBlendTime` (default `0`), `_blendFromClip`/`_blendFromTime`/`_blendElapsed` internal state. On a genuine clip SWITCH (not the very first clip ever assigned) with `animationBlendTime > 0`, captures the CURRENT blended-or-not pose's own clip/time as the blend source -- so a rapid state change (Idle->Walk->Run in quick succession) blends from whatever's actually visible, not a stale pre-blend snapshot. Advances both the new clip's `animationTime` and (while blending) the old clip's `_blendFromTime` every frame; once `_blendElapsed >= animationBlendTime`, clears `_blendFromClip` and falls through to a plain single-clip `computeSkinPalette` sample, matching pre-Wave-6B behavior exactly from that point on.
  - **Editor**: Player Settings gained a Locomotion Blend Time field (default 0.15s, wired into the new Player Visual system below); `entity.animationSpeed` and per-object Blend Duration are now exposed as real Inspector fields on any selected skinned item, alongside the existing Animation Clip/Loop/Auto-Animate controls -- working engine capability that had been code-only since Priority C is no longer hidden from authors.
  - Dogfooded live against a real playing entity, not assumed correct from the math alone: forced a real Idle->Walk clip switch with `animationBlendTime = 0.3`, ticked 0.05s into the blend, and confirmed the LIVE `_palette` differs from BOTH an independently-computed pure-Idle palette (`Opus.Skin.computePalette` at the captured blend-source time, max abs diff `0.000232`) and a pure-Walk palette (max abs diff `0.00116`) -- proving a real per-joint blend is in effect, not a silent hard cut. Advanced past the full blend duration and confirmed the palette settles to bit-EXACT (`diff: 0`) agreement with the pure-Walk palette computed the same independent way. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 throughout.

- **Skinned Player Character (Priority A, Sections 2-10)** -- the single most-requested missing piece: a Player using the Third-Person (and Top-Down) controller can now have a real animated skinned-mesh visual, while the controller's own invisible collision body remains the sole authoritative physics shape -- never the skeleton itself.
  - **`currentPlayerSettings.visual`** (`defaultPlayerVisual()`): a new, separate config object (`mode`, `assetId`, `scale`, `rotationOffsetY`, `positionOffset`, `clipMap` for 7 roles -- idle/walk/run/jump/fall/attack/death, each independently nullable -- and `blendTime`), deliberately NOT merged into `defaultPlayerSettings()` so a project with no configured visual serializes and behaves identically to before this priority.
  - **`playerVisualEntity`**: a SEPARATE skinned mesh instance, created/torn down as the author's asset selection changes, synced every frame from INSIDE `updatePlayerController(dt, elapsed)` (the one function every one of the four controllers' own `.update()` already funnels through, run immediately after the controller computes that frame's fresh grounded/velocity state) -- deliberately not a separately-registered `app.onUpdate` callback, which fire in registration order and would read one-frame-stale state. `syncPlayerVisual()` follows the controller body's position/rotation (plus the author's offset/scale) every frame including while frozen (a cutscene-frozen player still shows its visual correctly posed), and the controller body itself is hidden (not destroyed) the moment a visual is active.
  - **`resolvePlayerClip(role)`**: checks the author's explicit `clipMap[role]` first (including a deliberate "None" = `null`), falling back to the SAME `findCharacterClip`/`CHARACTER_ANIM_KEYWORDS` name-matching table Priority M's AI Auto-Animate already established (extended with `jump`/`fall` roles) -- one shared keyword convention for both AI and Player, never a second one. `drivePlayerVisualLocomotion()` reads REAL controller state (grounded + speed thresholds) to pick Idle/Walk/Run/Jump, falling back through a chain (jump/fall -> run -> walk -> idle) when a role has no resolvable clip, so the Player is never left frozen on a missing clip.
  - **Attack**: `fireAttack()`'s existing no-op `triggerCharacterAttackAnim(player)` call is replaced with `triggerPlayerAttackAnim()`, reusing Priority M's exact override-deadline mechanism so locomotion doesn't stomp the Attack clip mid-swing, and weapon fire stays exactly synchronized with the existing immediate-damage behavior (no new animation-event system).
  - **Death**: `applyHitDamage`'s `hit.type === 'player'` branch gained the SAME `wasAlreadyDead` re-entrancy guard Priority M's own object-death fix established -- only the genuine alive-to-dead transition triggers `triggerPlayerDeathAnim()`, which (only if a Death clip is actually mapped) plays it, freezes the controller (`playerFrozen = true`), and queues a `deferredCalls` entry (the same generic `{fireAt, fn}` scheduler Priority M built) to call `endGame('lose')` after the clip's own duration. With NO Death clip mapped, this branch does nothing automatic -- bit-for-bit the pre-existing behavior (an author wires their own Node Graph "Health At Most(0) -> Lose"), never a silent change to a project with no skinned Player configured.
  - **Inspector**: a new Player Visual section (asset picker filtered to `assets.type === 'skinnedMesh'`, scale/rotation/position-offset fields, and -- once an asset is selected -- 7 clip-mapping dropdowns plus an "Auto Detect Clips" button running `findCharacterClip` against every unset role) shown only for Third-Person/Top-Down controller types; the controller-type switch handler preserves `.visual` across a switch the same way it already preserves `maxHealth`.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end with real per-frame ticking, not assumed: confirmed visual-entity creation and body-hiding; confirmed Idle/Walk/Run classification at the correct speed thresholds (after correctly ruling out two false alarms as designed behavior, not bugs -- a transient `fall`-state at spawn from pre-landing gravity with no Fall clip mapped correctly falling back to `walk` per the documented fallback chain, and normal exponential-deceleration lag after releasing input, both confirmed correct by ticking further and observing the expected settle); confirmed Jump detection via vertical velocity; confirmed Attack override correctly blocks locomotion for exactly its clip's duration then reverts; confirmed the full Death sequence end-to-end (a lethal `applyHitDamage({type:'player'}, ...)` call correctly clamps health to 0, sets the visual to the Death clip, freezes the controller, and queues exactly one deferred call; a SECOND lethal hit while already dead correctly queues nothing further, proving the re-entrancy guard; advancing sim time past the clip duration and running the real `processDeferredCalls()` correctly fires `endGame('lose')` exactly once). Confirmed a real `serializeProject()`/`loadProjectFull()` round-trip preserves `currentPlayerSettings.visual` bit-exactly (deep-equal before/after). `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 throughout.
  - **Deliberately out of scope this pass**: First-Person optional arms model (assessed as a utility gate, not required -- First-Person keeps its existing invisible collision body unconditionally, matching the spec's own explicit boundary). No animation-event system beyond the existing immediate-damage Attack hook.

- **Crouch ceiling clearance (Priority C, Sections 18-20)** -- the remaining bound Priority B's own CHANGELOG entry explicitly named ("standing back up doesn't check for overhead clearance first") is now fixed.
  - **`canStandAt(position, radius, feetY, standingHeadY)`**: a bounded AABB-approximation ceiling check -- deliberately treats every solid's real (possibly oriented or spherical) collider as an axis-aligned box for this ONE check, since this engine has no general box-vs-box SAT routine (every existing collision function resolves a circular mover against a box, never box-vs-box). Iterates `placed` for solid, non-ramp items via the existing `itemColliderConfig`, testing whether the player's would-be standing extent overlaps any solid above their current position.
  - **`applyCrouchState(entity, wantsCrouch, radius)`** (was `(entity, crouchDown)`): now returns the ACTUALLY-applied crouch state rather than mirroring the input directly -- a player who wants to stand but is currently crouched under something low stays crouched (checked via `canStandAt`) until genuinely clear, distinguishing crouch-REQUESTED from currently-crouched from standing-blocked. Both Third-Person and First-Person call sites updated to use the actually-applied return value for speed calculation, not the raw input.
  - Dogfooded live with real held-key input against real colliders: walked a player through a standing-height hallway into a low duct (forced to crouch to fit), released the Crouch key while still inside the duct, confirmed the player remains crouched (blocked by the duct's own low ceiling, not merely reflecting the released key), walked out of the duct into open space, and confirmed the player automatically stands back up on the next update once genuinely clear. All 19 shipped games reverified loading and playing clean; zero new console errors. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (entirely editor-side; no new engine API needed).

- **True textured terrain layers (Priority D, Sections 21-26)** -- extends (never replaces) the existing painted-color system with up to 3 layers, each with an optional real Texture Asset + UV tiling, reusing the exact same paint weights Priority G's brush already authors -- no second weight-map system.
  - **Mesh** (`engine/src/19-terrain.js`): `createTerrainMesh` now also builds a per-vertex `layerWeights` buffer (`Float32Array`, 3 floats/vertex -- matching the editor's own `PAINT_MAX_LAYERS=3` cap), populated straight from the SAME `opts.paintEdits.weights` grid the existing color blend already reads. All-zero (a complete no-op downstream) for any terrain with no paintEdits at all.
  - **`createColoredMesh`** (`engine/src/08-mesh.js`) gained an optional 5th `layerWeights` parameter, creating a 4th attribute buffer at location 3 only when supplied -- terrain is the only caller, so no other mesh kind is affected; omitting it (every pre-existing call site) allocates an all-zero buffer, bit-identical to before.
  - **Shaders** (`engine/src/07-shader.js`): `TERRAIN_VERT_SRC` passes `a_layerWeights` through as `v_layerWeights`; `TERRAIN_FRAG_SRC` gained 3 optional `sampler2D` + `bool` (`u_terrainHasTexN`) + `float` (`u_terrainUVScaleN`) uniform triples. A textured layer's sample (world-space planar projection, `v_worldPos.xz / uvScale` -- no per-layer UV attribute needed, since every layer tiles uniformly across the terrain's own footprint) is `mix`ed into the albedo weighted by that layer's own `v_layerWeights` component, REPLACING its own flat-color contribution where painted. `u_terrainHasTexN` defaults false for every terrain that has never had a layer texture assigned, making the entire block a no-op and leaving the pre-existing `v_color`-only result bit-identical.
  - **Renderer** (`engine/src/09-renderer.js`): `renderTerrain` binds up to 3 layer textures on units 0/2/3 (never 1 -- already claimed by the shadow map in this same draw call) read from `terrain.layerTextures[li]` (a GPU texture handle) and `terrain.paintLayers[li].uvScale`.
  - **`Opus.App.createTerrain`** (`engine/src/22-api.js`): the returned public terrain handle gained a `layerTextures` accessor (get/set, proxying straight through to the internal render-terrain's own array) -- the SAME "editor resolves Texture-Asset-id to a real GPU handle and assigns it directly, engine just reads whatever's assigned" pattern `entity.texture` already established, since the engine itself has no concept of a Texture Asset Library. `renderTerrain.paintLayers` is a live reference to `opts.paintEdits.layers`, so a UV Scale edit takes effect next frame with no remove/recreate needed.
  - **Editor**: `syncTerrainLayerTextures(item)` -- the one place that resolves each paint layer's optional `textureAssetId` into a real handle via the existing lazy `getTextureHandle`, called after every `app.createTerrain(...)` call site (initial placement, `regenerateTerrain`, duplicate, load) since a fresh handle always resets to `[null,null,null]`; registers the SAME `pendingCallbacks` convention `applyTextureToEntity` already uses for a texture whose image hasn't finished decoding yet. Terrain Inspector's paint-layer rows gained a Texture dropdown (None + every Texture Asset) and a UV Scale (tile size) field per layer, alongside the existing color swatch.
  - **Asset usage-counting + export**: `computeTextureUsageCount`/`serializeTextureAssets` now also scan `record.kind === 'terrain'`'s own `paintEdits.layers[].textureAssetId` (via new shared `terrainLayerTextureIds`) -- a texture referenced ONLY by a terrain layer is no longer wrongly reportable as unused, and is correctly bundled into a portable/standalone export.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end, not assumed from the shader math alone: built a real isolated test terrain, assigned a synthetic checkerboard texture to a paint layer via the REAL Inspector dropdown (a genuine `form_input` click, confirmed to fire `syncTerrainLayerTextures` and update the live handle), and confirmed the rendered result -- a clearly tiled, unmistakable checker pattern across the whole terrain, screenshotted from multiple angles. Confirmed removing the texture assignment reverts the terrain to its exact prior flat-paint-color appearance (the pre-Priority-D look), proving the no-op/backward-compat path. Confirmed usage-counting (`before:0 -> after:1`) and export-inclusion (`includedInExport:true`) for a texture referenced only through a terrain layer. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 throughout.
  - **`painted-wilds.json` upgraded** (the primary Terrain Texture dogfood game, per spec): its existing Path/Ash paint layers now carry real procedurally-generated (noise + speckle, matching this project's established "algorithmic, not hand-drawn" texture-asset house style) tile textures, and a new third Grass layer was added and auto-painted to cover every vertex the original Path/Ash layers left unpainted (2076 of 2401 vertices) -- an obvious, immediately visible upgrade from 3 flat colors to real tiled, speckled ground textures. Verified via a genuine `fetch()` + `loadProjectFull()` (the real client load path, not an in-memory shortcut) followed by `enterPlayMode()` and 60 real ticks: loads and plays clean, zero console errors, screenshotted confirming the textured grass/path/rock all render correctly in both Edit and Play mode.

- **Sequence authoring (Priority E, Sections 27-33)** -- bounded, NOT a cinematic timeline: a Sequence is an ordered list of existing actions (+ an optional Delay/Wait step), reusing `ACTION_TYPES`/`executeAction` directly with no second per-action implementation, and no track/layer/keyframe concept.
  - **Data model** (`editor/src/01-editor.js`): `currentSequences` (per-level, same "apply whenever a level never customized this" pattern Environment/Player/UI/Ambient Audio already follow) -- each `{id, name, steps}`, a step being `{kind:'action', actionType, params}` or `{kind:'delay', seconds}`. Persisted via `syncCurrentLevelObjects`/`applyLevelSequences`, the same per-level sync/apply pair every other authoring surface uses.
  - **Runtime** (`runSequenceSteps`/`playSequence`): executes steps synchronously through `executeAction` until a Delay step, which schedules its own continuation via the SAME generic `deferredCalls` scheduler Priority M's death-animation delay already built -- simulation time, not `setTimeout`, so a Sequence's Delay keeps advancing correctly through a Freeze-Player cutscene (Freeze Player doesn't pause simulation). `runningSequenceIds` is the reentrancy guard: a `playSequence` call for a sequence already running is a no-op (documented **Ignore** policy), never a silent duplicate overlapping run.
  - **New shared action `playSequence`** (`ACTION_TYPES.playSequence`, a `'sequence'` field type in `renderActionFieldInput`): usable from a Behavior or the Node Graph exactly like any other action (the graph's own `GRAPH_NODE_TYPES` picks it up automatically via the existing `Object.entries(ACTION_TYPES)` derivation loop -- no new graph-wiring code needed, same precedent Freeze/Unfreeze Player already established).
  - **Sequence Editor UI**: a new collapsible "Sequences" section on the Level tab -- Sequence select/New/Rename/Duplicate/Delete, and a step list (Add Step with an action-type dropdown covering every `ACTION_TYPES` entry except the two that open their own sub-editor, Move Up/Down, Duplicate Step, Delete Step, each action step's own fields rendered via the SAME `renderActionFieldInput` the fixed-dropdown Add Behavior UI and Node Graph node UI already share).
  - **Cleanup on Stop Play / Restart Level / Switch Level**: `runningSequenceIds`/sequence-tagged `deferredCalls` (a new `sequenceRun: true` tag, distinguishing a Sequence's own continuation from unrelated deferred work like an in-flight object-death removal) are cleared in `enterPlayMode`, `exitPlayMode`, and `switchToLevelDuringPlay` (the shared function Restart Level/Switch Level both funnel through) -- a mid-cutscene Delay can never fire its continuation into a level it no longer belongs to.
  - **A real bug found and fixed while dogfooding, not shipped silently broken**: `serializeProject`'s own level-mapping explicitly whitelists which fields get persisted (`id/name/objects/environment/playerSettings/ui/description/ambientAudio`) -- `sequences` was missing from that list even though `syncCurrentLevelObjects` correctly wrote it onto the in-memory level object, so a real save/load round-trip silently DROPPED every authored Sequence while everything else appeared to work. Caught by an explicit before/after round-trip check (not assumed from the runtime tests alone), fixed by adding `sequences` to both the save-side whitelist and the load-side `normalizeProjectData` mapping; confirmed fixed with a bit-exact round-trip afterward.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end through the REAL Sequence Editor UI (not direct function calls alone): created a sequence via the actual "+ New" button, added 4 real steps (Freeze Player / Delay / Show Message / Unfreeze Player) via the real "+ Add Step" flow, edited the Message's text field and the Delay's seconds field through their real rendered inputs, and exercised Move Up/Down/Duplicate/Delete through real button clicks -- confirming the step order and step data end up exactly as expected after each operation. Confirmed real execution end-to-end: `playSequence` fires Freeze Player synchronously, correctly pauses at the Delay step (schedules a tagged deferred call), and completes Message + Unfreeze Player once the delay elapses (`playerFrozen` false again, `runningSequenceIds` empty). Confirmed the reentrancy guard (a second `playSequence` call mid-run queues nothing extra). Confirmed `executeAction('playSequence', {sequenceId}, item)` -- the exact path a Behavior/Node Graph node uses -- correctly triggers a run. Confirmed `exitPlayMode()` cleanly cancels an in-flight sequence (no dangling deferred continuation). Bounded regression sweep: Blacksite/Moving Temple/Iron Arena/Painted Wilds all load, enter Play, and exit Play clean with zero new console errors. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (entirely editor-side; no new engine API needed).

- **Animation Events utility gate, assessed (Priority F, Section 34) -- deferred, not built.** The concrete example the spec gives ("Attack clip at 0.35s -> Fire Weapon") is already fully satisfied by Priority A's own Attack-animation hook: `triggerPlayerAttackAnim`/`fireAttack` already fire the weapon immediately and stay exactly synchronized with the existing immediate-damage combat behavior, with no timing gap a clip-time event could close. A real per-clip event system (time-tagged action triggers, fired once per pass through that time, reset on loop/clip-switch, correct across multiple instances sharing one skinned asset) is a genuine, non-trivial addition on its own -- and with the one concrete motivating use case already solved, building it now would be complexity without a matching capability gain. Revisit if a later stress game surfaces a real timing gap Priority A's own hook can't cover.

- **Footstep audio (Priority J, Sections 44-45)** -- plays only while the Player is grounded AND actually moving, reusing the exact grounded/velocity state `drivePlayerVisualLocomotion` (Priority A) already computes every frame, no second physics read.
  - **`driveFootstepAudio(dt)`**, called from the same `updatePlayerController` dispatch point every other per-frame Player system already funnels through. Fields (`footstepAudioAssetId`/`footstepInterval`/`footstepWalkVolume`/`footstepRunVolume`) are read lazily straight off `currentPlayerSettings` with sensible code-level defaults (the same `??` pattern `sprintSpeedMultiplier`/`crouchSpeedMultiplier` already use) -- a project saved before this priority has none of these fields and is completely unaffected (`footstepAudioAssetId` is `null` -> the whole function is a no-op). Scoped to Third-Person/First-Person only, matching Crouch/Sprint's own controller scoping (Priority B) -- Hovercraft/Top-Down have no "footsteps on the ground" concept. Walk vs Run volume picks off the SAME `sprintSpeed * 0.7` threshold `drivePlayerVisualLocomotion` already uses to classify Walk vs Run, so footstep cadence/volume and the Player Visual's own locomotion animation always agree about which state the Player is in.
  - **Inspector**: a new Footstep section (Sound dropdown from the existing Audio Library, Interval/Walk Volume/Run Volume) shown only for Third-Person/First-Person, preserved across a controller-type switch the same way `.visual`/`maxHealth` already are.
  - **Terrain-layer-based footstep sound selection -- assessed, NOT built.** The spec's own gate ("ONLY if easy via Priority D's dominant-layer data") turned out not to be easy: no function anywhere in this engine samples "which paint layer is dominant at this XZ position" (Priority D's paint weights live in a CPU-side grid consumed only at mesh-build time, not exposed as a runtime query), so building this would mean adding a genuinely new terrain-sampling API, not wiring up something that already exists. Deferred as a real, named, bounded-out piece rather than silently skipped -- a single authored footstep sound per Player (this priority's actual scope) is what shipped.
  - Dogfooded live with real held-key input and real per-frame ticking, not assumed: spawned a real Third-Person player, let it land (confirmed `grounded: true`), walked forward for 3 real seconds and confirmed footstep calls fire at EXACTLY the configured 0.3s interval (9 calls, every gap `0.300`) at the configured Walk volume; held Sprint and confirmed footstep calls correctly switch to the Run volume once actual velocity crosses the same threshold the locomotion animation uses (and confirmed the brief still-accelerating window reads as Walk, matching the same "acceleration ramp takes real time" behavior Priority B's own Sprint dogfood already established as correct, not a bug); confirmed stopping (grounded, zero input) produces ZERO further footstep calls and resets the internal timer to 0. Confirmed the real Inspector UI renders the Sound dropdown (populated from the real Audio Library) and all three number fields with the correct live values. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (entirely editor-side; no new engine API needed).

- **Multi-select Rotate/Scale (Priority L, Sections 48-50)** -- Wave 6's own Pivot gate found Rotate/Scale only ever affected the primary selected object even with several selected; both gizmos now operate on the WHOLE selection.
  - **`selectionRoots(itemsSet)`** -- the one new function this priority needed, and the key to the parent/child hard edge the spec calls out: filters a selection down to only its ROOTS (an item whose parent, if any, is NOT also in the same selection). A child whose parent IS co-selected is deliberately left untouched by the gizmo drag itself -- the EXISTING `resolveWorldTransform`/`updateHierarchyTransforms` machinery (already running every frame) automatically recomposes that child's correct new world position/orientation/scale from its unchanged `localPosition`/local rotation against the parent's newly-transformed world transform, the exact same mechanism that already correctly carries a child along when only its parent moves. Directly transforming both parent and child independently would double-transform the child; `selectionRoots` is what prevents that.
  - **Group Rotate**: `gizmoDragState.items` (was a single `item`) now covers every selection root, with a shared `pivot` (the roots' own position average -- "Selection Center"). Each root's OWN orientation rotates by the drag angle (same math as before) AND its position orbits the pivot by that same angle/quaternion -- one shared code path for both single- and multi-object selections, since a single-item selection has `pivot === that item's own position`, making the orbit term mathematically zero and reducing to bit-exact pre-Priority-L behavior.
  - **Group Uniform Scale**: same `items`/`pivot` shape -- each root's own scale multiplies by the drag ratio (unchanged math) AND its position is pulled toward/pushed away from the pivot by that SAME ratio, so the group visibly scales as one coherent whole. Same single-item reduction to bit-exact old behavior (`pivot === item's own position` -> zero position term).
  - **Group per-axis Scale (sx/sy/sz)**: deliberately bounded to each root's own scale component only (an additive delta, unchanged math per root) -- positions are left unchanged. Scaling group POSITIONS apart along a single world axis is a real but separate, more ambiguous design question than the pivot-relative uniform case above; not built this pass, to avoid scope creep past what the spec actually asked for. One gesture is still one Undo step for all three (the existing `pushUndo()`-on-first-move gate, untouched).
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end through REAL synthetic pointer drag events on the actual canvas (`pointerdown`/`pointermove`/`pointerup`, dispatched through the genuine event listeners -- not direct function calls), not assumed correct from the math alone. **A real integration bug found and fixed while setting up this exact dogfood, unrelated to the Priority L math itself**: the canvas's own `pointerup` listener lives on `window`, not `canvas` (an existing, correct, deliberate choice so a drag ending outside the canvas still resolves) -- an early test dispatching synthetic `pointerup` on `canvas` left `activePointerId` permanently non-null, silently blocking every subsequent `pointerdown` (`if (playMode || activePointerId !== null) return;`); once dispatched on `window` instead, `activePointerId` correctly reset to `null` every time. Confirmed Group Rotate: two unparented cubes symmetric about a pivot rotated to the SAME orientation quaternion and orbited the pivot with distance-from-pivot preserved EXACTLY (rigid rotation, not distortion). Confirmed the parent/child hard edge directly and non-degenerately: built a real parent+child pair via the actual `reparentItem`, multi-selected both, rotated ~121 degrees around a world axis, and confirmed the child's resulting world position matched an INDEPENDENTLY computed `parent.position + parentQuat.rotate(child.localPosition)` to floating-point precision -- proof the child was carried correctly through the existing hierarchy mechanism with no double-transform. Confirmed Group Uniform Scale: both items scaled by an identical ratio (3.194x) and their distance-from-pivot grew by that EXACT same ratio. Confirmed Group per-axis Scale: both items' chosen axis grew identically, positions correctly left unchanged. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (entirely editor-side; no new engine API needed).

- **Character Authoring Presets (Priorities H/I, Sections 41-43)** -- two one-click convenience buttons over EXISTING mechanisms only (no hidden runtime, no new saved-data shape) -- both are literally "click through these already-working fields for me," not a new feature underneath.
  - **"Create Animated Player"** (Player Controller section): switches Controller Type to Third-Person (via the real `<select>`'s own onchange handler, so the existing maxHealth/visual/footstep preserve-across-switch logic runs in exactly the one place it already lives), sets Player Visual to Skinned Asset (picking the first imported skinned asset if none was already chosen), and runs the exact same clip-name-matching pass the existing "Auto Detect Clips" button already used -- factored into a new shared `autoDetectPlayerClips(visual, asset)` so both call sites share one implementation. Speeds are left at Third-Person's own existing sensible defaults; nothing new needed there.
  - **"Create Animated Enemy"** (Inspector, shown for any selected object with a skinned mesh): fills in AI Follow (if no AI configured yet), Health (100/100 via the existing `ensureObjectHealthInit`), Auto-Animate (`characterAnimSync = true`), and a Ranged Attack slot with sensible Range/Cooldown/Damage defaults -- Projectile Prefab is deliberately left unset (a preset can't guess which prefab an author wants firing; the status message says so explicitly) rather than guessing wrong.
  - Dogfooded live through the REAL buttons (not direct function calls): loaded Iron Arena's own `IronSentry` skinned asset into a fresh session, clicked the real "✨ Create Animated Player" button and confirmed the Inspector shows Controller Type=Third-Person, Player Visual=Skinned Asset, Mesh Asset=IronSentry, with 5 of 7 clips correctly auto-mapped (Jump/Fall correctly left unmapped -- the source asset genuinely has no matching clips, not a bug). Placed a real skinned instance, clicked the real "✨ Create Animated Enemy" button, and confirmed via the Inspector AND direct data inspection that AI/Health/Auto-Animate were all set correctly, and that `attackPrefab` genuinely stayed `undefined` (the dropdown's own first-option-as-default HTML rendering behavior visually SHOWS the first prefab in the list, a pre-existing minor UI quirk shared by every "Ranged Attack" Projectile Prefab dropdown in this editor, not something this priority introduced or needs to fix -- confirmed by reading the actual underlying data, not just the visible dropdown). `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (entirely editor-side; no new engine API needed).

- **Camera sequence polish + Camera Blend Duration (Priority K, Sections 46-47)** -- a Sequence's Change Camera / Delay / Change Camera steps already worked for free (Priority E reuses `ACTION_TYPES.changeCamera` directly, no new wiring needed there); this priority is the optional Camera Blend Duration gate, assessed and built because the existing camera math made it genuinely easy -- no new camera architecture, per the spec's own explicit escape hatch.
  - **`applyCameraPresetBlended(cameraItem, duration)`/`tickCameraBlend(dt)`**: the trick is that `app.camera`'s existing `eye`/`currentTarget` getters already return a real world position/lookAt regardless of which mode (chase/orbit/fixed/followLookAt) happens to be active, and the EXISTING `'fixed'` mode already accepts an arbitrary literal eye+target via `setFixedOptions` -- so `'fixed'` mode is repurposed as the interpolation VEHICLE for the blend window only, never a new camera concept. Start state is read off the live getters (whatever mode was actually active); end state is read the SAME way immediately after really calling `applyCameraPreset` (so "end" is always exactly correct, never a second hand-derived copy of the camera math); every frame in between overwrites `'fixed'` mode's own eye/target/FOV with the lerp; once elapsed reaches the duration, `applyCameraPreset` is called for REAL, handing off to the target camera's own actual configured mode -- a blend into a chase/orbit/followLookAt camera doesn't get stuck in the blend's borrowed `'fixed'` mode.
  - **`changeCamera` action** gained an optional Blend Duration field (seconds, default/omitted = 0 = instant cut, bit-identical to every project saved before this priority) -- `0` routes straight to the unchanged `applyCameraPreset`, so `activeCameraBlend` is never even created for the old behavior.
  - **Cleanup**: `activeCameraBlend` is cleared in `enterPlayMode`/`exitPlayMode`/`switchToLevelDuringPlay`, the same three reset points Priority E's Sequence runtime already established -- a mid-blend camera transition can never keep interpolating into a level or session it no longer belongs to.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end with real ticking, not assumed from the math alone: placed two real Camera objects far apart with different positions/FOVs, started a real 1-second blend via `executeAction('changeCamera', {cameraId, blendSeconds:1}, null)` (the exact path a Sequence step or Node Graph node uses), and confirmed the eye/target/FOV at the exact halfway tick match the expected linear interpolation to floating-point precision on all three; confirmed the blend completes with the camera landing EXACTLY on the target camera's real applied position/mode (not merely close); confirmed the instant-cut path (`blendSeconds` omitted) creates no `activeCameraBlend` at all, bit-identical to the pre-Priority-K behavior; confirmed `exitPlayMode()` correctly cancels an in-flight blend. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (entirely editor-side; no new engine API needed).

- **Level Duplicate + asset-heavy dogfood (Priority M, Section 51)** -- Duplicate Level already existed (Wave 5); this priority's own required dogfood (a level with a skinned Player, skinned NPCs, a Sequence, Prefabs, and audio -- duplicate it, modify the duplicate, verify independence while assets stay correctly shared) surfaced a real bug, found and fixed, not assumed correct from reading the code.
  - **A real bug found and fixed while dogfooding, matching the exact class of gap Priority E's own `serializeProject` fix already caught once this wave**: the Duplicate Level handler explicitly whitelists which per-level fields get cloned (`objects`/`environment`/`playerSettings`/`ui`/`description`/`ambientAudio`) -- `sequences` was missing from that list, so duplicating a level with an authored Sequence silently DROPPED it entirely from the new copy. Fixed by adding `sequences` to both the clone-source and the newly-spliced level record.
  - **A second, deeper correctness issue fixed alongside it**: a Sequence step can reference another object by id (`changeCamera`'s `cameraId`, `moveObject`'s `targetId`, ...) -- these ids live OUTSIDE `objects` itself, so naively cloning `sequences` verbatim would leave the duplicate's own Sequence pointing at the ORIGINAL level's Camera/target object, not its own. Fixed by extending the existing `remapObjectIds` whole-JSON-string-substitution mechanism (already used for Prefab instantiation) to optionally take a SECOND array and remap ids across both together -- a Sequence step's object-id reference gets rewritten to the duplicate's own remapped id for free, the same substitution pass, no separate per-field reference-walking code. The single-array call site (Prefab instantiation) is untouched and unaffected.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end through the REAL "Duplicate Level" button (not a direct function call): loaded Iron Arena (a real skinned-NPC combat level with 5 real Prefabs), added a real Camera object and a real Sequence (`Freeze Player -> Change Camera -> Delay -> Unfreeze Player`) referencing that camera by id, configured a real skinned Player Visual off the level's own `IronSentry` asset, clicked the actual button, and confirmed: the duplicate's Sequence carries its own REMAPPED `cameraId` (a real, different object id, verified to resolve to a genuine, independent Camera object in the duplicate's own `objects` array -- not the original's); modifying the duplicate's Sequence (renamed, added a step) and Player Settings (`moveSpeed`) left the ORIGINAL level's own copies of both completely unchanged; the project-wide asset arrays (skinned mesh, prefabs) stayed at exactly their original counts throughout (1 skinned asset, 5 prefabs -- correctly SHARED by reference across both levels, never duplicated); the duplicate's own (modified) Sequence executes correctly in a real Play session (`playSequence` correctly freezes the player and tracks the run). `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (entirely editor-side; no new engine API needed).

- **Skinned asset sharing + save size, verified (Sections 53-54)** -- both explicitly asked to be VERIFIED (and fixed if violated), not assumed correct; both checked live and found already correct, no fix needed.
  - **Sharing**: placed 25 real skinned instances (via the real `placeAssetInstance`) of the SAME asset and confirmed all 25 `entity.skin` references point to the exact same object (`getAssetMeshHandle`'s own `if (!asset.meshHandle)` cache, already established by Priority C, does exactly this correctly) -- the immutable geometry/skin/clip payload is genuinely shared, not duplicated 25 times. Diverged per-instance runtime state (animation clip/time, Health, AI) on two of them and confirmed each instance's own state stayed fully independent.
  - **Save size**: serialized a real project containing 28 skinned instances (25 test + Iron Arena's own 3 Sentries) and confirmed NONE of their individual saved records carry any skin/geometry/clip payload (`positions`/`joints`/`weights`/`clips`/`skin` all absent) -- each instance's record is just a lightweight reference (`assetId`) plus its own per-instance fields, while `project.assets` carries the actual skinned geometry/rig data exactly ONCE, confirmed by directly inspecting the serialized output, not assumed from the code alone.

- **Binding-aware HUD prompts (Priority G, part of Sections 35-40)** -- a HUD Label/Objective can now embed `{bind:actionName}` (e.g. `[{bind:interact}] Open Door`), resolving to the CURRENT effective binding's readable label so a rebound key shows correctly in-HUD too, not just in the Controls panel.
  - **`resolveHudBindText(text)`**: reuses the exact same `keyCodeToLabel` the Controls/rebind panel already uses (so "Mouse 2"/"Right Click"-style labels read identically everywhere, no separate formatting) and `effectiveInputMap` (the same live binding state Player Controllers themselves read every frame) -- readable text only, no icon art, matching the spec's own explicit scope. An unrecognized action name (`{bind:notReal}`) is left as literal text rather than silently vanishing, so an author notices the typo instead of shipping a blank prompt. Wired into HUD Label AND Objective rendering (`renderHudLayer`), running BEFORE the existing generic `{counterName}` token substitution so both can appear in the same authored string.
  - **Touch deliberately never shows a keyboard/mouse binding at all** (there isn't a meaningful one to show on a touchscreen) -- it always reads a generic action label (`INPUT_ACTION_LABELS[action]`, e.g. "Interact") instead, per the spec's own explicit requirement.
  - Dogfooded live: confirmed the default binding resolves correctly (`{bind:interact}` -> `F`, `{bind:attack}` -> `Q`); confirmed a real rebind (`effectiveInputMap.interact = 'Mouse2'`) immediately changes the resolved HUD text to `Right Click` and reverts correctly once un-rebound; confirmed an unknown action name is left untouched rather than blanked; confirmed the touch-mode branch reads the plain `INPUT_ACTION_LABELS` entry. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (entirely editor-side; no new engine API needed).

- **"Relic Runner 2" -- required animated Third-Person stress game, shipped as `games/relic-runner-2.json` (Section 61).** The real threshold this whole Priority-A-through-M arc was building toward: select a character asset, map its clips, press Play, and get a visually animated Third-Person character that walks/sprints/jumps/attacks/dies/blends naturally across textured, ramped terrain, inside a finished playable game with a cutscene and Win/Lose.
  - Built entirely from THIS wave's own new pieces, not a Wave 6 fallback: a real textured, painted island terrain (two layers -- Grass and Rock, both real procedurally-generated tile textures, Rock concentrated around the ramp/platform cluster) with a Ramp leading up to a solid elevated platform, two solid obstacle colliders, a real skinned Player (Third-Person, the shipped `IronSentry` asset, auto-mapped Idle/Walk/Run/Attack/Death clips, Sprint/Crouch/Jump all enabled, a real Hitscan weapon for mouse Attack), a real Animated Enemy (AI Follow + Auto-Animate + Ranged Attack, built with the exact fields Priority I's own "Create Animated Enemy" preset fills in), a Checkpoint trigger, a glowing Relic Win trigger (a real `sound` behavior + `win` behavior on enter), Health-depletion Lose (the Player Visual's mapped Death clip drives the same automatic freeze-then-Lose sequence Priority A built), a real binding-aware HUD prompt (`[{bind:attack}] Attack -- [{bind:sprint}] Sprint -- [{bind:crouch}] Crouch`), and a real intro Sequence/cutscene (Freeze Player -> Change Camera -> Message -> Delay -> Change Camera back to gameplay, blended -> Unfreeze Player).
  - **A real, non-obvious engine-ordering fact found and worked around while authoring the cutscene, not a product bug**: `enterPlayMode`'s own call order runs `runStartBehaviors()` (which is what fires an "On Start" Sequence) BEFORE `spawnPlayer()` -- so a Sequence's own Change Camera step, if it's the FIRST thing to run, gets its camera change immediately overwritten by the Player's own spawn-time camera setup moments later. This is a pre-existing property of the engine's own start-up ordering (equally true of a plain "On Start: Change Camera" Node Graph/Behavior, not something Priority E's Sequence system introduced), worked around here by inserting a tiny (0.05s) Delay as the Sequence's own first real step -- its continuation fires on a LATER tick, after spawn has already fully completed, so the camera change correctly sticks. Documented here as a real authoring lesson for anyone else building an On-Start camera cutscene.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end via a genuine `fetch()` + `loadProjectFull()` load of the actual saved file (not an in-memory shortcut) followed by real per-frame ticking through the app's own registered update callbacks (not hand-picked partial ticks): confirmed the intro Sequence runs correctly (Freeze -> fixed intro camera -> message -> unfreeze -> blended return to the gameplay chase camera); confirmed the Animated Enemy's AI/Auto-Animate are live; confirmed the HUD's binding-aware prompt resolves to readable text; confirmed reaching the Relic correctly ends the game with the authored Win title/message; confirmed a full-health-loss death correctly plays through the Death clip and ends the game with the authored Lose title/message; confirmed zero console errors throughout. Screenshotted the textured terrain (a clearly visible grass-to-rock transition around the ramp) for visual confirmation. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 throughout.
  - **A small, generally-useful fix made while polishing the HUD prompt**: `keyCodeToLabel` (used by both the Controls panel and the new `{bind:X}` HUD tokens) gained readable labels for Shift/Ctrl/Alt (collapsing Left/Right variants into one clean label), Digit keys, and Arrow keys -- found because the default Sprint binding (`ShiftLeft`) rendered as the literal, clunky "ShiftLeft" in a real HUD prompt instead of "Shift".

- **Continue (Priority G, part of Sections 35-40)** -- a real, previously-unbuilt piece the Main Menu needed: a "▶ Continue" button that appears ONLY when a valid save already exists, reusing the exact same `Opus.SaveSystem('doengine:playersave')` slot the existing Save Game/Load Game actions already read and write (`SaveSystem.has('default')`), never a second save-slot system. Wired into `updateMainMenuExtras` (the same function that already toggles How To Play/Credits visibility) so it's gated fresh every time the Main Menu is shown. Clicking it dismisses the Main Menu and runs the real `loadGame` action for real -- exactly what a player expects, no separate "continue" data path.
  - Dogfooded live: confirmed Continue stays hidden with no save present; confirmed it appears the moment a real save is written (`executeAction('saveGame', ...)`); confirmed clicking it actually restores the saved `window.__gameState` (a real value round-tripped through a genuine save+overwrite+Continue-click sequence) and dismisses the Main Menu overlay. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (entirely editor-side; no new engine API needed -- `SaveSystem.has` already existed, just had no editor-side caller until now).

- **A real, significant pre-existing bug found and fixed while dogfooding Signal Zero's own required multi-level/multi-game-load scenario, not assumed correct from reading the code alone.** `effectiveInputMap` (the ONE Proxy every controller/gamepad/touch/interact call site reads to get "whichever binding is actually in effect") was constructed as `new Proxy(inputActionMap, {...})` -- passing the `inputActionMap` OBJECT as the Proxy's own `target`, captured once at module load. `loadProjectFull` REASSIGNS the `inputActionMap` variable to a brand-new object on every project load (`inputActionMap = {...INPUT_ACTION_DEFAULTS, ...(normalized.inputActionMap || {})}`) -- but a Proxy's target is bound at construction and can never follow a later reassignment of the variable that was passed in. The result: `effectiveInputMap` silently kept reading whichever project happened to be loaded FIRST in a page session (or the hardcoded pre-any-load default) forever after -- every subsequently loaded project's own custom input bindings (e.g. Signal Zero's Attack rebound to `Mouse0`) were completely inert in real gameplay, even though `inputActionMap` itself correctly held the right value the whole time (so the Controls panel and every other direct reader looked completely correct, masking the bug). **Fixed** by closing over the outer `inputActionMap` binding (a `let`, always current) inside the Proxy's `get` trap instead of relying on its own frozen target -- confirmed with a real three-project load sequence (Iron Arena `KeyQ` -> Signal Zero `Mouse0` -> Iron Arena `KeyQ` again) showing `effectiveInputMap.attack` correctly tracking each fresh load instead of staying stuck on the first one. This bug likely affected every multi-game editor session throughout this entire Wave (a project's authored non-default bindings never actually worked once a second project had been loaded that session) -- caught only because Priority G's own required stress-game dogfood specifically exercises loading multiple real games back-to-back in one session, exactly the scenario needed to surface it.

- **"Signal Zero" -- required game-shell/export stress game, shipped as `games/signal-zero.json` (Section 62).** A small, finished 2-level game exercising the full menu/save/export shell, not a feature laboratory: Main Menu (with Continue -- this wave's own new piece, see its CHANGELOG entry above -- How To Play, Credits, Controls, Settings all reachable), Pause Menu, a Sequence-driven intro cutscene, a real skinned Player (Third-Person, mouse-button Attack via a real Hitscan weapon, Sprint, Crouch), an Animated Enemy per level, a Save Game trigger that also performs a real mid-play Switch Level (fade transition), and a Win/Lose pair on the second level.
  - **Level 1 ("Signal Outpost")**: On-Start Sequence (Freeze -> [tiny Delay, the same spawn-ordering workaround Relic Runner 2's own CHANGELOG entry documents] -> Change Camera -> Message -> Delay -> Change Camera back to gameplay, blended -> Unfreeze), a real Animated Enemy, and a glowing "Relay Console" trigger whose THREE real behaviors (Save Game -> a synth sound -> Switch Level to level 2) all fire off one single `enter` event.
  - **Level 2 ("Deep Relay")**: no Start Screen (mid-game, not a fresh session), its own Animated Enemy, and a "Signal Core" Win trigger.
  - **`inputActionMap`** deliberately authors Attack as `Mouse0` (not the usual `KeyQ` default) specifically to exercise a genuinely different binding end-to-end -- this is exactly the authored choice that surfaced the `effectiveInputMap` staleness bug above.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end via genuine `fetch()` + `loadProjectFull()` loads (not in-memory shortcuts) and real per-frame ticking through the app's own registered update callbacks: confirmed Continue stays hidden with no save and correctly appears/persists across a full page reload once the Relay Console is reached; confirmed How To Play/Credits buttons show (real authored text); confirmed the intro cutscene runs correctly; confirmed reaching the Relay Console saves AND switches to level 2 (a real `mode:'fade'` transition, confirmed to complete on its own real-world timer independent of simulated-time ticking -- a genuine, documented distinction between simulated `dt` and the fade transition's own real `setTimeout`, not a bug); confirmed the Signal Core correctly ends the game with the authored Win title/message on level 2; confirmed zero console errors throughout the full multi-level, multi-load session. `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 throughout.

- **A real, SEVERE bug found and fixed while dogfooding Playable Export in a genuine clean-room (Priority G, Sections 55-56) -- every Standalone/Playable export shipped with the entire game engine silently missing.** `buildExportHTML`'s first `.replace()` call still searched for the OLD literal engine-script tag (`<script src="../dist/opus-engine.js"></script>`) to inline `dist/opus-engine.js` into the export -- but Wave 6's own Priority C already changed that tag into a `document.write`-based cache-buster (to fix a DIFFERENT, unrelated stale-bundle dev problem), so that exact literal string no longer exists anywhere in `index.html`, making this `.replace()` call a guaranteed silent no-op. Worse, the SECOND `.replace()` (a regex that correctly tracks the current `document.write` block's shape, added specifically to survive the editor script's own version of this exact hazard) matches the WHOLE dev-time block -- which, as of Priority C, contains BOTH the engine's and the editor's `document.write` calls together -- and replaces the entire thing with ONLY the editor JS inlined.
