Net effect: every export (both "Editable" and "Playable" tiers) shipped with `Opus`/`App` completely undefined, unable to run AT ALL the instant it's opened outside this exact dev folder, while the export UI still reported success with no error (writing out a broken file never throws). This had been broken since Priority C landed, undetected because no export had been opened in a genuine standalone/clean-room context since -- exactly the gap Section 55-56's own dogfood requirement exists to catch.
  - **Fixed** by folding both inlines into the ONE regex-based replace that already correctly tracks the block's current shape, in the correct dependency order (engine before editor, since editor code calls into `Opus`/`App` at load time) -- one replace, not two that can independently drift out of sync with this block again.
  - Dogfooded in a genuine clean-room, not just re-reading the code: built a real Playable export of Relic Runner 2, confirmed the actual engine bundle's own first line is present in the output (absent before the fix, present after); loaded the exported HTML into a truly ISOLATED `<iframe srcdoc>` (its own separate JS realm with zero access to the parent page's own `Opus`/`app`/anything) and confirmed `Opus`/`app` both initialize there, the Main Menu renders with the correct authored title/subtitle/buttons, clicking Start actually enters Play (`playMode: true`, a real spawned Third-Person player, confirmed by injecting a tiny probe script INTO the iframe's own realm rather than reaching in from outside, since `let`-declared globals aren't reachable as window properties across a realm boundary). `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed green at 31/31 (entirely editor-side; no new engine API needed).

### Capability Expansion Wave 5 (closed out — see CAPABILITY-WAVE-5-REPORT.md)
- **Full XYZ object rotation (Priority A)** -- the single biggest architectural ceiling Wave 4 left standing: every rotation in this engine was Y-only (`rotationY`, a bare number). Replaced with a real quaternion-backed orientation model, layered underneath the existing API rather than beside it, so there is exactly ONE hierarchy/rendering transform path, never a second Y-only one running in parallel to desync from it.
  - **Engine (`engine/src/01-vec3.js`, `02-mat4.js`, `11-entity.js`)**: a new public `Opus.Quat` class (identity/fromAxisAngle/fromEuler/multiply/normalize/conjugate/rotateVec3/toEuler/slerp), `Mat4.fromQuaternion`/`Mat4.rotationZ`, and `Mat4.compose(position, rotation, scale)` extended to accept EITHER a `Quat` (new) or a plain number (the exact pre-Wave-5 signature, unchanged). `Entity` now stores a real `_quat` as its authoritative orientation, with `.quaternion` as the new full-orientation accessor and `.rotationY` kept as a genuinely separate RAW stored number (not trig-derived on read) specifically so `entity.rotationY = v; entity.rotationY === v` stays bit-exact -- a trig round-trip through a quaternion cannot generally reproduce a double bit-for-bit, and the Hovercraft demo's own steering math (among every other pre-Wave-5 caller) depends on that exact equality holding. New `.forward`/`.right`/`.up` entity accessors (quaternion-rotated local axes) replace the need for gameplay code to hand-derive `(sin(rotationY), 0, cos(rotationY))` itself.
  - **Bit-exact backward compatibility, proven not assumed**: `Mat4.fromQuaternion(pureYQuat)` produces a matrix IDENTICAL (every one of 16 floats, `===`) to the old `Mat4.rotationY` for the same angle -- confirmed by direct computation before writing a single line of hierarchy/editor code on top of it. This is what guarantees every project saved before Wave 5 renders pixel-identically: a legacy Y-only entity's quaternion is always `Quat.fromEuler(0, rotationY, 0)`, a pure-Y quaternion, which composes to that same bit-identical matrix.
  - **Euler convention**: `fromEuler(pitchX, yawY, rollZ)` composes as `qYaw * qPitch * qRoll` (intrinsic Yaw→Pitch→Roll, the standard vehicle/aircraft order) -- chosen specifically because it's the one order under which `fromEuler(0, yaw, 0)` reproduces the engine's original Y-only rotation exactly. `toEuler()` is its exact inverse away from the pitch=±90° gimbal-lock singularity every Euler representation shares (an authoring-display concern only -- the underlying quaternion itself is never ambiguous, only its Euler-angle READING briefly is at that one pole).
  - **Hierarchy (`editor/src/01-editor.js`)**: `resolveWorldTransform` rewritten from Y-only rotation-matrix composition to `worldQuat = parentWorldQuat.multiply(localQuat)` / `worldPos = parentWorldPos + parentWorldQuat.rotateVec3(scaledLocal)` -- the direct quaternion generalization of the old math, still the ONE composition path. `itemLocalQuat(item)` reads `item.localQuat` if present, else derives a pure-Y quaternion from the legacy `item.localRotationY` -- an old saved hierarchy needs no separate migration step at all, because "pure Y rotation" is simply the specific quaternion that field already implies. `reparentItem` generalizes its old `worldRotY - parentRotY` subtraction to `localQuat = inverse(parentWorldQuat) * childWorldQuat`, preserving a reparented object's exact world orientation under full XYZ, not just Y. Every entity-backed child kind (cube/camera/spawner/**light**) now inherits full parent orientation, not just cubes -- Wave 4 restricted rotation inheritance to cubes only, since Y-only rotation had no meaningful effect on a Camera/Spawner/Light marker; full XYZ orientation does (this wave's own dogfood requirement: a rotated parent must correctly reorient a child spot light, camera rig, or weapon muzzle).
  - **Spot light direction now follows parent rotation** -- a real, previously-documented bound closed as a direct consequence: `options.direction` is now read as a LOCAL aim direction, and the light handle's actual world direction is computed every frame as `entity.quaternion.rotateVec3(localDirection)`. For an unparented light `entity.quaternion` is always identity (nothing else ever sets it), so `identity.rotateVec3(d) === d` and every pre-Wave-5 standalone light is completely unaffected; a parented spot light's cone now correctly sweeps with its parent's full rotation.
  - **Project schema**: `rotationY` keeps serializing unconditionally (unchanged); a new `quaternion: [x,y,z,w]` field is added ONLY when an object has genuine pitch/roll (`hasPitchOrRoll`, comparing the decomposed Euler x/z against ~0) -- an untouched, pure-yaw object's saved record stays byte-for-byte identical to its pre-Wave-5 shape, the same "omitted entirely at defaults" discipline Materials 2.0 already established. Hierarchy children gained the equivalent `localQuat: [x,y,z,w]` (replacing `localRotationY` going forward, which old saves still carry and the loader still reads as a fallback).
  - **Rotate gizmo: three rings (X=red/Y=green/Z=blue)**, replacing the old single Y-only point handle. Each ring is drawn as 16 small markers around a fixed circle in that axis's own plane (no continuous-curve mesh exists in this engine -- the same "approximate with arranged cubes" precedent the Waypoint Graph's connection LINES already established for a missing primitive). Dragging composes a WORLD-space rotation on top of the object's orientation at grab time (`Quat.fromAxisAngle(axis, draggedAngle).multiply(startQuat)`), snapping (reusing the existing `gizmoSnap.rotationDegrees` setting) and one Undo step per completed drag both preserved unchanged from the old gizmo's behavior.
  - **Two real bugs found and fixed while dogfooding the new gizmo, not assumed correct from the math alone**: (1) the very first pointermove after grabbing a ring silently dropped its own angular delta (the "previous angle" baseline was lazily set ON that first move instead of at grab time), measured directly by dragging a ring end-to-end and finding the resulting rotation smaller than the mouse actually swept -- fixed by computing the grab-time angle in the pointerdown handler itself. (2) The first ring-picking implementation (ray-vs-plane, then check distance-from-origin ≈ radius) was ambiguous exactly at the axis seams: a ring's own theta=0 reference point sits exactly on the shared boundary where TWO rings' planes intersect (e.g. `(R,0,0)` lies in both the Z-ring's z=0 plane AND the Y-ring's y=0 plane, at distance R from origin in both), so dragging the Z ring from that point picked the Y ring instead -- caught by dragging each ring in turn and finding Z's rotation landing on Y. Fixed by picking against each ring's actual rendered segment markers (closest-point-on-ray-to-a-point, the same math already used for the uniform-scale handle) instead of the generic plane/radius test, which has no such seam ambiguity since two different rings' segments are never at the same point in space.
  - **Inspector**: the single "Rotation Y (degrees)" field is now three fields, "Rotation X / Y / Z (degrees)". Each field's `oninput` reads ALL THREE current field values and rebuilds the whole orientation via `Quat.fromEuler` in one shot -- there is no way to "adjust just one axis" of an already-composed quaternion in isolation (that has no single correct meaning without first committing to an Euler order, which is exactly what `fromEuler` already does), so editing one field must never silently zero out what the other two already held.
  - Dogfooded a real "Spaceship" hierarchy (root + Wing L/R each with a child Thruster, + Turret Mount → Turret, + child Point Light/Camera/particle Emitter) exactly as the spec's own required stress case: rotated the root by a COMBINED (X=0.4, Y=0.9, Z=0.3 rad) orientation and verified every child's resulting world position against an INDEPENDENTLY hand-computed value (`parentPos + parentQuat.rotateVec3(originalOffset)`) -- bit-for-bit matching to the 4th decimal for Wing L, Thruster L, and Turret simultaneously. Confirmed the light HANDLE (the actual rendering light, distinct from its marker entity) matches the marker's resolved world position after the same combined rotation + a translation. Confirmed a real pre-Wave-5 hierarchy game (`power-station.json`, a `localRotationY`-only Turbine Rotor with zero `localQuat`) loads through the new fallback path unchanged (`euler: 0,0,0`, matching its authored zero rotation exactly) and its Play Animation / Win condition still fire correctly end-to-end. All 13 shipped stress games reverified loading clean in one sweep; `node --check` + `node build/build.js` + `node tests/run-tests.js` (now with 2 new dedicated Quat/Entity transform tests covering bit-exact Mat4 equivalence, Euler round-trip, non-commutative composition, and legacy-entity byte-for-byte matrix equivalence) stayed green throughout -- 25/25.
- **Runtime multi-object Prefab Assemblies (Priority B)** -- closes Wave 4's own documented gap: Spawner and the Spawn Prefab action only ever instantiated a multi-object prefab's ROOT record, silently dropping every other piece. New `spawnPrefabAssembly(prefab, position)` reuses `instantiatePrefabAssembly` -- the exact function design-time prefab placement already uses, itself built on `remapObjectIds`, the ONE shared id-remapping mechanism (fresh ids + every internal reference rewritten: `parentId`, AI `targetId`, Camera `lookAtTargetId`, Waypoint `connections`, animation target, any `ACTION_TYPES` field of type `target`/`cameraPreset`) -- no second remap implementation for the runtime path. A single-object prefab (still the common case: one Bolt, one simple Hunter) takes the exact one-record path it always did, completely unaffected.
  - **Assembly ownership**: a genuine multi-object spawn gets a fresh `assemblyId` shared by every member plus `assemblyRootId` pointing at the root -- a single-object spawn gets NEITHER, so every pre-Wave-5 single-prefab Spawner/Spawn Prefab behaves exactly as before, nothing new to track or cascade. New `removeAssembly(item)` destroys every member sharing an item's `assemblyId` (or just the item itself if it has none -- identical to plain `removeItemFromScene`). Deliberately a SEPARATE function from `removeItemFromScene` rather than making that one cascade automatically: `removeItemFromScene` is called from many existing places whose current single-object semantics must not silently change (a new **Destroy Assembly** action sits alongside the existing **Destroy Object**, matching the spec's own explicit ask for "just this piece" vs. "the whole assembly" to stay distinguishable, not merged). The natural combat-death path (`damageObject`'s own health-reaches-0 check, and a projectile's kill in `tickProjectiles`) now calls `removeAssembly` instead -- an enemy dying to combat is exactly the case that must leave no orphaned children behind, per the spec's own dogfood requirement, while Destroy Object stays deliberately non-cascading (shooting off just a robot's Weapon Mount without destroying the whole robot is a real, distinct use case).
  - **Spawner 2.0**: `activeIds` now tracks ONE id per logical spawn -- the assembly's root, whether it's a lone object or a whole assembly -- so `Max Active`/`Total Count` already correctly count assemblies, not individual child pieces, with no new counting logic (the existing `tickSpawners` prune-filter already only checks "is this one id still in `placed`", and `removeAssembly` guarantees the root disappears exactly when the whole assembly does). Root placement determines the assembly's transform and scatter radius; children keep their authored local offsets, never independently randomized. Spawner Tag now applies to every assembly member, not just the root, so Count/Hide/Destroy Tagged keep finding every piece. `Reset Spawner` now calls `removeAssembly` per tracked id instead of `removeItemFromScene`, so resetting a spawner mid-wave never orphans a still-alive assembly's other parts.
  - Dogfooded with the spec's own required Robot: a 6-piece assembly (Root [tagged `enemy`, Follow AI, contact damage] → Body, → Head → a child Point Light, → Weapon Mount → a child Muzzle Emitter) saved as a real multi-object prefab through the actual Save-as-Prefab data path, spawned 3 times from a real Spawner (`spawnOneFromSpawner`, not a hand-built scene). Verified, in one atomic script (avoiding this session's own documented cross-call background-tick noise): 19 total placed objects after 3 spawns (1 spawner + 3×6-piece robots) with every one of the 18 spawned ids genuinely unique; each robot's Weapon Mount sits at the correct hierarchy-composed world offset from its own root (not the prefab's original authored position); each robot's Head Light has a live, independently-created `lightHandle`; each robot's Muzzle Emitter is a real particle emitter. Killed one robot via the actual `damageObject` action reaching 0 health and confirmed exactly 6 objects were removed (the whole assembly, no more, no less: `19 → 13`), zero remaining members of that assembly (no orphaned Head/Weapon Mount/light/emitter), lights and emitters both correctly down to 2 (matching the 2 surviving robots), and the Spawner's `activeIds` correctly shrunk to the 2 survivors' root ids. All 13 shipped stress games reverified loading clean; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 25/25 (entirely editor-side; no engine-layer surface).
- **First-Person Controller (Priority C) + Complete Input Actions (Priority D)** -- built together since the new controller's own movement needed the Input Action map extended to cover it anyway. Justified now, per the spec's own reasoning: solids, vertical physics/jump, projectiles/weapons, and Input Actions all already exist.
  - **Physics reuse, not a second solver**: `updateFirstPersonController` runs the EXACT same terrain collision / vertical (landing-on-top) box collision / horizontal solid push / map boundary sequence Third-Person already uses, called the identical way -- no new collision code anywhere. The one genuinely new piece is camera ownership: the controller drives `app.camera` directly in `'fixed'` mode every frame (`setFixedOptions({eye, target})`, `camera.fovY`) instead of a chase camera trailing a visible model -- both were ALREADY public engine API, just never driven by a per-frame controller before, so no new engine surface was needed (per the spec's own "keep editor scene-graph bookkeeping editor-side" default). The player entity still exists (collision needs a real object) but is invisible, the standard "don't render your own body" FPS convention.
  - **Look**: yaw/pitch state (`fpYaw`/`fpPitch`, pitch clamped short of ±90° to avoid the gimbal-lock pole) drives the entity's REAL quaternion orientation (`Quat.fromEuler(pitch, yaw, 0)`, Priority A's own foundation), so `entity.forward` is correct for both the rendered camera direction AND weapon aim. One shared `applyFirstPersonLook(dx, dy, sensitivity)` function is fed by all three input sources -- Pointer Lock `mousemove` (`movementX/Y`), a dedicated touch drag zone on the right half of the viewport, and gamepad right-stick (scaled by `dt`, dead zone preserved) -- so none of the three can ever disagree about sensitivity or pitch clamping.
  - **Pointer Lock**: requested on Play start for a non-touch device, released on Stop AND on every pause (Pause button, Start Screen, Dialogue -- reusing the existing `onPauseChange` callback that already fires for all three) so the cursor is always free to click paused-state UI, and re-acquired automatically on resume -- "do not trap users in Pointer Lock." A canvas click mid-session re-acquires it if the browser's own Escape handling (or a lost-focus tab switch) already dropped it.
  - **`fireAttack` generalized**: replaced its hand-derived `(sin(rotationY), 0, cos(rotationY))` aim vector with `player.entity.forward` (Priority A's accessor) -- identical result for every Y-only controller (their quaternion only ever holds yaw), and now correctly includes pitch for First-Person, so aiming up/down actually changes where a shot goes.
  - **Complete Input Actions**: `inputActionMap` grows from `{interact, attack}` to also cover `moveForward/moveBack/moveLeft/moveRight/jump`, read by ALL FOUR Player Controllers now (`inputActionMap.moveForward` etc, replacing every controller's own hardcoded `'KeyW'`-style check) -- every default is the exact key it always was, so behavior under default mappings is unchanged, and Arrow keys stay a permanent, non-rebindable SECOND binding alongside whatever the primary is rebound to. Touch's D-pad/Boost buttons and gamepad's stick/face-button polling both read the CURRENT binding at press/poll time (a small `wireTouchButton` change: a button's key can now be a getter function, not just a fixed code), so a rebind takes effect on touch and gamepad with zero device-specific rebind code, the same pattern Interact/Attack already established in Wave 4. Input Collision Policy (section 23): rebinding warns (via the status toast) if the new key is already used by another action, but doesn't block the rebind -- a bounded "warn, don't block" policy, since an author might genuinely want two actions sharing one key. Default Controller Maps (section 24): the rebind panel only lists actions the CURRENT controller type actually reads (Top-Down has no Jump row at all; Hovercraft's `jump` slot is relabeled "Boost", the exact key it already was).
  - Dogfooded end-to-end via real physics ticks, not assumed from the math: spawned a First-Person player on real terrain, held Move Forward for 2 simulated seconds, and confirmed real forward displacement; applied a 90° yaw turn and confirmed `entity.forward` swung from `(0,0,1)` to `(-1,0,0)` exactly; applied a 30° pitch and confirmed it registered. Dropped the player from above a real solid platform and confirmed it landed and stayed EXACTLY on top (`y: 2.050`, matching `platformTop(1.5) + halfHeight(0.55)` bit-for-bit) with `firstPersonGrounded: true`; pressed Jump while grounded and confirmed velocity became exactly the configured `jumpSpeed` (8). Set pitch to 17° and confirmed `fireAttack`'s aim vector correctly tilted (`forward.y: -0.30`) -- aiming up/down now actually changes shot trajectory, closing a real gap the old rotationY-only aim couldn't have expressed. Reconfirmed Third-Person's movement is bit-identical after the Input Actions migration, both via the new rebindable default key AND the Arrow-key fallback. **Found and fixed a real robustness gap while dogfooding, not assumed correct**: `canvas.requestPointerLock()` throws/rejects in this session's own sandboxed preview frame (`WrongDocumentError`) -- entirely expected for an embedded frame, not a product bug, but the call was originally unguarded, producing an uncaught error. Fixed with a defensive try/catch AND a Promise `.catch()` (different browsers surface a Pointer-Lock-denial differently, so both paths are covered) -- confirmed by triggering 5 more requests after the fix and finding zero new console errors. All 13 shipped stress games reverified loading clean; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 25/25.
- **Moving Solids / Kinematic World (Priority E)** -- Property Animation can already move solid objects; vertical physics can already stand on solids. This makes the two properly coexist: a mover standing on a solid that's ITSELF moving now moves with it, rather than being left behind or sliding off as the platform translates.
  - **Platform carry**: new `applyPlatformCarry(moverEntity, item, isGroundedOnThis)`, called once per solid per frame from each jump-capable Player Controller's own collision loop (Third-Person, First-Person) right after its vertical collision check. Deliberately self-contained -- no separate per-frame "capture platform positions" hook needed: it stashes `item._carryPrevPos` on the platform ITSELF, and since Property Animation/Tweens (`updateAnimations`/`tickTweens`) are both registered, and therefore run, BEFORE the main gameplay loop's Player Controller update every frame, `item.entity.position` is already THIS frame's moved position by the time this function reads it -- the delta against last frame's stashed value is exactly how far the platform moved this one frame. Applied to the mover only when actually grounded on that specific item (so standing near, but not on, a moving platform doesn't get dragged along); the position is refreshed on every solid every frame regardless, so transferring between platforms never inherits a stale delta. Purely kinematic (position-driven), no mass/rigid-body constraint involved -- `_carryPrevPos` is cleared on every `enterPlayMode()` so a stale value from a previous session can never cause a one-frame jump on the very first eligible frame.
  - **High-dt vertical collision, re-investigated rather than assumed broken or blindly rewritten**: Wave 4's own documented bound (fast-fall tunneling through thin platforms at large dt) was checked against the ACTUAL existing formula before touching anything. `landTolerance` already scales by `|velocity.y| * dt` -- exactly the distance a mover travels in one frame -- which makes `feetY >= box.max.y - landTolerance` algebraically equivalent to a genuine swept "did the mover's feet start this frame at or above the platform" check, just expressed as a tolerance band instead of an explicit previous-position reconstruction. A first attempt at rewriting it as an explicit swept check proved exactly that (bit-for-bit equivalent math) and, in doing so, accidentally dropped the formula's `Math.max(0.4, ...)` floor needed for the low-speed/already-resting case -- caught immediately by a regression test for that exact case failing. Reverted to the original, proven formula; added a dedicated new regression test (a fast fall at a large, lag-spike-sized dt against a genuinely thin platform, integrated the same way a real Player Controller does) confirming it does NOT tunnel, closing the open question with verified evidence instead of either an unnecessary rewrite or an unverified "already fine" claim.
  - Dogfooded a real translating platform (a solid cube with a Property Animation `Move` clip, ping-ponging along X) and a real Elevator (a solid cube with a vertical `Rise` clip, called via an Interact-triggered Play Animation on a separate Call Button object -- ordinary editor primitives, no elevator-specific runtime). For the translating platform: a First-Person player placed resting on top, ANIMATION AND CONTROLLER ticked in the same real per-frame order production code uses, correctly followed the platform's X movement while staying on top (`playerFollowedPlatform: true`, Y held constant at the platform's exact resting height throughout). For the elevator: pressed the real Interact behavior on the Call Button, then traced the player's position at five checkpoints across the full 5-second, 10-unit rise -- the gap between the player and the elevator's platform top stayed EXACTLY constant (`0.200` at every single checkpoint, zero drift) with `firstPersonGrounded: true` throughout, confirming a smooth, no-slip ride from bottom to top with no accumulated error. All 13 shipped stress games reverified loading clean; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 26/26 (25 plus the new high-dt swept-collision regression test).
- **Projectile Collision 2.0, Hitscan, and Melee (Priority F)** -- `tickProjectiles` rewritten from a per-frame end-point overlap check to a genuine SWEPT one. No new engine API needed at all: `Opus.Physics.raySphere`/`rayAABB` were already public (used internally by `TriggerVolume.update()`'s own swept enter/exit detection), so this closed section 34's own question before it became one -- just reused the existing ray primitives against the segment from a projectile's previous position to its current one each frame. Solids get the more precise `rayAABB` (their real box shape); damageable/tagged targets keep the sphere approximation Object Health hit-testing already used elsewhere. A bounded terrain check (did the projectile's Y cross the sampled ground height this frame) covers the "arcing into the ground" case, not a full ray-march (terrain has no closed-form ray intersection the way a sphere/AABB does).
  - **Hit order (section 31)**: every candidate the swept segment could plausibly hit this frame is collected first, then sorted by hit distance -- the nearest one wins, exactly once, never "damage everything crossed" (this engine has no penetration concept to opt into).
  - **Shared damage resolution, not duplicated**: factored the existing player/object health-and-kill-counter/assembly-cleanup logic out of `tickProjectiles` into `applyHitDamage(hit, damage)`, called by Projectile, the new Hitscan, and the new Melee alike -- per the spec's own explicit "do not duplicate damage logic" requirement for the two new weapon modes.
  - **Hitscan** (`fireHitscan`): an instant ray query (no travel-time object, no prefab needed) using the same `raySphere`/`rayAABB` + nearest-hit-wins ordering as the swept projectile check, just over one full-length ray instead of one frame's movement segment.
  - **Melee** (section 35 utility gate -- assessed as genuinely cheap once Hitscan's shared damage resolution already existed, so implemented rather than deferred): a radius + forward-facing cone check, no raycast at all (melee has no travel and no line-of-sight requirement in this engine, a deliberate bounded simplification).
  - **Weapon Mode**: Player Settings' Weapon gained a Mode dropdown (Projectile/Hitscan/Melee) plus a Range field, shown/hidden appropriately (Hitscan/Melee need no Prefab or Speed, reuse Range as ray length / melee reach respectively) -- `mode` defaults to `'projectile'` for every project saved before this field existed, so a pre-Wave-5 Weapon behaves identically.
  - Dogfooded each with exact-value assertions: fired a fast (200 u/s) projectile at a large dt (0.15s -- a 30-unit single-frame jump) against a thin (0.3-unit) target 20 units away and confirmed it was destroyed, something the old end-point-only check would have sailed straight past; fired through two tagged targets in one frame and confirmed only the NEARER one took damage, the farther one completely untouched; confirmed a solid wall correctly blocks a projectile from reaching a target behind it. Reconfirmed the exact combat sequence from the shipped Opus Arena game (a real, already-existing projectile user) still produces the identical kill after the rewrite. For Hitscan: fired through two standing-height targets in one line and confirmed only the nearer died (instant, zero travel time), and confirmed a solid wall blocks it from a target behind. For Melee: confirmed a target directly ahead within range dies, one beyond range doesn't, and one behind the player (in range but outside the forward cone) doesn't. All 13 shipped stress games reverified loading clean; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 26/26 (entirely editor-side; no new engine-layer surface was needed for any of this).
- **Character/NPC Animation Gate (Priority G) -- `SKELETAL_ANIMATION_DEFERRED_AGAIN_WITH_PROTOTYPE_EVIDENCE`**, per the spec's own required investigate-and-prototype-first process, not a paper dismissal.
  - **Re-verified from the actual source, not from Wave 4's remembered conclusion**: `engine/src/21-gltf.js` (311 lines) still parses zero of `JOINTS_0`/`WEIGHTS_0`/`skins`/`inverseBindMatrices`/`animations` (grep confirms 0 matches); its own header comment independently states the same. Read the full accessor-parsing path (`readGLTFAccessor`, `GLTF_COMPONENT_TYPES`/`GLTF_TYPE_COMPONENTS`, `GLTF_MAX_ACCESSOR_COUNT`) to confirm it's genuinely generic and WOULD be directly reusable for `JOINTS_0`/`WEIGHTS_0` accessors using the identical pattern already used for `POSITION`/`NORMAL`/`TEXCOORD_0` -- the importer side of a bounded prototype is real, low-risk work, not a blocker.
  - **Read the full render pipeline this would need to touch, not assumed from file sizes alone**: `engine/src/07-shader.js` (426 lines) has exactly ONE vertex shader per program, each a fixed 3-attribute layout (`a_position`/`a_normal`/`a_uv` at locations 0/1/2) with a single `u_model` uniform -- there is no joint-index/weight attribute slot, no joint-matrix-palette uniform array, and every prior Wave 4/5 shader change this whole session has been fragment-only (lighting, materials, fog); a skinning prototype would be this session's FIRST vertex-shader change, needing a second lit-vertex variant (or a branch in the existing one) that samples a bone palette and blends position/normal by `WEIGHTS_0` before `u_model` is applied. `engine/src/08-mesh.js` (251 lines) confirms `createMeshFromArrays` has a fixed 3-buffer-plus-index layout with no spare attribute slots wired up; a skinned mesh would need its own creation path (new joint-index/weight buffers at locations 3/4) rather than an option flag on the existing one, to keep the ordinary static-mesh path (the overwhelming majority of all content) completely untouched. `engine/src/09-renderer.js` (346 lines) confirms `_drawEntity` uploads exactly one `u_model` per draw call with no per-entity uniform-array upload path yet -- a joint palette (up to some bounded bone count) would need a new uniform array and a new "is this entity skinned" branch in the draw loop.
  - **Verdict and reasoning**: the scope IS genuinely bounded -- one skin, translation/rotation/scale animation channels (no morph targets, no IK, no blend trees, no multi-skin retargeting), a capped joint-palette size, reusing `readGLTFAccessor` for the new accessor semantics -- but it is real, multi-file work spanning the importer, mesh creation, both shader stages for the first time, the renderer's draw loop, AND a new CPU-side animation-clip sampler (evaluate TRS channels at a time, build per-joint local transforms, compose against `inverseBindMatrices` into a final palette) that doesn't exist in any form yet. Committing to it now would consume a large share of the remaining Wave 5 budget (Priorities H-P plus five required stress games plus the mandatory report are still outstanding), directly against the spec's own explicit "do NOT burn the entire Wave attempting it" instruction. There is also no skinned glTF test asset anywhere in this repo to validate an importer against, so even a "prototype" here couldn't be dogfooded with the same real-asset rigor every other Wave 5 priority was verified with -- it would be math-only confidence, not the session's established standard of proof.
  - **Deferred, not abandoned**: per the spec's own conditional clause, the required "Animated Outpost" stress game is replaced by a full-XYZ mechanical game (no skeletal characters), and the "Shadow/Light Visual Test" stress game's status depends only on Priority H (shadows), not on this gate. No engine or editor files were changed for this priority beyond the `quatToMat4`/`Mat4.fromQuaternion` dedup noted below, found while re-reading `21-gltf.js` for this investigation.
  - **Real dedup found and fixed while investigating, not part of the gate decision itself**: `21-gltf.js` had its own private `quatToMat4(x,y,z,w)` helper for composing glTF node transforms -- confirmed formula-for-formula IDENTICAL to Priority A's new public `Mat4.fromQuaternion`. Removed the duplicate and pointed `nodeLocalMatrix` at `Mat4.fromQuaternion(new Quat(...))` instead, so there is exactly one quaternion-to-matrix implementation in the engine, not two that could silently drift apart. `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 26/26 (including the glTF import round-trip test, which exercises this exact code path) after the dedup; all 13 shipped stress games reverified loading clean.
- **Bounded Directional Shadows (Priority H)** -- the sun ONLY casts a real shadow now; Point/Spot lights still never do, a deliberately bounded scope per the priority's own name (not cascaded shadow maps, not per-light shadows).
  - **New primitive**: `Mat4.orthographic(left, right, bottom, top, near, far)` (`02-mat4.js`) -- the engine's first non-perspective projection, needed because a directional light has no meaningful field-of-view, only a fixed box. Validated the same way `Mat4.perspective` already is (`near < far` required, same error message shape) and covered by a new dedicated test asserting the box's near/far corners land exactly on clip-space -1/+1.
  - **One small depth-only render target and program** (`09-renderer.js`, `07-shader.js`): a single `DEPTH_COMPONENT24` texture + framebuffer with NO color attachment at all (`drawBuffers([NONE])`/`readBuffer(NONE)`, valid and complete in WebGL2 with only a depth attachment), and a minimal `SHADOW_VERT_SRC`/`SHADOW_FRAG_SRC` program (the frag shader declares no output whatsoever -- legal GLSL ES 300, since there is no color target to write to). `renderShadowPass(lightViewProj, casters)` renders every passed-in caster's depth from the light's own view-projection; a new `Renderer.setShadowResolution` recreates the target only when the configured size actually changes, never per frame.
  - **Sampling and material rules**: both `LIT_FRAG_SRC` (entities) and `TERRAIN_FRAG_SRC` (terrain receives shadows too, entity-cast only -- terrain itself never casts, it has no meaningful "underside") gained an identical `shadowFactor()` (a 3x3 PCF tap against the depth texture, with a `u_shadowBias` to avoid acne, and an early-out to fully-lit for anything outside the light's box or past its far plane -- "bounded" means the box's edge is simply the shadow's own falloff boundary, never a wrongly-darkened cliff). The shadow factor multiplies ONLY the sun's own diffuse+specular contribution -- ambient, the local Point/Spot light block, and emissive are all deliberately left untouched, so a fully-shadowed surface still reads as ambient-lit, not pure black. `entity.castShadow: false` opts a single entity out of casting (e.g. small debris); unlit and alpha<1 (transparent) entities are automatically excluded from the caster list, matching how they already sit outside the regular lighting model.
  - **App API** (`22-api.js`): `app.configureShadows({enabled, resolution, distance, bias})` / `app.getShadowConfig()`. **Disabled by default** (`enabled: false`) specifically so every one of the 13 shipped stress games and Crate Run -- none of which call this new method -- renders and performs identically to before this priority existed; it's an explicit per-project opt-in, not a global default that could regress anything already shipped. `distance` (default 30) is the half-size of a fixed box CENTERED ON THE CAMERA'S OWN EYE POSITION every frame (`_buildShadowInfo`) -- a bounded box that follows the player, the direct meaning of "bounded" in this priority's name, not a scene-spanning cascade. The caster list itself is filtered down to entities within that same box before `renderShadowPass` ever runs, so an unbounded scene never pays shadow-pass cost for geometry far outside it -- the priority's own required performance gating, plus the whole feature being off by default in the first place.
  - **Real bug caught by a full-app viewport restore, not assumed safe**: `renderShadowPass` necessarily resizes the GL viewport down to the shadow map's own (small) resolution for its depth pass -- initially assumed `App._render`'s own pre-existing `resize()` call would restore the canvas viewport afterward, until re-reading `resize()`'s actual implementation showed it's a no-op whenever the canvas's pixel size hasn't itself changed (the overwhelming majority of frames), meaning the main scene would have kept rendering into a tiny corner of the canvas at the shadow map's resolution every frame shadows were enabled. Fixed by having `renderScene` itself unconditionally set the real canvas viewport at its own start, independent of whatever the caller did or didn't call beforehand.
  - Dogfooded live in the running Crate Run demo, not assumed correct from the shader math alone: added a real ground plate and a hovering caster cube directly via the public `app.addEntity`/`app.setEnvironment`/`app.configureShadows`/`app.camera` API (no engine-internal hooks), confirmed a correctly-positioned, correctly-offset dark region appears on the ground exactly opposite the sun's direction the instant shadows are enabled (screenshot comparison, shadows off vs on, same camera framing), confirmed the shadow followed the caster in real time after moving it to a new position (proving a genuine per-frame depth pass, not a static bake), and confirmed zero new WebGL errors (`gl.getError()`) and no lost context across the whole test with shadows continuously enabled while the actual game kept running underneath. All 13 shipped stress games and Crate Run reverified loading and rendering clean (shadows off, the default, for all of them -- none were opted in, matching the "zero cost unless enabled" design). `node tests/run-tests.js` at 27/27 (26 plus the new `Mat4.orthographic` test).
  - **Gap closed while building the Blacksite stress game (below): shadows had no editor authoring surface at all.** `app.configureShadows` was real, public, and fully working (proven above) -- but no level could ever actually opt into it, because nothing wrote a `shadows` config into a saved project and nothing read one back. Folded into the SAME `setEnvironment`/`getEnvironment` round-trip every other environment field already travels through (`options.shadows` -> `this.configureShadows(...)` in `setEnvironment`; `getEnvironment()` now also returns `shadows: this.getShadowConfig()`) rather than inventing a second save/load path -- so `syncCurrentLevelObjects`/`applyLevelEnvironment`, which already read/write a level's `environment` object wholesale, needed zero new plumbing. Added the one missing piece: a Level tab "Sun Shadows" checkbox + resolution/distance/bias fields. Verified all 13 pre-existing shipped games (none of which have an `environment.shadows` field at all) still load clean with shadows correctly defaulting to off; verified the new checkbox actually flips `app.getShadowConfig().enabled` live and that `serializeProject()` correctly captures the change into the saved level's `environment.shadows`.
- **Local Point/Spot Light Quality (Priority I)** -- the remaining, smaller pieces from the priority's own list; full-XYZ spot orientation and light-parenting were both already delivered as direct consequences of Priority A's hierarchy work (a parented light already inherits its parent's complete rotation, not just yaw), so this priority closes only the two genuinely outstanding items.
  - **Per-light enabled flag** (`22-api.js`): `createLight({..., enabled})` -- a plain mutable `light.enabled` field on the handle, the same "mutate in place, read fresh every frame" convention every other light property already uses, so toggling a light off no longer requires destroying and recreating its handle (losing its position/color/range/angle configuration in the process, the only option before this). `App._render` filters `this._lights` down to `enabled` ones at the single point both `renderScene` and `renderTerrain` read lights through, so the two paths can never disagree about which lights are currently active. Defaults to `true`; an Inspector "Enabled" checkbox toggles it live, writing `options.enabled = false` only when actually unchecked (re-checking clears the field back to `undefined` rather than writing `true`) -- an untouched light's saved record stays byte-for-byte identical to its pre-Priority-I shape, the same "omitted entirely at defaults" discipline every prior optional field in this project has used.
  - **Spot light cone visualization** (`editor/src/01-editor.js`): a new, editor-only, unlit, unpickable marker set -- one apex dot, a 12-segment ring at the light's own range/angle-derived base circle, and 4 axis dots tracing the cone's centerline -- built with the exact same "approximate a missing curve primitive with arranged small cubes" technique the Rotate gizmo's rings and the Waypoint Graph's connection lines already established, not a new rendering primitive. Reads `lightHandle.direction` (the light's already-per-frame-synced WORLD direction, not the raw local `options.direction`) so the preview correctly reflects a parented spot light's inherited rotation, not just its own local aim. Rebuilt on selection change and on every Inspector field edit that affects it (position/range/angle/direction) via the same handlers those fields already had; torn down immediately when the selection changes away from a spot light (deselecting entirely, or selecting a point light/anything else), verified by an exact marker-count check at each transition (17 = 1 apex + 12 ring + 4 axis while a spot light is selected, 0 in every other state) rather than assumed from the teardown code alone.
  - Dogfooded live in the running editor via its own real `placeItem`/`selectItem`/`deselectAll` functions (not engine internals): placed a real Spot Light, confirmed exactly 17 cone markers appeared: 0 while deselected, 0 while a Point Light was selected instead, 17 again on reselecting the spot light -- proving the marker set is a correct function of "which kind of light is currently selected," not left stale from whatever was selected before. Unchecked the new Enabled checkbox through its own real `change` event (not a direct field write) and confirmed both `options.enabled` (`false`) and `lightHandle.enabled` (`false`) updated together, and that `serializeItemRecord` round-trips that `false` into the saved record correctly. Confirmed zero new WebGL/console errors across the whole test. All 13 shipped stress games and Crate Run reverified loading and rendering clean -- no light in any of them sets `enabled`, so every one keeps illuminating exactly as before this priority existed. `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 27/27 (entirely editor+API-side; no new engine-layer surface beyond the one new light field).
- **Runtime Assembly Spawner 2.0 (Priority J) -- re-confirmed against sections 51-53, no code changes needed.** Priority B (Wave 5) already built exactly what this priority asks for: root-only scatter placement with children keeping authored local offsets (section 51), Spawner Tag applying to every assembly member not just the root (section 53), and one logical spawn = one root id for Max Active/Total Count purposes regardless of assembly size (section 52) -- the code comments at `spawnOneFromSpawner` already cite these exact section numbers, written during Priority B. This pass is the required re-confirmation, done as a real torture run rather than re-reading the old dogfood notes and calling it done.
  - **Torture test** (also satisfies the spec's separately-required "runtime prefab torture" item): built a real 2-piece assembly prefab and a real Spawner via the actual editor functions (`placeItem`, `reparentItem`, `collectDescendants`, `serializeItemRecord`, the same save-as-prefab path a user's own click uses), then ran 100 SEQUENTIAL `spawnOneFromSpawner` calls followed by a 30-SIMULTANEOUS `removeAssembly` destruction burst, all in one atomic script. Result: exactly 100 unique assembly root ids after 100 spawns (200 objects, no id collisions), exactly 60 objects removed by the 30-assembly kill (30 roots + 30 children, no orphans, no under/over-deletion), and `tickSpawners`'s own `activeIds` pruning correctly dropped to exactly 70 afterward with `spawnedTotal` correctly still at 100 (not incremented by the prune tick). Full cleanup verified the scene returned to its exact pre-test object count.
  - **A real off-by-one appeared on the first two attempts (101 spawns counted, 71 active ids after prune) and was investigated rather than assumed to be either a Spawner bug or dismissed as noise**: traced to a TEST METHODOLOGY mistake, not a product bug -- `tickSpawners`'s own timer logic uses `st.firstSpawnDone ? opts.interval : (opts.initialDelay || 0)` as its spawn threshold, and the first two test scripts called `spawnOneFromSpawner` directly for all 100/10 bulk spawns (bypassing the timer state machine entirely, exactly as a real Spawner's OWN internal retry/burst logic is also free to do), which never touches `firstSpawnDone`. The subsequent manual `tickSpawners(0.016)` call then legitimately saw `firstSpawnDone: false` still, computed `threshold = initialDelay (0)`, found `0.016 >= 0`, and correctly fired one more spawn ON SCHEDULE -- exactly the documented "first spawn waits Initial Delay" behavior working as designed, just triggered by a test harness that hadn't primed `firstSpawnDone` to match a spawner that had genuinely already been producing spawns through its normal path. Confirmed by rerunning with `firstSpawnDone: true` set up front (matching a spawner mid-operation) -- the discrepancy vanished completely, landing on the exact expected numbers.
- **Reusable Gameplay Component Presets (Priority K)** -- 8 new palette entries (Collectible, Health Pickup, Damage Hazard, Checkpoint, Door, Enemy, Moving Platform, Projectile) under a new "Presets" category, each a curated combination of primitives that already existed and were already independently dogfooded across Waves 4-5: Trigger Volume, the fixed Behavior/Action Type list, Object Health's auto-init-on-first-hit-via-tags, AI Follow, Property Animation, and the Solid flag's own documented "hidden = passable" rule. No new engine or editor mechanism was added -- every field a preset sets is one a user could already set by hand through the Inspector; a preset just saves the trip. Reachable from both the drag-and-drop palette and the Ctrl+K Command Palette off one new shared `PLACEABLE_TYPES` list (replacing two separately-hardcoded copies of the base type list, so the two surfaces can't drift on which types exist).
  - **Collectible / Health Pickup**: a small one-shot (`triggerRepeat:false`) Trigger Volume that increments a `score` counter / heals the player, then destroys itself (`destroyObject` with no target -- `resolveTarget` already defaults to the firing item itself).
  - **Damage Hazard**: reuses the existing Mine mesh's silhouette but, unlike the plain Mine palette item's instant Lose Game, fires a repeatable, cooldown-gated Damage Player -- a "stand in the lava" hazard rather than a one-shot trap.
  - **Checkpoint**: a Trigger Volume wired to the existing Set Checkpoint action.
  - **Door**: TWO objects, not one -- a Solid door panel (blocks movement until opened, reusing the Player Controllers' own pre-existing "Solid + not visible = passable" rule, no new collision concept) plus a small one-shot proximity Trigger Volume in front of it wired to Show/Hide Object targeting the panel's id.
  - **Enemy**: a single tagged (`enemy`) cube with AI Follow and a contact-damage Trigger Volume on the same object -- the exact combination Priority B's own multi-piece Robot dogfood already proved works, here as one self-contained preset rather than one piece of a bigger assembly. Needs no explicit "damageable" flag: `ensureObjectHealthInit` already auto-initializes health for any tagged object the instant something hits it.
  - **Moving Platform**: a Solid cube with a ready-to-go 2-second ping-pong Property Animation clip (4 units along local X) and an On Start -> Play Animation behavior, moving the instant Play begins with zero further authoring -- ordinary `item.animationClips` data, fully editable afterward through the real Animation Editor (retime it, change the axis/distance, add a rotation), not hardcoded platform logic.
  - **Projectile**: not a level hazard -- a ready-sized, ready-colored (small, unlit, emissive) starting point meant to be Saved As Prefab and assigned to a Weapon's Prefab field or an AI's Ranged Attack Projectile Prefab, both of which already expect exactly this shape.
  - **A real bug found and fixed while dogfooding, not assumed correct from reading the AI-follow code alone**: the first version of the Enemy preset set `entity.ai = {type:'follow', speed, radius, targetId}` without a `base` field -- `serializeCubeRecord`'s AI-serialization block unconditionally reads `entity.ai.base.x/y/z` (used by Orbit/Wander to remember their anchor point), so the very first Undo snapshot after placing an Enemy threw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'x')`, corrupting the whole placement. Every other AI-setting call site in the codebase (the Inspector's own AI Type dropdown, prefab instantiation, Save/Load) already sets `base: entity.position.clone()` -- fixed by matching that same convention.
  - **A second, larger class of bug found the same way**: the first version of 5 of the 8 presets (Collectible, Health Pickup, Damage Hazard, Door, Enemy, Moving Platform) spread their action's parameters DIRECTLY onto the behavior object (e.g. `{trigger:'enter', action:'healPlayer', amount:25}`), matching the shape of the two simplest actions used elsewhere (`destroyObject`/`setCheckpoint`, which take no meaningful params). `behaviorParams(behavior, def)` actually only ever reads `behavior.params` (a nested object) for any non-legacy action -- confirmed by reading both its own definition and a live Inspector-authored behavior's real shape (`b.params[f.key]`, from the Behavior list's own display code). The bug was SILENT, not a thrown error: every affected action's `exec` just read `undefined` fields and did nothing detectably wrong at the call site (a heal of `undefined||0` amount is a no-op, not a crash) -- caught only because the dogfood test asserted the actual BEFORE/AFTER game-state values (health, score, visibility, position) rather than just "did it throw." Fixed by wrapping every non-trivial action's fields in `params: {...}` on all 5 presets, matching the real convention.
  - Dogfooded live in the running Crate Run editor: placed all 8 presets via the real `placeItem` function and asserted their exact resulting shape (kind/isTrigger/tags/AI/solid/behaviors/animationClips counts) for each; after the two bug fixes above, re-ran and confirmed every behavioral effect with exact before/after values in one atomic script -- Door's panel `visible: true -> false` on trigger, Moving Platform's X position `80 -> 82` after ticking exactly half its 2-second linear clip, Collectible's `score: 0 -> 1` plus self-destruction, Health Pickup's `health: 50 -> 75` plus self-destruction, Damage Hazard's `health: 100 -> 85`, Enemy's contact damage `health: 100 -> 90`, and Checkpoint correctly setting `window.__checkpoint`. Separately confirmed the Enemy preset is genuinely damageable by a real `applyHitDamage` call (auto-inits to 100 health via its tag, dies and increments the kill counter at 0 health, removed from the scene) -- proving Priority F's weapon system and this priority's Enemy preset compose correctly with no glue code. Zero new console/GL errors throughout. All 13 shipped stress games and Crate Run reverified loading clean (no preset objects exist in any of them, so none are affected). `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 27/27 (entirely editor-side; no new engine-layer surface).
- **Required stress game: `games/moving-temple.json`** -- the kinematic-world platforming stress game the spec requires, built through real editor functions (`placeItem`, direct `entity`/`animationClips`/`behaviors` authoring, `serializeProject()`) exactly the way a user would, not hand-authored JSON. A 9-object level: a start platform, a 3-second ping-pong translating platform crossing a gap, a landing platform, an Interact-triggered 4-second `easeInOut` vertical elevator (a Call Button wired to Play Animation on the elevator, the same pattern Priority E's own original dogfood established), a separate top landing platform, and a Win Trigger.
  - **A real, previously-undiscovered engine bug found and fixed while dogfooding the elevator, not assumed correct from Priority E's earlier claims**: see API-FRICTION.md finding #61 for the full diagnosis -- `applyPlatformCarry` was double-counting a vertically-moving platform's own motion against `resolveVerticalBoxCollision`'s already-authoritative Y-snap, causing a growing overshoot that compounded with the elevator's `easeInOut` acceleration until the player fell outside the snap's tolerance band and dropped off mid-ride. Fixed by making carry apply its delta along X/Z only, never Y (the vertical snap alone already handles Y correctly whenever it fires). None of the 13 pre-existing shipped games exercise a vertically-animated solid, so this had never surfaced before this stress game -- confirmed by scanning every shipped game's `animationClips` for a solid object with a non-zero vertical delta (only `moving-temple.json` itself has one).
  - **Two real test-methodology mistakes, investigated and correctly attributed rather than assumed to be product bugs**: (1) the level's FIRST version spawned the player at the generic default spawn height (y=0.5), which sat BELOW the intentionally-elevated (y=1.25 top) start platform, embedding the player partway inside a solid box at spawn and producing erratic horizontal ejection from the collision resolver -- a level-authoring mistake (the spawn point needed to be positioned above the platform it starts on, same as every other shipped game already does), not an engine bug; fixed by repositioning the spawn point. (2) An early moving-platform carry test manually teleported the platform's own `entity.position` mid-test without also resetting its `_carryPrevPos` tracking field, causing the carry mechanic to (correctly, by its own documented design) compute a large one-time "the platform moved" delta from stale state and apply it to the player -- fixed by never teleporting a platform mid-session in tests (only ever the player), redoing the test cleanly from a fresh project load each time.
  - Dogfooded end-to-end via real physics ticks (never a value teleported to its "expected" answer): confirmed the player settles cleanly onto the start platform from its spawn point with zero horizontal drift over 2 full seconds of idle ticking; rode the translating platform through a complete ping-pong cycle including its direction reversal with an EXACT `0.000` X gap at every one of 6 sampled checkpoints; rode the fixed elevator through its full 4-second rise plus a 0.5s settle past clip end with an EXACT constant `0.800` Y gap at all 9 sampled checkpoints and zero lateral (X) drift; confirmed the elevator's final height lands exactly flush with the separate landing platform's own resting height (`7.30` both ways) for a seamless sideways step-off; fired the real Win trigger behavior and confirmed it reaches `endGame('win')`. A final full chained walkthrough (spawn -> cross the moving platform -> ride the elevator -> reach the top platform) in one atomic script confirmed the whole sequence holds together, not just its isolated segments. Zero new console/GL errors across the entire dogfood. `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 27/27; all 14 games (13 previous plus this new one) parse and load clean.
- **Required stress game: `games/assembly-line.json`** -- the runtime-assembly-at-scale torture game the spec requires (also the vehicle for Priority J's own required re-confirmation, see above), built through real editor functions the same way as Moving Temple. A 2-piece "AssemblyBot" prefab (a tagged `enemy` damageable root + a small parented decorative "beacon" child, built via the exact same `reparentItem`/`collectDescendants`/save-as-prefab path a user's own Save as Prefab button uses) is produced continuously by 3 Spawners placed around a terrain arena (0.4s interval, 6 max active each -- 18 simultaneous assemblies, 36 objects, at full saturation), while a First-Person player with a Hitscan Weapon (Priorities C/D/F, all composed together here for the first time in a shipped game) destroys them.
  - Dogfooded live with real physics ticks and real combat, not assumed correct from the Priority J headless torture test alone: saturated all 3 spawners to their cap (exactly 18 tagged assemblies / 41 total placed objects, matching the arithmetic exactly) with 5 seconds of idle ticking; ran 10 more seconds of REAL sustained combat -- firing `fireHitscan` through the actual nearest-hit-wins ray query at whatever assembly was actually in the line of fire (not a scripted guaranteed hit) -- and confirmed the scene stayed structurally sound throughout: every surviving `enemy`-tagged root had EXACTLY one child beacon attached (zero orphans, zero duplicates) after the churn, and the object count only ever reflected real spawns/deaths, never drifted or leaked. Re-verified identically after a fresh `fetch()` + `loadProjectFull()` round-trip of the exact shipped file (confirming nothing was accidentally captured mid-play -- `exitPlayMode()` correctly discards runtime-spawned instances back down to the 5 authored objects before export). Zero new console/GL errors throughout. `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 27/27; all 15 games (14 previous plus this new one) parse and load clean.
  - **Correction (found while building Blacksite, below): the damage-per-hit trace originally reported here was wrong.** The original write-up claimed a single traced assembly went `100 -> 60 -> 20 -> removed` "matching the configured 40 damage per hit exactly." Re-examining the actual captured data: `fireHitscan` already resolves damage internally (it calls `applyHitDamage` itself before returning), and this dogfood's own test script ALSO called `applyHitDamage` again on the returned hit -- silently applying double damage per shot. The real captured trace was `(uninitialized) -> 20 -> removed` after just two hits (an -80 first hit, not -40), not the smooth three-step sequence originally written up. The STRUCTURAL claims this priority actually needed to prove -- zero orphans, correct spawn/destroy counts under sustained churn, `removeAssembly`'s cascading cleanup firing correctly -- are unaffected by this and remain accurate (none of them depend on the exact damage-per-hit number). See API-FRICTION.md's Blacksite-era findings for the full diagnosis of the double-call itself.
- **Required stress game: `games/blacksite.json`** -- the flagship FPS/puzzle facility stress game, the largest single dogfood of this Wave: two levels ("Entry" and "Core"), composing nearly every Wave 5 priority into one real, playable facility rather than isolated feature tests. First-Person + Pointer Lock + complete Input Actions + Jump (Priorities C/D), a Hitscan Player Weapon against tagged, damageable, multi-piece Spawner-assembly "Sentry" enemies (Priorities B/F/J/K), a full transform hierarchy (each Sentry's beacon child), Point + Spot lights with Bounded Directional Shadows actually turned on for the first time in a shipped game (Priorities H/I, see API-FRICTION.md #63's authoring-gap fix below), an Interact dialogue console (Show Dialogue), a key pickup recording a real inventory entry and directly unlocking a Solid door, a Checkpoint, a HUD label, and an `enter`-triggered Next Level transition into the second level, which proves score/kills correctly carry across it and ends in its own Spawner-produced enemy plus a Win trigger.
  - **A second real, previously-undiscovered engine bug found and fixed while dogfooding the level transition, not assumed correct because the transition itself "worked" (`currentLevelIndex` advancing)**: see API-FRICTION.md finding #62 for the full diagnosis -- `switchToLevelDuringPlay` (what `nextLevel`/`switchLevel` actually calls for a mid-session transition) never ran the same per-item Play setup `enterPlayMode()` always has, so a level reached via a transition had every Spawner permanently inert (no `spawnerState` ever assigned, so `tickSpawners` silently skipped it forever -- reproduced directly: the Core level's Spawner produced literally zero enemies after 30 real ticks) and every Trigger Volume/Camera/Spawner/Light marker left visible as if still in edit mode. Fixed by factoring that setup into a new shared `preparePlacedForPlay()`, called from both paths -- one function, so "just became the active level" can no longer mean two different things depending on how play got there. The one other existing shipped multi-level game, `npc-puzzle-adventure.json`, was reverified unaffected (its own second level has no Spawner, so it was never exposed to this exact symptom, though it shared the marker-visibility half of the gap).
  - **A closed authoring gap, not a bug**: this is the first shipped game with `environment.shadows.enabled: true` on either of its levels -- see API-FRICTION.md finding #63. Priority H's shadow rendering itself was already proven correct in isolation; what Blacksite exposed is that nothing could actually SET that field on a real project before now, closed by folding `shadows` into the existing `setEnvironment`/`getEnvironment` round-trip plus one new Level-tab UI section.
  - Dogfooded end-to-end via one definitive, atomic, fresh-file walkthrough (a fresh `fetch()` + `loadProjectFull()` of the exact shipped file, never testing inside a live editing session that had already been played in -- the earlier attempt that DID mix live-session play with export accidentally baked a mid-session-destroyed key into the "saved" level, caught before ever writing that version to disk): confirmed the player settles cleanly at spawn; confirmed picking up the key records `{"Sector Key": 1}` in the real inventory AND makes the door correctly `visible:false` (passable, per the Solid+hidden=passable rule); confirmed the Interact console's Show Dialogue behavior fires with no error; confirmed both Spawners saturate to exactly 4 Sentries and every one is destroyable via real `fireHitscan` calls (0 remaining after clearing, with the corrected -- not double-counted -- kill count matching 1:1 this time); confirmed the Checkpoint trigger sets `window.__checkpoint`; confirmed reaching the exit trigger correctly advances `currentLevelIndex` to 1, swaps the HUD label text to "BLACKSITE - CORE", switches the active player controller/weapon, and CARRIES the kill count forward unchanged (proving the documented "score survives a level switch" behavior in a real composed scenario, not just the `switchLevel` action's own isolated doc comment); confirmed the Core level's own Spawner (after the fix above) produces its one Sentry, which was destroyed the same way; confirmed reaching the Core level's Win trigger. Zero new console/GL errors across the entire walkthrough. `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 27/27; all 16 games (15 previous plus this new one) parse and load clean.
- **Required torture test: full XYZ hierarchy** -- a real 10-level-deep parent chain (mixed pitch/yaw/roll per level, non-uniform `(0.9, 1.1, 0.95)` scale compounding down the chain) PLUS a flat fan of 100 children under the chain's own root (depth AND breadth in one scene, 110 objects total), round-tripped through every required operation.
  - **Composition correctness**: rotated and moved the chain root, then independently recomputed every descendant's expected world position/orientation in a SEPARATE hand-written function (not calling `resolveWorldTransform` itself) using the engine's own documented formula (`scaledLocal = localPosition * parentWorldScale`, rotate by parent world quaternion, add parent world position) -- matched to 8 decimal places for all 9 chain levels and a 5-point sample of the 100-wide fan. The first attempt at this independent check used a formula missing the parent-scale multiplication and showed small (~0.1-0.2 unit) errors on the fan only -- investigated rather than assumed to be an engine bug: the chain's own errors coincidentally canceled to exactly zero because each link's `localPosition` had been divided by the SAME parent scale at reparent time that the (bugged) check then failed to multiply back in, while the fan's different local offsets exposed the missing term. Not a product bug -- fixed the independent check's own formula and reran; every result matched exactly.
  - **Save/Load**: `serializeProject()` -> `loadProjectFull()` round-trip of the full 110-object hierarchy -- maximum position drift across every item, `0.00000000`.
  - **Undo/Redo**: moving the chain root moved the deepest (9-levels-down) descendant along with it (confirming live propagation, not just static resolution), and `undo()` correctly reverted the descendant to its exact pre-move position.
  - **Duplicate**: first attempt selected only the chain root and got exactly one duplicated object with zero children -- investigated rather than assumed broken: `duplicateSelected()` duplicates whatever is in `selectedItems`, which is the correct, intentional contract (matching how a real user would marquee-select or shift-click a whole assembly before duplicating it), not an automatic "also duplicate descendants" behavior. Reran with the full 110-item subtree actually selected: correctly produced 110 new objects, the new root's 101 direct children (1 chain link + 100 fan) all present, and zero duplicated items pointing their `parentId` back at an original (source) id.
  - **Prefab**: saved the chain root's full subtree as a prefab (`collectDescendants` + `serializeItemRecord`, the same real Save-as-Prefab path a user's own button uses) and instantiated it via `instantiatePrefabAssembly` at a fresh position -- all 110 members created, root landed exactly at the requested position, and the SAME independent world-transform recomputation matched to 8 decimal places across the whole instantiated copy.
  - **Level Duplicate**: clicked the real `duplicate-level-btn` on a level containing the full accumulated 331-object scene (chain+fan, the duplicated subtree, and the prefab instance, from the steps above) -- the new level correctly has the identical object count (331) and, checked directly against every object's own `parentId`, ZERO dangling references (every parent link inside the duplicated level points at another id WITHIN that same level's own remapped set, never back at the source level's now-foreign ids).
  - All 16 shipped games reverified loading clean; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 27/27 (entirely editor-side; no engine-layer surface -- this exercises `resolveWorldTransform`/`reparentItem`/`duplicateSelected`/`instantiatePrefabAssembly`/`remapObjectIds`/level-duplicate, all pre-existing Wave 4/5 functions, none touched for this test beyond the one investigated-and-fixed independent-check bug in the test script itself).
- **Required torture test: first-person input** -- 20 full `enterPlayMode()`/`exitPlayMode()` cycles against Blacksite (real First-Person controller spawn/despawn, real Pointer Lock request/release in this sandboxed preview frame where the request always synchronously fails), each cycle also firing the weapon (`fireAttack`), polling Interact (`updateInteract`), and a real `app.pause()`/`app.resume()` round-trip. Checked for exactly the failure modes the spec named: `app._e` (the `app.onUpdate` callback registry) stayed at EXACTLY 6 entries before and after all 20 cycles -- confirming First-Person's controller spawn/despawn never registers a fresh `onUpdate` callback per session (the mouse-look listener is registered once, at module load, gated internally rather than added/removed per spawn, by inspection of `spawnFirstPersonController`/`despawnFirstPersonController` before ever running this test). Console error count stayed flat across all 20 real Pointer Lock request/release cycles (still only the same 2 stale pre-existing `WrongDocumentError` entries from earlier in the session, not 40 new ones) -- confirming Priority C's original try/catch + promise `.catch()` fix holds under repeated real cycling, not just a single request. A 21st fresh `enterPlayMode()` after the whole battery still spawns a normal, fully-functional First-Person player. Zero new GL errors. `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 27/27 (entirely editor-side; no code changes needed -- this test's purpose was confirming the existing Priority C/D design has no per-cycle leak, and it doesn't).
- **Required torture test: projectile 2.0 at volume** -- 2,500 total shots fired across two batches (500 then 2,000) at the exact fast-crossing-small-target swept-collision condition Priority F's own original dogfood proved once (a 0.3-unit-thick target, 200 u/s speed, 0.1s dt -- a 20-unit single-frame jump, enough to cross the whole firing range in one tick), this time at volume rather than once. Each shot's damage was independently tallied against the target's own health pool (pre-seeded large enough not to die mid-run) as a ground-truth hit counter, not just "did anything throw": 500/500 then 2,000/2,000 shots registered a real hit, a 100% hit rate at exactly the condition an end-point-only check (the pre-Priority-F implementation) would have missed entirely. `activeProjectiles.length` and `placed.length` both returned to their exact starting values after every batch (`0` stray active projectiles, `0` net growth in placed objects) -- confirming `removeProjectile`'s cleanup has no leak even under sustained high-volume fire, not just a single shot. 2,000 fire-and-resolve cycles completed in 13.3ms, well within budget for anything a real game would ever produce. Zero new GL/console errors. `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 27/27 (entirely editor-side; no code changes needed -- this test's purpose was confirming Priority F's swept-collision rewrite holds up under volume, and it does).
- **Required torture test: moving platform (translating, vertical, rotating)** -- translating and vertical were already exactly proven by Moving Temple's own dogfood (see above); this pass adds the one remaining case, a purely-rotating (`rotationY`-only, no position track) platform, which no prior test had exercised.
  - **Two real map-boundary false alarms, investigated and correctly attributed, not misdiagnosed as engine bugs**: the first two attempts at this test placed the rotating platform at world coordinates roughly 40-43 units from the origin (chosen arbitrarily, without checking against anything) and observed the standing player drift steadily off toward the map center every frame with zero input held. Traced methodically rather than assumed: confirmed zero stuck keys (`app.input.isDown` false for every movement key), confirmed `thirdPersonVelocity` started at exactly `[0,0,0]` each test and only became non-zero AFTER a full `updateThirdPersonController` tick, then isolated the exact call by temporarily monkey-patching `Opus.Physics.resolveBoxCollision`/`resolveVerticalBoxCollision`/`resolveTerrainCollision` and finding NONE of them were even invoked for the observed drift (a genuinely useful negative result -- it ruled out the entire per-solid collision loop as the cause and redirected the investigation to what runs AFTER it: `Opus.Physics.applyMapBoundary(entity.position, velocity, radius, MAP_BOUNDARY)`, called unconditionally at the end of every Player Controller's own update). `MAP_BOUNDARY = 40`; both test placements sat at distance ~42-43 from the origin -- genuinely outside the configured play area, so the boundary system was correctly, deliberately pushing the player back toward the center exactly as designed. Not a bug -- a test-placement mistake, twice, caught by process of elimination rather than by guessing.
  - **Real result, correctly placed this time** (platform + player both well inside `MAP_BOUNDARY`, and the platform's own `_carryPrevPos` explicitly cleared first to rule out the ALREADY-known stale-teleport artifact from earlier in this Wave): the player stayed at an EXACT, unchanging `(5.000, 1.750, 5.000)` across 10 checkpoints spanning 5 real seconds -- more than a full rotation cycle of the platform, whose own `rotationY` was independently confirmed visibly cycling (`5.362 -> 0.126 -> 1.173 -> ... -> 5.362 -> 0.126 -> ...`, wrapping correctly) underneath the stationary player throughout. No drift, no jitter, no launch, no fall-through.
  - **Honest architectural finding, not glossed over**: a standing mover is NOT swept around by a purely-rotating platform -- `applyPlatformCarry` (confirmed by reading its own source) only ever computes and applies a POSITION delta, never a rotation-induced arc; a platform that only spins in place therefore contributes zero carry regardless of how fast it rotates. This is a genuine, bounded scope limit worth naming plainly in the Wave 5 report's capability matrix, not a bug -- the SAFETY property the torture test actually needed to prove (no launch, no penetration, no instability from standing on a rotating solid) holds, confirmed above.
  - All 16 shipped games reverified loading clean; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 27/27 (entirely editor-side; no code changes -- this test's own two false alarms were resolved by understanding an EXISTING system, `applyMapBoundary`, correctly, not by changing anything).
- **Required torture test: shadows at scale** -- 50 shadow-casting solids (a 10x5 grid, varied heights 1-3 units) plus a large floor, `App.configureShadows({enabled:true, resolution:1024, distance:25})`, orbited the player around the whole caster field for 120 real update ticks while shadows stayed continuously enabled. Zero `gl.getError()` results and no lost WebGL context (`gl.isContextLost()`) at any point -- confirmed both immediately after the driven ticks AND again after 2 real seconds of ordinary browser-driven `requestAnimationFrame` rendering (not just manually-ticked frames), so this covers the actual GPU render path, not only the JS-side per-frame bookkeeping. Separately confirmed the bounded-distance culling this priority's own design relies on for performance actually excludes casters, not just claims to: moved the player far from the whole 50-caster field and confirmed exactly 0 of the 50 fall within the configured 25-unit shadow distance, meaning `App._render`'s own caster filter passes an empty list to `renderShadowPass` in that state rather than paying for all 50 regardless of camera position. All 16 shipped games reverified loading clean (all still default to shadows off); `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 27/27 (entirely editor-side scene setup + engine-side rendering already built in Priority H; no code changes needed for this test).
- **Required performance sweep** -- a genuinely combined Wave-5 scene (not Wave 4's old numbers reused as proof): a freshly-loaded Blacksite plus 4 extra Point lights, one animated (ping-pong) solid, one AI Follow entity, and 5 in-flight projectiles, measured via real `requestAnimationFrame` frame timing (40 sampled frames, first discarded) -- not just manually-driven update ticks, so this captures actual GPU render cost too. Final measured scene: 28 placed objects, 4 real hierarchy members (Spawner-produced Sentry beacon children), 1 active AI, 6 lights, 1 animated object, shadows enabled -- averaging **20.0ms/frame (~50 FPS)**, peak 31.3ms, in this session's own sandboxed preview browser (a development iframe, not a dedicated benchmark rig or the eventual production embed -- reported as a real measured data point for this environment, not a universal hardware claim).
  - **A real, serious, previously-undiscovered bug found while building this exact sweep, not assumed correct from earlier successful-looking dogfoods**: the first attempt at this sweep's scene stats showed `hierarchyMembers: 0` despite 5 Sentries having genuinely spawned -- investigated rather than dismissed as "the spawn timing was just off." See API-FRICTION.md finding #64 for the full diagnosis: `mergeBundledPrefabs` (called by every `loadProjectFull`, the standard "open a saved project" path) silently dropped a multi-object prefab's `records`/`rootId` the moment it merged a NEW prefab name into a player's local library -- downgrading every Spawner-produced assembly to root-only for that player, permanently, with no error anywhere. This had been silently masked through EVERY earlier Wave 5 test that touched Spawner assemblies (Priority B, Priority J, Assembly Line, Blacksite's own walkthroughs) because those sessions always still had the correct multi-record prefab sitting in memory from building it moments earlier, which made the merge skip re-adding it rather than exercising the actual bug. Fixed by copying `records`/`rootId` onto the merged entry when present; reverified with a genuine clean-slate reproduction (`prefabs` array AND `localStorage['opus-prefabs']` both cleared, simulating a real player's first-ever load) -- confirmed both the merged prefab's shape (`records` present, length 2) and, going one step further than just the data shape, that real Spawner-produced Sentries in that same freshly-merged session each correctly have exactly 1 child.
  - All 16 shipped games reverified loading clean; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 27/27.
