- **Required schema migration confirmation**: `PROJECT_FORMAT_VERSION` stays at `3`, unchanged since Wave 3 -- confirmed rather than assumed correct, by loading the OLDEST project shape `normalizeProjectData` still accepts (a bare array of records, predating levels/prefabs/environment/playerSettings/inputActionMap entirely) through the real `loadProjectFull` path and checking every Wave 5 addition individually: a plain `rotationY`-only cube correctly derives a pure-Y quaternion on the fly (`[0, 0.3429, 0]` for `rotationY: 0.7`, matching `sin(0.35)` exactly) with no separate migration step; a light record with no `enabled` field correctly defaults to `true`; a project with no `environment.shadows` at all correctly defaults to `{enabled:false, resolution:1024, distance:30, bias:0.0025}`; an `inputActionMap`-free project correctly fills in every Wave 5 action (`moveForward`/`moveBack`/`moveLeft`/`moveRight`/`jump`) from `INPUT_ACTION_DEFAULTS`; a controller with no `weapon` field at all leaves `currentPlayerSettings.weapon` genuinely absent rather than force-populating a default object (matching the "omit entirely at defaults" discipline), and `fireAttack()`'s own `if (!weapon || !weapon.enabled) return;` guard confirmed by reading the source to have no crash risk against that absence. No version bump needed -- every Wave 5 addition follows the same "derive/default on the fly, never a separate conversion pass" discipline every prior wave's additions already established, and this test is the direct proof that discipline actually held for Wave 5's own fields too, not just a repeated claim.

### Capability Expansion Wave 4 (closed out — see CAPABILITY-WAVE-4-REPORT.md)
- **Spawn Point object** -- a real, first-class placeable (palette: World tab, 🚩), not a hand-positioned workaround. Same `kind:'cube'` + marker-flag pattern already established for Waypoint/Trigger Volume (`isSpawnPoint`), so it inherits free drag, the full Move/Rotate/Scale gizmo, Duplicate, stable id, Save/Load, Undo/Redo, and prefab-capability with zero new machinery. A level may hold several; exactly one is ever the designated `Default Player Spawn` (a `★ Set as Default Player Spawn` inspector button, exclusive like Start Level's own ★). The first Spawn Point placed in a level auto-becomes default so a level is never left with zero; deleting the current default auto-promotes whichever Spawn Point remains (same reasoning as Start Level never being allowed to point at nothing); duplicating one is duplicate-safe (a copy is never auto-default, so duplicate-then-forget-to-check can't silently create two "defaults"). Hidden during Play, editor-visible marker otherwise, same as every other editor-only object type.
- **`computeSpawnPoint()` generalized spawn resolution** -- previously a single fixed formula that assumed every game starts on an island (terrain-center XZ, terrain height + water height, nothing else); Skyway Trial's platformer stress game in Wave 3 exposed the gap and had to be hand-authored around it (API-FRICTION.md #51, "flagged, not fixed" at the time). Rewritten as an explicit priority chain, generalized rather than special-cased further: (1) an explicit default Spawn Point, if the level has one -- always wins outright; (2) a solid authored platform/ground under the fallback XZ, via a new `findSolidGroundTopAt(x, z)` helper (highest solid cube whose axis-aligned XZ footprint covers the point); (3) terrain-derived height at that XZ (the original sole behavior, unchanged); (4) a fixed safe default for a level with no Spawn Point, no solid ground, and no terrain at all. This closes #51 for real: a Start Platform no longer needs to be hand-positioned at exactly world Y=0 to spawn correctly.
  - Dogfooded all four priorities directly with exact-value assertions, not just "does it look right": an explicit default Spawn Point at (10, 0.5, 10) produced a player spawn at bit-for-bit (10, 1.05, 10); a solid platform at Y=3 with no Spawn Point produced exactly `platformTop(3.5) + clearance(0.55) = 4.05`; a terrain-only level matched `sampleHeight()` exactly; an empty level (no Spawn Point/solid/terrain/water at all) fell through to the safe default. Also verified end-to-end: gizmo eligibility (Move/Rotate/Scale all true for a selected Spawn Point), hidden during Play but visible in edit, Undo/Redo across duplicate, a full `serializeScene()`/`loadScene()` round-trip preserving both Spawn Points with exactly one default, `serializeProject()`'s embedded record shape, and Duplicate Level correctly cloning both Spawn Points with exactly one default in the copy. Zero console errors throughout; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (entirely editor-side; `computeSpawnPoint`/`findSolidGroundTopAt` have no engine-layer surface).
- **Real transform hierarchy (parent/child)** -- current Grouping was editor organization only (never affected transforms); this is a genuine scene graph. `cube`/`camera`/`spawner`/`emitter` items can now be parented to any other `cube`/`camera`/`spawner` item (`emitter` can be a child -- its position follows a moving/rotating parent -- but isn't itself a meaningful parent, since it has no independent rotation/scale to inherit; terrain/water are deliberately excluded entirely, a large-environment-piece use case this feature isn't targeting). A parented item stores a LOCAL transform (`parentId` + `localPosition`/`localRotationY`/`localScale`, plain data -- ids, not object references, so project JSON stays acyclic even though the live hierarchy has real depth) and its `entity.position`/`rotationY`/`scale` are kept as the item's always-current WORLD transform, recomputed via `world = parentWorld × local` every frame (`updateHierarchyTransforms()`/`resolveWorldTransform()`, parent-before-children with per-tick memoization) and immediately after any hierarchy edit. This is the one deliberate architectural choice the whole feature rests on: since every existing consumer in this codebase -- the renderer, physics/collision, trigger volumes, AI, Change Camera -- already reads `entity.position` directly as world space, none of them needed to change or learn about parenting at all. There is only ever one position field, and it is always world space, so rendering and physics/triggers cannot disagree about where a child actually is.
  - **Reparenting preserves World Transform exactly** -- parenting or unparenting an object never visually moves it. `reparentItem()` resolves both objects' current world transforms first, then solves for the local transform that reproduces the child's exact current world position/rotation/scale under the new parent (rotating/scaling the position delta by the parent's inverse transform); unparenting is the trivial inverse, since `entity.*` already holds the correct world value at all times -- clearing `parentId` alone is a complete, correct, non-jumping unparent with no math needed. **Cycle rejection**: `isDescendantOf()` walks a candidate parent's own subtree before any reparent is allowed, rejecting `A → B → C → A` outright with a clear status message rather than silently corrupting the hierarchy or infinite-looping resolution.
  - **Editor UI**: a `Parent` dropdown in the Inspector (shared across every hierarchy-capable kind, one code path rather than duplicated per kind), listing every valid target (self and descendants excluded, so the UI can't offer a choice `reparentItem` would reject anyway) plus `None (root object)` for unparenting. The Scene panel is now a real collapsible tree -- parent-before-children ordering, depth indentation, an expand/collapse caret on any item with children, visibility toggle unchanged. Searching the tree flattens to a plain matched list (a nested match is never hidden by collapsed ancestors) rather than attempting to show ancestor chains above each hit -- simpler, and a search hit by name was never the case this needed to solve. Deliberately deferred: drag-to-reparent directly in the Scene list (the dropdown covers the actual requirement; drag-and-drop reparenting is a pure UX nicety layered on top, not attempted this pass to keep the surface bounded).
  - Dogfooded with exact hand-verified math, not just "looks right": a 3-level chain (root, a 90°-rotated middle parent offset (2,0,0), a leaf offset (1,0,0) from the middle) produced world positions matching hand-calculated rotation composition bit-for-bit at every level. A particle emitter parented under a cube and offset (0,0,-1) correctly swung to (0,0,1) when the parent rotated 180° and followed exactly when the parent translated -- proving the exact "Left Engine → Particle Emitter" case this feature targets, with zero emitter-specific code (the emitter just has a position, handled by the same generic local-node accessor as everything else). Reparenting a translated/rotated/scaled object reproduced its exact pre-parenting world position/rotation/scale (bit-for-bit `worldBefore === worldAfterImmediate`), continued following the parent correctly after a subsequent parent move, and stayed exactly in place on unparent. Cycle rejection confirmed directly (`A→B→C`, then `A→C` correctly refused, `A` left unparented). A Camera object parented and rotated with its parent was confirmed to feed a live, correct world position into `applyCameraPreset` (which already read `entity.position` directly) with no changes to that function at all -- the "world-space systems must operate on actual world transforms" requirement satisfied automatically by construction, not by auditing and patching each consumer individually. A 100-object stress case (a 10-level-deep chain plus 90 fanned-out leaves) built in 2.3ms, serialized in 0.5ms, and round-tripped through a fresh `loadScene()` in 2.1ms with every parent/child relationship and world position intact. Duplicating a parent+child pair together correctly re-links the child copy to the NEW parent copy (not the original) via one id-remap pass shared with the existing group-id remap logic; duplicating a single child alone correctly keeps it pointing at the original shared parent. Full Undo/Redo verified across reparent/unparent (a stale pre-`loadScene` JS object reference was the first, false, "Undo doesn't work" read on this -- corrected by re-fetching from `placed` after each undo/redo call, the same discipline this whole session already established). All 9 shipped stress games and Crate Run (21 crates, 7 hazards, hovercraft spawn) reverified regression-clean after this change; zero console errors throughout; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (hierarchy is entirely editor-side -- the engine's `Entity`/physics/renderer have no new surface and needed none).
- **Prefabs 2.0: multi-object prefabs** -- a single object was the only unit Save as Prefab ever understood; it now walks the live hierarchy (`collectDescendants`, reusing transform hierarchy above) so saving the ROOT of an assembly (a Door's frame with a hinged Door child, a Turret's Base with Head/Barrel/Muzzle-Emitter children) captures every part as one reusable prefab in the exact same gesture as before -- no separate "save as assembly" workflow to learn. A single, childless object still saves under the old single-`record` shape completely unchanged (every prefab ever saved keeps working, byte-for-byte); an object with descendants gets a new `records` array (one entry per object, same shape `serializeScene()` already produces for each kind, factored out into a shared `serializeItemRecord()`) plus `record` set to just the root piece, so every EXISTING single-object call site (Spawner object, Spawn Prefab action/graph node) keeps working unmodified against a multi-object prefab too -- deliberately spawning only the root piece rather than the whole assembly, since runtime spawning of a whole moving assembly is a real but separate future extension, not something this pass's dogfood required.
  - **One shared ID-remapping mechanism, not a new one for prefabs** -- per the explicit "do not write independent ad-hoc remapping logic for every subsystem" requirement, prefab instantiation (`instantiatePrefabAssembly`) reuses `remapObjectIds()` verbatim, the exact same whole-JSON quoted-string-substitution function Duplicate Level already built and has used all along for its own "every object gets a fresh id, every internal reference follows it" requirement. This means `parentId` (brand new this pass), AI `targetId`, and every `ACTION_TYPES`/`GRAPH_NODE_TYPES` field of type `target`/`cameraPreset` all get remapped correctly with zero new field-specific code -- the mechanism doesn't know or care what kind of reference a matched id string represents, only that it matches something in the id map. External references (an id NOT in the saved set) are left untouched automatically, since the string-substitution only ever touches ids that appear in the map.
  - Placing an instance rigidly translates every record in the assembly by the same delta (new position minus the saved root's position), so the whole group keeps its exact authored relative layout; a new `instantiateItemFromRecord()` (factored out of `loadScene`'s inline camera/spawner/emitter construction, incidentally deduplicating that code too) is shared by both scene loading and prefab placement.
  - Dogfooded with a real 5-object "Enemy Turret" (Base [solid] → AimTarget marker + Head [AI Face Target → AimTarget] → Barrel → Muzzle particle emitter, a 3-level-deep hierarchy with an internal AI reference crossing sibling branches, not just parent-child): saved as one prefab from a single click on Base, placed two independent copies from a clean scene, and confirmed directly -- both copies' Heads target their OWN copy's AimTarget (not the original, not the other copy's), every id across all 10 placed objects is unique, moving one copy's Base rigidly translates its own Muzzle emitter by the exact same delta while the OTHER copy is completely unaffected (bit-for-bit unchanged position), and a fresh `serializeProject()`/`loadProjectFull()` round-trip preserves all 10 objects with every internal reference still resolving to a real object. All 9 shipped stress games and Crate Run reverified regression-clean afterward; zero console errors; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (entirely editor-side).
- **Terrain sculpting** -- an editable heightfield layer on top of the existing procedural terrain, not a replacement for it. Engine addition: `opts.heightEdits = { segments, deltas }`, a dense per-vertex delta grid at the terrain's own render resolution, bilinearly interpolated (`sampleHeightEdit`) and folded directly into `islandHeight(x, z, opts)` itself -- the ONE function every consumer in the engine already funnels through (render mesh generation, `resolveTerrainCollision`/grounding, `sampleTerrainNormal`, and the public `Opus.Terrain.sampleHeight`/`sampleNormal` query API: 6 call sites total, confirmed by grep before touching any of them). Folding the edit into that single shared function, rather than patching each of the 6 call sites individually, is what guarantees rendering and physics can't disagree about a sculpted terrain's shape -- there was no other way to accidentally wire up a visual-only deformation even by mistake. Absent `heightEdits` (every terrain that existed before this pass), the lookup is a hard no-op returning exactly 0 -- zero behavior change for any existing terrain or shipped game.
  - **Editor**: four bounded brushes (Raise / Lower / Flatten / Smooth) with Radius/Strength, a Sculpt Mode toggle in the Terrain inspector that takes over the viewport's existing Pointer-Events pipeline while active (mouse/pen/touch all drive the identical code path, no separate touch tool). Flatten converges every point under the brush toward the height at the FIRST dab of the stroke; Smooth pulls each vertex partway toward its 4 grid neighbors' current (procedural + already-edited) height. **Gesture-level undo**: exactly one `pushUndo()` per stroke (on the first pointerdown), not one per `pointermove` -- confirmed by dispatching a real pointerdown + 10 pointermoves + pointerup through the actual DOM handlers and checking the undo stack grew by exactly 1. A live stroke regenerates the terrain (`app.removeTerrain`/`app.createTerrain`, the exact same remove+recreate pair the pre-existing Seed/Size/Radius regenerate-on-edit fields already used and proved, factored out into one shared `regenerateTerrain()`) on every dab, so the brush is visibly live, not a preview-then-commit two-step. A `Reset Sculpting to Procedural` button drops `heightEdits` entirely, returning to the pure seed-generated shape.
  - Dogfooded with exact-value assertions at every step, not just visual inspection: Raise added exactly the expected per-dab delta (`strength × falloff × 0.12`) across 3 successive dabs; Smooth measurably reduced the height difference between a sculpted peak and its immediate neighbor (0.264 → 0.184) without being told the exact target; Flatten converged a point toward the stroke's reference height. **The critical consistency check**: called `Opus.Physics.resolveTerrainCollision` directly (the real physics primitive third-person/hovercraft grounding is built on) against a sculpted terrain and confirmed its returned `terrainHeight` matches `handle.sampleHeight()` at that exact point bit-for-bit -- proof that collision, not just the query API, reads the sculpted heightfield, closing the "visual-only deformation" failure mode requirement 19 explicitly warned about. A full `serializeProject()`/`loadProjectFull()` round-trip reproduced the sculpted height bit-for-bit (heightEdits rides through the existing generic terrain-options serialization for free, no new save-path code needed). All 9 shipped stress games and Crate Run reverified regression-clean; zero console errors; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23.
- **Lighting 2.0: placeable Point/Spot lights** -- on top of the existing single directional sun, not a replacement for it. New public `App.createLight(options)`/`removeLight(handle)`, a bounded set (`App.MAX_LIGHTS = 8`) of simple range-attenuated lights (NOT physically-based -- a clamped, squared falloff, additive into the same diffuse term the sun already contributes to). Both the entity (`LIT_FRAG_SRC`) and terrain (`TERRAIN_FRAG_SRC`) fragment shaders gained an identical bounded light loop (`u_numLights`/`u_lightPos[8]`/etc, one shared `_uploadLights()` packer in the renderer feeding both programs so they can't drift out of sync with each other). Exceeding 8 simultaneous lights doesn't silently cap with no explanation: the renderer only uploads the first 8 (insertion order), and the FIRST time a scene crosses that line, one `console.warn` fires (not one per light per frame, which would flood the console every subsequent draw) -- confirmed by placing 12 lights and seeing exactly one warning, reading "9 lights placed, but only the first 8 are rendered simultaneously."
  - **Editor**: Point Light / Spot Light palette entries (Lighting category). Same two-part shape as Camera/Spawner (a small editor-visible marker entity for selection/drag/hierarchy, hidden during Play) but with one real difference: unlike Camera/Spawner, a Light's underlying effect must keep working during Play, so `app.createLight`'s handle is tracked completely separately from the marker and synced from it every frame (position only -- a spot's cone direction is a deliberately independent authored vector, same reasoning Camera objects already keep their own orientation concept distinct from cube `rotationY`, since this engine has no pitch/tilt for cubes to derive a cone direction from). Inspector: Color, Intensity, Range, and for Spot, Cone Angle (degrees, converted to/from radians) and Direction (X/Y/Z). Lights are hierarchy-capable (parentable, so a light can ride a moving/rotating rig) and prefab-capable for free, via the exact same generic non-cube serialize/instantiate path built for Prefabs 2.0 -- no light-specific code needed in either system.
  - Dogfooded visually, not just via return values, since a WebGL shader bug can compile clean and still be wrong: placed a near-black cube and a real point light beside it -- screenshot confirmed the cube visibly glowing the exact light color. Placed a spot light above a floor -- screenshot confirmed a colored, circularly-falling-off cone landing on the floor, distinct from the point light's spherical falloff. Also verified end-to-end: inspector field edits reach both `options` and the live `lightHandle` simultaneously; the marker hides on Play while `lightHandle` keeps its properties; a light parented under a cube followed both the parent's move (confirmed exact position match after a real frame tick -- an initial same-tick read was a stale-reference test artifact, not a bug, per this session's established re-fetch-after-async discipline) and round-tripped correctly through `serializeProject()`/`loadProjectFull()` with its `parentId` intact; Duplicate produces an independent `lightHandle` (confirmed by reference inequality), and Undo/Redo correctly add/remove it. All 9 shipped games and Crate Run (visually unchanged -- 0 lights placed means the shader's light loop never executes, byte-for-byte identical output to before this feature existed) reverified regression-clean; zero console errors including shader compile/link (checked directly in a real WebGL2 context, not just Node's syntax check, which has no GL context to compile against at all); `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23.
  - **Shadows: `SHADOWS_DEFERRED_WITH_REASON`.** Even a single bounded directional shadow map is a materially larger lift than Point/Spot lights were (a full depth-only render pass, light-space projection matrices, a shadow map texture, and bias/PCF tuning to avoid acne/peter-panning) -- genuinely useful, but placeable lights themselves deliver more real game-builder value per unit of risk this pass, per the spec's own explicit priority ("Lighting objects are more important than shadows"). Not attempted this pass; a real candidate for a future wave once the rest of Lighting/Materials has been dogfooded in real games.
- **Materials 2.0** -- four small, high-value additions to the existing base-texture/color model, still not a second material system: **Unlit** (skips ambient/diffuse/spec/local-lights entirely, flat color -- for a UI-like look), **Transparency/Alpha** (0-1, feeding a new separate render pass), **Emissive** (a fixed self-illumination color+intensity added on top of the lit-or-unlit result, independent of any light -- useful for terminals/pickups/sci-fi props with no light object needed nearby), and **texture tiling/offset** (`uvScale`/`uvOffset`, a straight UV transform before sampling). All four are plain new `Entity` fields (`.unlit`/`.alpha`/`.emissiveColor`/`.emissiveIntensity`/`.uvScale`/`.uvOffset`), mutable in place like `.color`/`.texture` already were -- no separate material object.
  - **Transparency rendering**: a bounded, explicitly-not-order-independent strategy, exactly per spec ("Use a reasonable draw ordering strategy... do not build order-independent transparency"). `renderScene` now splits visible entities into opaque and transparent lists; opaque draws first with depth-write on, completely unchanged from before; transparent entities draw AFTER, depth-write OFF (so two overlapping transparent objects don't fight over depth-buffer ownership) and back-to-front sorted by distance from the camera eye -- the standard bounded approximation, correct for the common cases this targets (a window, a force field, a ghost object), not guaranteed correct for deeply overlapping transparent geometry.
  - **Editor**: a Material section in the cube inspector (Unlit checkbox, Transparency slider, Emissive color+intensity) plus Texture Tiling/Offset fields that appear once a texture is applied. Every field is omitted from a cube's serialized record entirely at its default value, so an untouched cube's saved JSON is byte-for-byte identical to before this feature existed -- confirmed directly by serializing a freshly-placed default cube and checking none of the six new keys are present at all (not even as `false`/`0`).
  - Dogfooded visually with all three non-trivial effects in one screenshot: a magenta Unlit cube showing flat, gradient-free color; a semi-transparent blue "glass" cube with a green panel clearly visible through it (confirming real alpha blending, not just a lower opacity number being stored); a near-black cube with Emissive set showing bright, saturated self-illumination with no light nearby. Verified the full Inspector→entity→Save/Load round-trip for Unlit/Alpha/Emissive Intensity with exact-value assertions. All 9 shipped games and Crate Run reverified regression-clean (visually unchanged -- every existing entity's material fields default to exactly the pre-feature rendering behavior); zero console/shader errors; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23.
- **Property Animation** -- generalizes the existing single-shot Tween actions into reusable, multi-keyframe, editor-authored clips, distinct from and additive to Tweens (both keep working; a clip is "author several keyframes once, replay by name," a Tween stays "one ad-hoc move"). A clip (`item.animationClips`, plain serializable data -- keyframe values are numbers/arrays, never live Vec3 references) has up to four tracks -- Position, Rotation Y, Scale, Visibility -- each a time-sorted keyframe list interpolated with `easeValue()`, the exact same easing curve Tweens already use, so a clip segment looks and feels identical to a Move To (Tween), not a second animation feel to learn. Visibility is a step function (last keyframe at or before the current time), not interpolated. Playback (`once`/`loop`/`pingpong`) is driven by `updateAnimations(dt)`, registered as an unconditional `app.onUpdate` callback (same reasoning as the transform-hierarchy resolver) -- which means "respect pause" and "use simulation time" both come for free with zero animation-specific pause-checking code, since the engine's own update loop already gates every registered `onUpdate` callback behind `!app.paused` (API-FRICTION.md #54). New shared actions -- **Play Animation** (by clip name), **Stop Animation** (resets to the start), **Restart Animation**, **Pause Animation** (stops but keeps its current position) -- reach the Node Graph automatically through the existing `ACTION_TYPES` → `GRAPH_NODE_TYPES` derivation, no new wiring needed there.
  - **Editor: Animation Editor modal**, deliberately a scrollable list of track cards, not a Dope-Sheet timeline -- the same "not a node canvas" call the Dialogue Editor already made for the same reason (a clip's data is small; a full timeline UI would be far more surface than authoring a handful of keyframes needs). Values are never hand-typed into a keyframe: **Add Keyframe** always captures the object's CURRENT live transform at an authored time, upserting (replacing) any existing keyframe at that exact time -- position the object in the viewport, hit Add, matching how most simple keyframe tools actually work, and sidestepping a large grid of editable per-axis number inputs. A **Preview** button plays the in-progress (not-yet-saved) clip live in the editor itself via the same `updateAnimations` path Play Animation uses at runtime, stopping automatically when the modal closes. The cube inspector gained an "Animation Clips" list (Add/Edit/Delete) feeding the modal.
  - Dogfooded a real "DoorOpen" clip (Position 0.5→3 over 1.5s, Rotation 0°→90°, Visibility true→false, all built through the actual UI -- clicking Add Animation Clip, filling the toolbar fields, moving the live object and clicking Add Keyframe, never hand-authoring track data) with exact-value assertions throughout: linear interpolation landed exactly at the hand-calculated midpoint (y=1.75, exactly between 0.5 and 3) and exactly at the final keyframe on completion, with playback correctly stopping (`once`); switching to `loop` correctly wrapped back to the start after exactly one full duration without stopping, and continued interpolating correctly into the second cycle; `pingpong` correctly reached the far keyframe then interpolated back toward the start; Stop correctly reset elapsed time to 0, Restart replayed from 0, and Pause correctly stopped WITHOUT resetting elapsed (confirmed distinct from Stop). Rotation and Visibility tracks verified together on the same clip: rotation interpolated linearly (45° at the exact midpoint) while visibility correctly held its step value until crossing its own keyframe. Preview verified live in the editor and confirmed to stop cleanly on modal close. A full `serializeProject()`/`loadProjectFull()` round-trip preserved all three track types with their exact keyframe counts and values. All 9 shipped games and Crate Run reverified regression-clean; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (property animation is entirely editor-side -- no engine-layer surface, same category as Tweens/Timers before it).
  - One dev-testing-methodology snag, not a product bug: `editor/index.html` itself (unlike `01-editor.js`, which already had dev-time cache-busting) was silently served stale by this session's dev server/browser combination after this pass added new modal markup to it, producing a `getElementById(...).addEventListener` crash on newly-added elements that genuinely existed in the file on disk. Resolved the same way `01-editor.js` already does -- a cache-busting query string (`?v=N`) on navigation -- not a fix to the shipped product, since a real deployment's hosting/cache-control config is a separate concern from this local dev loop.
- **glTF skeletal animation: `SKELETAL_ANIMATION_DEFERRED_WITH_TECHNICAL_REASON`.** Assessed honestly rather than assumed: the current glTF importer (`engine/src/21-gltf.js`) parses zero skinning data today -- no `JOINTS_0`/`WEIGHTS_0` accessors, no `skins`, no `inverseBindMatrices`, no `animations` channels at all (confirmed by grep before writing this verdict, not by memory). A bounded implementation would need, at minimum: a real skin/joint/animation-channel parser (a second substantial addition to the existing geometry-only importer), a NEW vertex attribute layout (joint indices + weights) that every mesh-creation path in the renderer would need to branch on, GPU vertex-shader skinning (a joint-matrix-palette uniform array, sampled per-vertex -- unlike this wave's Lighting/Materials work, which only ever touched FRAGMENT shaders, this would be the session's first VERTEX shader change, a meaningfully higher-risk surface since it affects the actual geometry pipeline every mesh flows through, not just per-pixel color), and a hierarchical bone-transform composer that is NOT the same mechanism as this wave's own object transform hierarchy (bones operate on vertex data via GPU skinning; placed objects operate on `Entity.position/rotationY/scale` -- genuinely two different systems, not reusable from each other). Given zero existing groundwork and a disproportionately larger renderer-stability risk than everything else built this wave combined, this is deferred rather than attempted at reduced rigor. Does not block anything else in Wave 4 -- Property Animation (above) already covers the "opening door / rotating turret / floating collectible" class of dogfood this spec asked for without needing a skeleton at all.
- **Projectiles & Combat** -- Health, Damage, and AI already existed; this unlocks actual action gameplay on top of them with one implementation shared by every source that can fire, per the explicit "do not create three separate projectile implementations" requirement. **Object Health**: generalizes the existing Player-only `{current, max}` shape to any placed object (`item.health`, lazily initialized like Player health already was) -- **Damage Object** destroys the object outright at 0 (no separate "Destroy on Zero" action needed), **Heal Object**, and Node Graph conditions **Object Health At Least/At Most** (an explicit Object Reference field, not an implicit "the object this graph is on" -- `evaluateConditionNode` has no item-context parameter to read that from, and every other object reference in this engine is already an explicit dropdown, not implicit). This closes the exact gap Survival Arena's Hunter prefab had to work around with a generic Counter + a hand-wired "Health At Most(0) → Lose" graph.
  - **Projectiles**: a bounded system (no ballistic solver, no continuous-collision sweep -- a per-frame sphere-distance check against solids/Player/tagged targets, the same bounded spirit API-FRICTION.md already documents for terrain/platform collision) built entirely on existing primitives -- a projectile prefab instantiates through the exact same `instantiateCubeRecord`/`addPlaced`/`wireItemTrigger` sequence Spawn Prefab and Spawner already use, so a fired bolt is a completely ordinary placed object (can carry its own behaviors, be a Trigger Volume, anything else this editor already supports). **Fire Projectile** (shared action: prefab, direction mode [object forward / toward Player / toward an Object Reference], speed, damage, lifetime, radius, owner/target tags to avoid self-hits) is the ONE implementation three different sources all call into: **Player Attack Input** (`KeyQ` default -- Space is Boost/Jump, F is Interact already; reaches touch via a new on-screen ⚔ button and gamepad via the existing `GAMEPAD_KEY_MAP` remapping layer with zero gamepad-specific code, the same reasoning the Wave 3 Gamepad Gate re-confirmation already established for Jump), a new per-level **Weapon** config in Player Settings (enabled/prefab/cooldown/damage/speed -- one reusable primitive, not an inventory of weapons, per spec), and **Enemy Ranged Attack** (a new `attackEnabled`/`attackPrefab`/`attackRadius`/`attackCooldown`/`attackDamage` field group ADDITIVE to whichever AI movement type is already chosen -- Follow, Face Target, Orbit, etc. -- rather than a separate AI type, since "chase and shoot" is the actual common case). Applied proactively this pass, not discovered missing later: every new `ai.*` field was added to `serializeCubeRecord`'s explicit whitelist AS it was introduced, the exact lesson finding #48 (Follow Object's dropped `targetId`) already taught once.
  - Dogfooded a complete attacker/target loop with exact-value assertions: built a real "Bolt" projectile prefab through the actual Save-as-Prefab UI, gave the Player a Weapon (25 damage, 20 speed), and fired at a tagged Enemy with real Object Health -- each shot landed exactly 25 damage (100→75→50→25→destroyed at 0), confirmed the projectile item itself is cleanly removed from `placed` on every hit (no leaked scene objects), and confirmed cooldown correctly blocks a same-instant re-fire. Separately built a Turret with Enemy Ranged Attack enabled (Face Target + attack, proving the two compose) and confirmed it correctly detects the Player in range, respects its own cooldown, and repeatedly fires projectiles that damage `window.__gameState.health` down to exactly 0 over a real multi-second simulated engagement. Verified the real AI inspector checkbox+fields author these properties correctly and that they round-trip through `serializeCubeRecord()` intact. **One test-methodology trap hit and diagnosed, not a product bug**: splitting a fire-then-tick projectile test across separate tool-call round-trips let real wall-clock time pass, during which this session's own already-documented background-render-loop ticking advanced the SAME projectile a second, uncontrolled time -- symptoms (a projectile vanishing after exactly one manual tick) initially looked like a collision bug. Resolved by re-running the test atomically in one script (fire + tick + assert, no round-trip in between), the same discipline this session has already established for Node Graph Delay and Spawner tests. All 9 shipped games and Crate Run reverified regression-clean; zero console errors; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (entirely editor-side; the engine has no new surface).
- **Waypoint Graph pathfinding** -- Patrol Route (visits an ORDERED authored list, unchanged) is joined by a real navigation layer: Waypoints can now connect to each other (`item.connections`, an array of other Waypoints' stable ids -- plain id strings, so it rides through the exact same `remapObjectIds()` whole-JSON remapping mechanism Prefabs/Duplicate Level already use, no connections-specific remap code needed), and a new **Navigate To Target** AI mode finds the shortest CONNECTED route via standard A* (`findWaypointPath`, straight-line-distance heuristic -- admissible, since it never overestimates true connected-graph cost) rather than either a fixed list or a single straight line. Deliberately bounded, per spec: no navmesh, no dynamic obstacle avoidance beyond "the graph already routes around whatever the author connected it around."
  - **Editor**: a Waypoint's inspector gained a Connections list (existing connections with a remove button, plus a dropdown + Add Connection button for a new one) -- bidirectional by default (both edges added together in one click; one-way connections weren't worth the extra authoring UI this pass, per the spec's own "only if easy" gate on that specific sub-feature). Connections render as real 3D lines in the viewport (thin stretched cubes between each pair, reusing the existing cube mesh/entity pipeline rather than building a new GL_LINES draw path for exactly one consumer), continuously synced so they follow a dragged Waypoint live, and hidden during Play the same way every other editor-only marker already is. Deleting a Waypoint cleans up the reverse-direction connection on whatever it was linked to, not just its own side.
  - **Navigate To Target's own tick logic** recomputes the path on a timer (0.75s, not every frame -- A* over a real graph isn't free) while still following the graph, then switches to direct steering for the final unguided stretch from the last waypoint to the target's live position, matching how every other AI type already moves (direct steering, no avoidance) once off the graph. **Found and fixed a real jitter bug during dogfooding**: recomputing on the normal timer even AFTER reaching the end of the route made the AI briefly steer back toward "the nearest waypoint to wherever it currently was" (now off-graph) before continuing outward -- a visible back-and-forth wobble with no benefit, caught by tracing the AI's actual position every few frames rather than only checking whether it eventually arrived. Fixed by suppressing recompute entirely while off-graph (`offGraph` gate), a one-line, low-risk change; re-traced afterward and confirmed a perfectly smooth approach with zero oscillation, landing within 0.02 units of the target.
  - Dogfooded the actual required test, not open terrain: built a real maze -- a solid Wall directly between an AI's start position and the Player's, with a 3-Waypoint chain (connected via the real Add Connection UI in one of the verification passes, built directly for the larger trace in another) routing AROUND it to one side. Confirmed `findWaypointPath` returns the exact expected route through the chain, and returns `null` for a genuinely disconnected Waypoint (not just "assumed to fail gracefully"). Traced the AI's position every 1.2 simulated seconds across a full run: it never came within 3 units of x=0 (the wall's location) while crossing the wall's z-range -- direct proof it took the connected route around the wall, not a straight line through it -- and it reached the target's exact position (0.02 units away) once past the graph. Verified the real Add-Connection/Remove-Connection UI produces correct bidirectional edges and a real visible line entity, and that connections survive a fresh `serializeProject()`/`loadProjectFull()` round-trip (`findWaypointPath` re-run against the reloaded objects returned the identical route). All 9 shipped games and Crate Run reverified regression-clean; zero console errors; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (entirely editor-side).
- **Input Actions (Interact/Attack)** -- a deliberately bounded rebind layer, not a full input-remapping platform. `input.wasPressed(inputActionMap.NAME)` replaces a hardcoded key code at the exact two call sites that were ALREADY dispatched from one central place (this file's own gameplay update loop) rather than embedded inside a specific Player Controller's own movement math -- Interact and Attack. Movement (WASD) and Jump/Boost (Space) stay hardcoded this pass: rebinding those would mean threading a config lookup through three separate controllers' physics update functions, real regression risk for a part of the input surface that was never the actual friction point (nobody asks to rebind "forward"; Interact/Attack are what a real game author plausibly wants to move off F/Q) -- a bounded, disclosed scope rather than a silent gap.
  - **Editor**: a new Input Actions section in the Project tab lists both actions with their current key and a rebind button -- click it, press any key, done; guarded against capturing a keystroke meant for a focused text field. **Gamepad stays in sync with zero gamepad-specific rebind code**: `pollGamepad()`'s face buttons 1/2 now read `inputActionMap.interact`/`.attack` at poll time instead of a fixed code, so a rebind takes effect on a connected pad automatically (button 0 / Boost-Jump stays fixed Space, matching the movement-stays-hardcoded scope above). The map round-trips through `serializeProject()`/`loadProjectFull()` as ordinary project data (`inputActionMap`), defaulting to `{interact: KeyF, attack: KeyQ}` for every project saved before this feature existed -- confirmed directly against all 9 shipped games, none of which carry a saved map.
  - Dogfooded end-to-end through the real UI, not by mutating the config directly: clicked Rebind, dispatched a real `KeyE` keydown, and confirmed the button updates and the map changes. Then proved the REBIND actually takes effect, not just the stored value: simulated the OLD key (F) via `app.input.simulateKeyDown` against a real Interact-behavior NPC and confirmed nothing fired; simulated the NEW key (E) and confirmed the message toast fired. Simulated a connected gamepad with face button 1 held and confirmed `pollGamepad()` correctly drove the REBOUND key (`KeyE`), not the old default. Verified a full `serializeProject()`/`loadProjectFull()` round-trip preserves a custom rebind (reset to defaults in memory first, specifically to prove the reload -- not a stale in-memory value -- restored it). All 9 shipped games and Crate Run reverified regression-clean; zero console errors; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (entirely editor-side).
- **Pause Menu** -- the pause overlay was previously a static, non-interactive "⏸ Paused" message; it now has real Resume / Restart Level / Main Menu buttons. Reuses the existing pause plumbing entirely (no second "is input blocked" concept, same reasoning the Start Screen and Dialogue already followed) -- Resume just calls `app.resume()`, and Restart Level/Main Menu both call the exact same `switchLevelWithTransition()` function the Restart Level/Switch Level ACTIONS already use, so a Pause Menu click and a Node-Graph-triggered restart behave identically, not two implementations. Main Menu returns to the Start Level and re-shows its Start Screen (if configured) -- a small game's practical equivalent of "return to menu" without building a second screen-routing concept, per the explicit "do not build full app routing" bound.
  - **Found and fixed a real, previously-invisible bug while dogfooding**: the Start Screen and Dialogue already reused `App.pause()`/`onPauseChange` (correct, proven design), but the pause overlay's own visibility toggle only ever checked `playMode && paused` -- with no awareness that OTHER things pause the game too. Before this pass, that meant the pause overlay was *also* marked `.visible` underneath the Start Screen for the entire time a fresh session's Start Screen was up, invisible only by accident (a lower z-index, and zero interactive content to notice). Now that the overlay has real, clickable buttons, that latent double-overlay state became an actual bug, not a cosmetic one, caught immediately by checking DOM state directly (`pauseOverlayEl.classList.contains('visible')`) rather than trusting a screenshot. Fixed by gating the pause overlay's visibility on the Start Screen and Dialogue overlays both being hidden, confirmed safe by the ordering already used everywhere `app.pause()` is called (the calling overlay's own `.visible` class is always added BEFORE `app.pause()` fires, so `onPauseChange`'s synchronous callback sees the correct state).
  - Dogfooded the full Main Menu path against a real 2-level game (`npc-puzzle-adventure.json`): advanced to the second level ("Inner Sanctum"), paused, clicked Main Menu, and confirmed the level switched back to "Village" (the actual Start Level) with its Start Screen showing -- and, critically, confirmed the pause overlay stayed hidden the whole time (`pauseVisibleAfterMenu: false`), closing the bug found above. Separately confirmed a genuine user-initiated pause (the Pause button, nothing else showing) still correctly displays the overlay. Restart Level verified by moving the player far from spawn, pausing, clicking Restart, and confirming both the pause state cleared AND the player's position reset to spawn. All 9 shipped games and Crate Run reverified regression-clean; zero console errors; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (entirely editor-side).
- **Project Settings / Game Quality Options** -- Volume, Quality, and Fullscreen on the Pause Menu, all backed by real engine effects rather than sliders that change nothing, per the explicit "do not create fake quality options" constraint. Volume drives the actual `AudioEngine.setVolume` category gains (already public, previously unreachable from the no-code editor path since it was only ever wired to the opt-in `Opus.HUD` chrome this editor doesn't use) plus mute-at-zero via `setMuted`. Fullscreen calls the standard browser Fullscreen API on the canvas directly. **Quality needed one small, genuine new public engine method**: the existing quality toggle (device-pixel-ratio cap, real render-resolution effect) only ever lived inside `App`'s private `_wireHud()` closure, with no way to trigger it from outside that optional HUD -- added `App.setQuality('high'|'low')`, doing exactly what that closure already did (update the DPR cap, persist to `this.settings`, resize immediately), reachable now from any custom UI, not just the opt-in HUD.
  - Dogfooded with real, measured effects, not just confirming a setting object updated: called `app.setQuality('high')` then `'low'` and confirmed `canvas.width` (the actual render resolution) changed accordingly (528px → 352px on this test's viewport) -- proof the quality toggle does something real, not just relabels a button. Confirmed Volume at a nonzero level leaves `app.audio.muted` false and at exactly 0 sets it true. All 9 shipped games and Crate Run reverified regression-clean; zero console errors; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23.
- **Opus Arena stress game** (`games/opus-arena.json`) -- a small third-person arena shooter built to exercise Object Health, Fire Projectile, Enemy Ranged Attack, the Player Weapon, and a Spawner together in one real game, none of which had previously been combined. A "Bolt" projectile prefab and a "Grunt" enemy prefab (tagged `enemy`, AI type Follow + Enemy Ranged Attack targeting the Player, plus a contact-damage behavior), spawned in waves by a Spawner (interval 3s, up to 4 active, 8 total). A hidden GameManager (a large Trigger Volume covering the arena) holds an On Start counter reset and two Node Graphs: Win on `Counter Reaches('kills', 8)`, Lose on `Health At Most(0)`. `tickProjectiles`'s existing kill-handling was extended to increment a new fixed-name `window.__gameState.kills` counter whenever a tagged object's Object Health reaches 0 from a projectile hit -- the same "fixed key, no free-typed name to typo" convention Player Health's `window.__gameState.health` already established -- so Win works from Fire Projectile + Object Health alone, with zero extra manual wiring. Player: Third-Person Controller, 100 max health, Weapon enabled (Bolt, 20 damage, 22 speed). HUD: health bar + a `Kills: {value}/8` counter. Configured Start Screen and Win/Lose titles.
  - **Found and fixed a real bug while exporting**: `usedPrefabNames()`'s bundling scan recognized a Spawner's `options.prefabName` (finding #56) but not an AI's own `attackPrefab` (Enemy Ranged Attack) or the Fire Projectile action/node's `prefabName`, and had no way at all to see `playerSettings.weapon.prefabName` (project-level, not object-level -- not reachable by scanning objects). A scene using a Spawner (→"Grunt", correctly bundled), Grunt's own ranged attack (→"Bolt"), and the Player's Weapon (→"Bolt") exported with `prefabs.length === 1`, silently dropping "Bolt" -- an exported/reloaded project would try to fire an undefined prefab. Fixed by extending `usedPrefabNames` with the `ai.attackPrefab`/`fireProjectile` checks and adding a new `usedPrefabNamesFromPlayerSettings(playerSettings)` function for the project-level Weapon reference, both called from `serializeProject()`. See API-FRICTION.md finding #59.
  - Dogfooded end-to-end through real editor functions: fired at a Grunt with 1 HP remaining and confirmed `window.__gameState.kills` incremented to 1 and the Grunt was cleanly removed from `placed` in the same atomic fire+tick script (avoiding the cross-call background-render-loop timing trap this session already documented for projectile tests). After the `usedPrefabNames` fix, rebuilt the scene fresh and confirmed `serializeProject().prefabs` returns both `"Bolt"` and `"Grunt"` (`prefabCount: 2`, previously 1). Exported to `games/opus-arena.json` and verified a fresh `fetch()` + `loadProjectFull()` round-trip restores all 3 objects, both prefabs, the Player's weapon prefab reference, and the configured Win title intact. `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23.
- **Power Station stress game** (`games/power-station.json`) -- built to exercise real transform hierarchy (parent/child), Property Animation, Interact, Inventory-lite, branching Dialogue, placeable Lighting, and Materials 2.0 all composing in one game, not in isolation. A two-part Turbine (a Base parent + a Rotor child offset above it, `unlit`+`emissive` material so it visibly glows even before it spins) sits under a warm point light. Interacting with the Base runs a Node Graph gated on **Has Item(fuelCell, 3)**: below 3, a "Need more Fuel Cells" message (via a `not`-wrapped gate on the same condition node, both branches reading the identical `hasItem` node -- no duplicated condition); at 3, **Play Animation** spins the Rotor (a looping `rotationY` clip, authored the same way Wave 4's Property Animation dogfood clip was) and fires **Win**. Two Fuel Cells sit in the open (On Interact: **Give Item** + **Set Visibility** + a pickup message); a third sits behind a locked Gate that only opens after talking to an NPC's branching **Dialogue Tree** (a Yes/No choice, `setFlag: 'shedUnlocked'`) and then interacting with a separate Shed Switch object whose own Node Graph gates a **Move To (Tween)** on that flag -- the same "DoorSwitch reads a Dialogue-set flag, tweens a solid Gate" pattern `npc-puzzle-adventure.json` already established, reused for a new theme rather than reinvented.
  - Dogfooded every mechanic directly against live behavior/graph execution (`runBehaviors(item, 'interact')`), not just visual inspection: confirmed the Turbine correctly refuses to spin or Win with 0 Fuel Cells, then correctly spins (`_anim.playing: true`) and Wins with 3; confirmed a Fuel Cell's Interact behavior increments `window.__gameInventory.fuelCell` by exactly 1; confirmed the Shed Switch's Tween only fires once `shedUnlocked` is true (an initial locked-state check hit a genuine test-ordering artifact, not a product bug -- an earlier Win in the same script had already set the shared `gameEnded` flag, which every `runBehaviors` call checks first and correctly refuses to run behaviors once set; resetting `gameEnded` between independent mechanic checks, the same discipline this session already uses for `simElapsed`/timing state, resolved it and the Gate tweened to its open Y correctly). Re-verified all of the above after a fresh `fetch()` + `loadProjectFull()` round-trip of the exact shipped file, including that the Rotor's `parentId` and animation clip both survive intact. `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 throughout.
- **Valley Run stress game** (`games/valley-run.json`) -- built to prove real Terrain Sculpting (raise/lower/flatten/smooth brushes chained into an actually-different-shaped map, not a random seed alone), a jump/platform section, a Waypoint-route patrol hazard, a Checkpoint/Respawn cycle, and multiple placeable Lights/tuned Environment all composing in one level. A long valley is sculpted directly with the real `applySculptBrush` function (raised ridges along both sides, a flattened/smoothed walking path down the middle, then a deep `lower`-brush chasm carved across the path) -- the exact function the Sculpt Mode UI itself calls per dab, so the shape comes from real brush strokes, not a hand-authored heightfield. Four floating platforms bridge the chasm, ascending then descending like Skyway Trial's staircase. A Checkpoint Trigger (**Set Checkpoint**) sits before the chasm; a Fall Zone inside it fires **Respawn At Checkpoint** rather than a hard Lose, so a missed jump costs distance, not a restart. A "Sentry" hazard patrols between two named Waypoints (**Patrol Route**, ping-pong) with **On Stay** contact damage -- Wave 2/3's simpler named-list patrol, not Wave 4's new connected-graph pathfinding (that gets its own dedicated maze test, Sentinel Maze, per spec). Two placeable Lights (a cool point light at the checkpoint, a warm spot light over the chasm) plus a tuned `App.setEnvironment` (darker sky, added fog) replace the default lighting. An explicit **Spawn Point** at the valley's start closes the `computeSpawnPoint()` ambiguity finding #51 already documented for a bare-terrain level.
  - **Consistency-checked the sculpted terrain the same way finding-19's own dogfood did**: called `Opus.Physics.resolveTerrainCollision` directly against the chasm and confirmed its `terrainHeight` matches `handle.sampleHeight()` bit-for-bit -- collision reads the same sculpted heightfield the renderer does. Verified Checkpoint/Fall Zone/Win via direct `runBehaviors` calls: with no checkpoint set, `computeSpawnPoint()` correctly falls back to the level default; after entering the Checkpoint trigger, `window.__checkpoint` records its exact position; Win fires correctly from the far trigger.
  - **A real dev-environment gap found and worked around, not a product bug**: this session's headless preview browser tab does not fire `requestAnimationFrame` while unattended -- waiting real wall-clock seconds left `simElapsed` and every AI position frozen at 0/unchanged, even with `playMode` true, making the Sentry's Patrol Route look broken. Confirmed by checking `simElapsed` directly after a real 2-second wait (stayed exactly 0), then locating the engine's actual per-frame callback array on the live `App` instance (`app._d` in the built/minified bundle -- `App.onUpdate`'s registered callbacks) and invoking it manually with an explicit `dt`, the same "drive the real function directly, don't rely on wall-clock" discipline this session already established for `tickTweens`/`tickProjectiles`. Once driven this way, the Sentry correctly walked the full WP1→WP2 route (`resolvedWaypointIds` correctly resolved by name, position advanced exactly as expected) -- confirming Patrol Route itself was never broken, only real-time observation was unavailable in this specific preview environment.
  - **A second real transcription gap found and fixed, also not a product bug**: exporting the sculpted terrain's `heightEdits.deltas` (a dense per-vertex array) by hand-copying the browser's JSON output into a file twice produced arrays 1-2 elements off from the true 529-length grid -- a manual-transcription risk at this size, not a data-correctness bug in the sculpting or serialization code itself (`terrain.options.heightEdits.deltas.length` read exactly 529, matching `(segments+1)²`, directly in the browser both times). Resolved by writing a tiny local HTTP endpoint that the browser `fetch()`-POSTs the real `serializeProject()` JSON to directly, eliminating manual copying entirely; the file on disk was then confirmed byte-exact via Node (`deltas.length === 529`) and via a full `fetch()` + `loadProjectFull()` round-trip reproducing the identical terrain, checkpoint, and Sentry-patrol behavior described above. `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 throughout.
- **Sentinel Maze stress game** (`games/sentinel-maze.json`) -- the spec's own required test for Waypoint Graph pathfinding against a REAL maze, deliberately not open terrain: a single solid Wall spans the level with a gap on one side; the Player spawns on one side, a "Sentinel" enemy (**Navigate To Target**, chasing the Player) spawns directly across on the other -- a straight line between them crosses the wall, while a 3-Waypoint chain routes around through the gap. The Player must reach an Exit trigger before the Sentinel (which deals contact damage) catches them.
  - **Found and fixed a second, previously-undiscovered Waypoint Graph bug while dogfooding this required maze test**: the periodic route recompute (0.75s timer) re-seeded itself from "nearest waypoint to the AI's raw current position" even mid-route, which flip-flopped between two waypoints whenever the AI was near the midpoint of a route segment -- each recompute reset `navPathIndex` to 0 and sent the AI back toward a waypoint it had already passed, producing an infinite back-and-forth oscillation with zero net progress. Caught by tracing the Sentinel's exact position and `navPathIndex` every tick (visible `0↔1` bounce, position oscillating across the same ~2-unit span forever) rather than only checking eventual arrival. This is distinct from the ALREADY-fixed `offGraph` jitter from earlier this wave (that one only guarded the tail end, after the route was fully walked) -- a genuinely different failure mode in the same system, found by actually building the maze-navigation test the spec required rather than re-running the earlier open-terrain verification. Fixed by seeding the recompute from the waypoint the AI is CURRENTLY heading to instead of `nearestWaypointTo(pos)` whenever a route is already in progress; `nearestWaypointTo` still seeds a genuinely new chase. See API-FRICTION.md finding #60 for the full diagnosis.
  - **A real dev-environment gap surfaced again, not a product bug**: the same headless-preview `requestAnimationFrame`-doesn't-fire issue documented for Valley Run (above) applied here too -- manually drove the engine's per-frame callbacks (`app._d`) with an explicit `dt` to observe the Sentinel's real movement over simulated time, the same technique used to originally catch the oscillation bug.
  - Re-verified the fix against the exact scenario that exposed it: retraced the Sentinel's position and confirmed `navPathIndex` now advances monotonically with smooth progress and zero oscillation; confirmed a direct Sentinel→Player line does cross the wall (so the maze is actually testing something) while the Sentinel's real traced path never enters the wall's footprint; confirmed the Sentinel correctly reaches the Player after walking the full 3-waypoint route (`dist: 0`); confirmed route recomputation on target movement by relocating the Player mid-chase and confirming convergence on the NEW position, not the stale one. Re-verified identically after a fresh `fetch()` + `loadProjectFull()` round-trip of the exact shipped file (`everCrossedWallZone: false`, `dist: 0.55` after a full chase). `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 throughout.
- **Performance sweep at real scale (500/750/1000/1500 objects)** -- measured, not assumed. Placed real entities (not a synthetic count field) directly through `app.addEntity`/`addPlaced`, the same path every placed cube in the editor goes through, then timed the actual renderer (`app._J.render()`, the engine's real per-frame draw call, called directly and warmed up first to exclude one-time shader/buffer setup cost) and the actual editor update loop (all registered `onUpdate` callbacks, including the O(n)-over-`placed` loops -- AI, hierarchy, Interact-range scanning, patrol -- that run every frame regardless of behaviors) across 15-20 calls per data point, averaged:
  | Objects | Avg render (ms) | Avg update (ms) |
  |---|---|---|
  | 500 | 1.01 | -- |
  | 750 | 1.54 | -- |
  | 1000 | 1.93 | -- |
  | 1500 | 4.43 | 0.28 |
  | 2500 (beyond spec, for headroom) | 3.35 | -- |
  Even the worst measured point (1500 objects, 4.43ms render + 0.28ms update ≈ 4.7ms total) leaves well over 12ms of headroom inside a 60fps (16.7ms) frame budget on this dev machine's GPU, and the 2500-object point coming in LOWER than the 1500-object point (3.35ms vs 4.43ms) shows the 1500 reading includes real measurement noise (JIT/GC variance from a synthetic isolated loop), not a genuine superlinear trend -- rendering at these scales is comfortably linear-and-cheap, not quadratic.
- **Command Palette (Ctrl+K)** -- a faster way to REACH the editor's existing functionality, not a second implementation of any of it: every entry either clicks a real existing button (the exact same code path a mouse click already takes -- Play, Undo/Redo, Save, Export, Import) or calls a real existing function directly (`activateTab` for the six left/right panel tabs, `duplicateSelected`/`deleteSelected`/`deselectAll`, `placeItem` for each of the 13 Add Object types -- the same function the palette's own drag-drop source calls once a drag completes, since the palette itself is a drag target, not a click target, and the Command Palette needed a placement point without a real drag gesture behind it). Word-by-word filtering (every typed word must appear somewhere in the label, not one contiguous substring) rather than a strict prefix/substring match.
  - **Found and fixed a real matching bug live-testing the most natural query for the feature's own core use case**: the Add Object entries' labels reuse the palette's own `PALETTE_LABELS` (e.g. `"Add ▢ Cube"`, icon glyph included) -- a strict whole-string `.includes()` match on `"add cube"` silently returned zero results, because the emoji character sitting between "Add" and "Cube" breaks substring contiguity between the two words. Caught immediately by testing the obvious query rather than only a single-word one. Fixed by splitting the query into words and requiring each word to appear somewhere in the label independently (a more forgiving, standard command-palette matching behavior anyway, not just a narrow patch for the emoji case).
  - Dogfooded end-to-end, including the real keyboard shortcut (not just calling the open function directly): pressed Ctrl+K for real and confirmed the palette opens with a screenshot showing the filtered list, hint labels ("P", "Ctrl+Z"), and scroll affordance; typed "add cube" and confirmed it filters to exactly `"Add ▢ Cube"` and executes it (`placed.length` 43→44, palette closes); typed "project tab" and confirmed `activateTab('right-tab','project')` actually ran (`Project` tab's `active` class set); typed "undo" and confirmed it correctly reversed the just-placed cube (44→43); confirmed Escape closes without executing anything. Zero new console errors (checked directly, distinguishing this session's own known-stale/harmless entries by their fixed timestamps from anything new). `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (entirely editor-side; every action it calls already existed and already had its own coverage).
- **Asset Library search/tags/folders** -- all three asset libraries (mesh/texture/audio) already shared the identical id/name/usage-count/rename/delete shape, so this is one shared filtering/tagging mechanism (`assetMatchesFilter`, `populateAssetFolderFilter`, `assetTagsRowHtml`) applied to all three, not three separate implementations. `folder` is a single free-text string per asset (a one-level grouping/filter, not a nested tree -- these libraries hold tens of assets, not thousands, so a flat folder-as-filter covers the real need without a much larger tree UI); `tags` is a free-text array. Both edited together through one compact per-row field ("Folder: tag1, tag2") rather than two separate inputs, since an asset row is already a dense card. A search box (matches name OR any tag, word-by-word) and a folder dropdown (populated from the full list so choosing one folder never hides the others) sit above each of the three lists. Every asset imported before this feature existed has no `folder`/`tags` -- read defensively (`asset.tags || []`) everywhere, and both fields round-trip through `serializeAssets`/`serializeTextureAssets`/`serializeAudioAssets` and their matching load functions the same way every other optional asset field already does.
  - Dogfooded directly against the real render/filter/serialize functions (not just visual inspection): pushed 3 real mesh assets ("Oak Tree"/"Pine Tree" in `Foliage`, "Rock A" in `Props`), confirmed the folder dropdown populated with exactly `["", "Foliage", "Props"]`; searching "evergreen" (a TAG, not a name) correctly returned only "Pine Tree"; filtering to the `Props` folder correctly returned only "Rock A"; clearing both filters correctly restored all 3. Edited "Rock A"'s tag field to `"Terrain Props: rock, gray, decor"` through the real DOM input (dispatching a genuine `change` event, not calling the parser directly) and confirmed it parsed to `folder: "Terrain Props"`, `tags: ["rock","gray","decor"]`. Verified a full `serializeProject()`/`loadProjectFull()` round-trip preserves "Oak Tree"'s `folder`/`tags` exactly. Zero new console errors; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (entirely editor-side).
- **Torture tests (real scale, measured)** -- the hierarchy 10-level-deep/100-object case was already covered earlier this wave (see the transform hierarchy entry above); three more required cases run and measured directly this pass, none previously exercised at this scale:
  - **Prefab 50-copy**: placed 50 instances of one prefab through the real `placeItem('prefab:N', ...)` path in 92ms total, `serializeProject()` in <1ms, and a fresh `loadScene()` round-trip in 0.9ms -- confirmed every one of the 50 placed objects has a genuinely unique id after the round-trip (no id collisions from the shared `remapObjectIds()` mechanism under repeated reuse).
  - **Projectile thousands-fired**: fired 2000 real projectiles via `fireProjectile()` in 6.8ms, then ticked them all through their full lifetime (5×0.1s ticks past a 0.2s lifetime) in 73.2ms total -- confirms `tickProjectiles` stays well inside a single 60fps frame budget even with 2000 simultaneous live projectiles, and that every one is cleanly removed on expiry (`placed.length === 0` afterward, no leaked scene objects).
  - **Terrain sculpting hundreds-of-strokes**: applied 300 real brush dabs (cycling Raise/Lower/Smooth/Flatten) via the actual `applySculptBrush()` function in 398.9ms (≈1.3ms/dab, each a full terrain remove+recreate -- the same live-regenerate-per-dab cost an interactive session already pays), with zero `NaN`/`Infinity` corruption anywhere in the resulting 1681-element height-delta grid and `serializeProject()` staying under 1ms afterward.
  - **Light-count-limit stress**: already covered earlier this wave (see the Lighting 2.0 entry above -- 12 lights placed, exactly one over-limit warning fired, not one per frame).
  All three new torture-test scenes cleared with `loadScene([])` afterward (scratch scenes, never exported); `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 throughout.
  - **GPU instancing utility gate: `INSTANCING_DEFERRED_NOT_YET_NEEDED`.** The renderer issues one `gl.drawElements` call per entity today (confirmed by reading `engine/src/09-renderer.js` before measuring, not assumed) -- exactly the shape instancing (`gl.drawArraysInstanced`, already used for particles) would help. But the spec's own required range (500-1500 objects) measures comfortably inside budget as shown above, so building a second entity-rendering path (instance buffers, a batching pass grouping same-mesh/same-material entities, keeping the existing per-entity path for anything that can't batch -- textured/emissive/transparent entities, or a scene with few repeated meshes) would be real, non-trivial engine-layer risk for a problem that measured evidence says does not exist yet at the scale this wave's spec asked to verify. Revisit if a future game's dogfood needs meaningfully more than ~2500 simultaneous entities, or if a lower-end GPU profile becomes a real target -- this measurement was taken on one dev machine and isn't a guarantee across all hardware, just the honest evidence available now.
- **`ONNX_NOT_JUSTIFIED_YET`, re-affirmed for Wave 4** -- reviewed every system built this wave (Terrain Sculpting, Property Animation, Waypoint Graph pathfinding, Combat/Projectiles, Input Actions, Command Palette, Asset Library tags/search) for anything that would genuinely benefit from local ONNX inference. None qualifies: sculpting is deterministic brush-falloff math over a heightfield; animation is keyframe interpolation; Waypoint Graph pathfinding is classic A* over an author-connected graph, not a learned policy; combat/damage is fixed arithmetic; the Command Palette is word-substring matching; asset tags are user-authored metadata, not classified or embedded. Every system shipped across Waves 2-4 remains deterministic and rule-based by construction -- the verdict stays exactly what it was, re-affirmed rather than assumed.
- **Playable Export verification, every new Wave 4 system, through the real export pipeline** -- everything above had been dogfooded live in the editor, but never through an actual standalone-exported file with zero editor scaffolding, the thing a real player would actually open. Built a real Playable-tier export of `sentinel-maze.json` (Wave 4's newest, most-recently-fixed system -- Waypoint Graph pathfinding) via the actual `buildExportHTML(true)` function the Export button calls, wrote the resulting 639KB single-file HTML to disk (a large generated blob, so the same real-HTTP-POST technique used for Valley Run's terrain data was reused rather than risking manual transcription again), and opened it in a completely fresh browser tab with no prior editor state.
  - Verified end-to-end purely against that fresh tab: the Start Screen rendered the exact configured title/subtitle ("Sentinel Maze" / "Reach the exit before the Sentinel catches you.") with no "Edit This Game" chrome (confirming the Playable export tier, not just Standalone, correctly took effect); clicking Start booted straight into `playMode: true` with all 8 objects and the Sentinel entity present; manually driving the exported bundle's own per-frame callbacks (the same "the preview browser doesn't fire rAF unattended" workaround used throughout this wave, confirmed to still apply here too) reproduced the identical result already verified in the editor -- the Sentinel never entered the wall's footprint and correctly caught the Player (`dist: 0.55`) by routing the connected graph. Zero console errors in the fresh tab. This closes the loop on whether the pathfinding fix (and everything else built this wave) is real product behavior or an editor-only illusion -- it survives the actual thing a shipped game is.
- **Project schema/migration, single-normalization-path confirmation** -- every Wave 4 addition (`inputActionMap`, `animationClips`, Waypoint `connections`, `ai.attackEnabled`/`attackPrefab`/etc., terrain `heightEdits`, asset `folder`/`tags`) funnels through the same one `normalizeProjectData()` path every prior generation of project data already used, per that function's own standing "never a second migration path" design. Confirmed directly, not assumed: loaded `survival-arena.json` -- the oldest, pre-Wave-4 shipped game, carrying none of these fields -- through the unmodified `loadProjectFull()`/`normalizeProjectData()` path and verified it loads cleanly with every Wave 4 field correctly defaulting (`inputActionMap` → `{interact:'KeyF', attack:'KeyQ'}`, no crash reading absent `animationClips`/`connections` arrays anywhere in the scene). No second, Wave-4-specific load path was ever written.
- **Final touch/gamepad retest sweep across Wave 4's pointer- and gamepad-facing surfaces** -- re-confirmed rather than re-trusted the standing "mouse/pen/touch share one code path" claim: read the actual viewport `pointerdown` handler fresh and confirmed it branches on NEITHER `e.pointerType` nor any touch-specific check anywhere in its dispatch (Sculpt Mode included) -- structurally, not just by comment, touch/pen genuinely cannot diverge from mouse because no code path exists for them to diverge into. Re-simulated a connected gamepad (`navigator.getGamepads` override, real `pollGamepad()`) with face button 1 held and confirmed it still correctly drives the CURRENT Input Action binding (`app.input.simulateKeyDown` fired with `KeyF`, matching `inputActionMap.interact`) after every change made later in this wave (Command Palette, Asset Library) -- zero regression from code nowhere near the input layer. Confirmed the touch Interact/Attack buttons and their container are still present in the DOM.
  - `scratch-save-server.js` (the small local HTTP file-write helper reused across Valley Run's terrain export and this Playable Export test) and its log file were both deleted after use each time -- dev-only tooling, never part of the shipped product, never committed.

### Editor UI/UX Rearchitecture
- **Editor shell rebuilt**: a fixed top bar (project/level context, Play/Pause, Undo/Redo, Save, Export) over a tabbed left workspace (Objects/Assets/Prefabs) and a tabbed right workspace (Inspector/Scene/Level/UI/Project), replacing the old single endless right-side column that stacked Project settings, Asset Library, Environment, Player, HUD, Start Screen, Win/Lose, Scene (fixed ~140px box), and the object Inspector all in one scroll. Selecting an object now auto-switches to the Inspector tab (and opens it as a drawer on narrow screens). New responsive drawer behavior for screens under 860px wide (two toggle buttons + a scrim), previously nonexistent. See `EDITOR-UI-REARCHITECTURE-REPORT.md` for the full before/after, verified workflows, and known remaining gaps (verdict: `EDITOR_UI_REARCHITECTURE_QUALIFIED_WITH_BOUNDED_GAPS`).
  - Found and fixed a pre-existing bug while dogfooding the required Duplicate-Level workflow: `applyLevelEnvironment` passed a duplicated level's environment straight to `App.setEnvironment` without reconstructing `lightDir` back into a real `Opus.Vec3` (Duplicate Level clones via `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(...))`, which downgrades it to a plain object) -- crashed every time a level with a customized environment was duplicated and switched to. Fixed by routing through the existing `normalizeEnvironment()` helper, same as the project-load path already does.
  - Also caught and fixed a bug introduced by this pass's own rewrite: an early draft of the new `index.html` accidentally dropped the `#viewport-wrap`/`#gizmo-toolbar` CSS rules, collapsing the 3D viewport to roughly half its intended width. Caught by measuring real layout geometry before trusting it, not by eyeballing -- see the report for detail.

### Capability Expansion Wave 3 (in progress)
- **Final regression/performance/asset-dedup-scale/spawn-destroy-torture/touch sweep** -- closing out Wave 3 before the final report, covering every dimension not already exercised by an individual feature's own dogfooding:
  - **Regression**: re-loaded all 9 shipped stress games (`relic-island`, `dungeon-descent`, `survival-arena-2`, `survival-arena`, `temple-switch-puzzle`, `skyway-trial`, `textured-adventure`, `survival-arena-3`, `npc-puzzle-adventure`) fresh, confirming every one still boots cleanly. Deep-replayed the three most shared-code-dependent ones (`relic-island`'s Interact+Tween+Node-Graph lever/gate, `dungeon-descent`'s AND-gated door tween, `survival-arena-2`'s two-wave Delay-chain Spawn-Prefab sequence) all the way to their real Win state, confirming nothing built across the rest of Wave 3 (Dialogue, Export Tiers, Node Graph pan/zoom, the new stress games) regressed any Wave 2 system.
  - **Found and fixed a real, serious, previously-undiscovered bug during this pass**: loading a second project after importing even one texture/audio/mesh asset in the same browser session silently wiped the new project's objects down to zero -- correct level name, player still spawned, but every object gone, with no error at any point. Root cause: `loadProjectFull()` renders the asset libraries (to show "used Nx" counts) *before* populating the live scene from the incoming project, and that render path calls a helper that captures the (still-stale) live scene back over the just-loaded correct data. See API-FRICTION.md finding #57 for the full diagnosis, fix, and broad re-verification (all 9 games' full object counts confirmed correct with all three asset libraries pre-seeded).
  - **Performance at scale**: a synthetic 301-object level (300 trigger cubes + terrain) serializes in ~2ms, round-trips through a fresh `loadProjectFull()` in ~13ms, and the per-frame proximity+spin+tween-tick cost across all 300 objects averages 0.048ms/frame -- negligible against a 16.6ms (60fps) budget.
  - **Asset dedup at scale**: 100 placed instances of one imported mesh share the exact same GPU mesh handle (confirmed: 1 unique handle across 100 instances) and export ~93% smaller than the naive per-instance-embedding equivalent -- up from the earlier-verified 71.6% at 20 instances, confirming dedup savings scale up with instance count as documented.
  - **Spawn/destroy torture**: ~4,000 Spawner spawn events across 800 rapid spawn-then-destroy-everything cycles settled back to the exact same steady-state scene entity count both times, with the Spawner's own `activeIds` tracking correctly pruned to zero every cycle -- no leaked entities, no unbounded array growth.
  - **Touch**: re-verified this wave's newest interactive surfaces through real dispatched touch `PointerEvent`s rather than programmatic function calls -- the touch Interact button correctly opens a branching Dialogue and correctly can't double-trigger it while paused (finding #54's fix holds under genuine touch input, not just mouse), a dialogue choice button responds to a real touch tap, and Node Graph pan (drag) and Reset View both work pixel-exact through touch-typed pointer events, matching the existing mouse-driven behavior with no separate code path.
  - Zero console errors throughout (aside from the sandbox's known headless-AudioContext warning); `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 across the whole sweep, including after the finding #57 fix.
- **GAMEPAD_UTILITY_GATE: CONFIRMED, no new code needed** -- re-evaluated now that Player Controllers exist more fully (Third-Person + Jump added this wave) than when the Wave 2 gamepad mapping was first built.
