The existing generic key-code mapping (left stick/d-pad → WASD, face button 0 → Space, face button 1 → KeyF) already covers everything a controller needs: movement (unchanged), Interact (unchanged), Boost (unchanged, Hovercraft-only), and **Jump** -- which needed zero new gamepad code, since Jump and Boost share the exact same `Space` keycode and no two Player Controllers are ever active at once, so button 0 already means "the current controller's primary action button" regardless of which controller that happens to be. Camera has no keyboard equivalent to map either (every camera mode is either automatic chase-follow or author-fixed, never player-steered) -- gamepad parity with keyboard is already exact, nothing missing.
  - Dogfooded live rather than just reasoned about: mocked a connected gamepad (`navigator.getGamepads` override, real per-frame `pollGamepad()` code, not a stub) with face button 0 held on a real Third-Person+Jump level and confirmed an isolated single-frame step produced `thirdPersonVelocity.y === 8` (the exact configured jump speed) -- the identical edge-triggering/grounded-check code path the keyboard uses, exercised through the gamepad input layer instead. Also reconfirmed (unaffected by anything built this wave): face button 1 correctly sets `KeyF` (Interact), left-stick deflection past the dead zone correctly sets `KeyW`, small in-dead-zone stick noise (0.1, under the 0.25 threshold) correctly produces no movement, and a real keyboard key held down is untouched by an idle connected pad (the two input sources don't fight over shared key-state). One test-methodology trap caught along the way: an earlier attempt's `navigator.getGamepads` override was left in place across a "fresh" reset, silently reintroducing gamepad input into what was meant to be a keyboard-only control frame -- resolved by explicitly resetting the mock (not a product bug, a test-isolation mistake). Zero console errors; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (no code changed).
- **ONNX_NOT_JUSTIFIED_YET, re-affirmed** -- reviewed every system built across Wave 3 (textures, the audio asset pipeline, branching Dialogue with conditional gating, Export Tiers, Node Graph pan/zoom, the Gamepad re-confirmation above) for anything that would genuinely benefit from local ONNX inference -- classification, generation, embedding/similarity, or any learned decision a fixed rule can't already express. None qualifies: texture/audio assets are author-imported and browser-decoded, not classified or generated; Dialogue branching is deterministic condition evaluation (flags/items/health/counters), the same category of logic every other condition in this engine already uses; Export Tiers and Node Graph pan/zoom are pure editor tooling with no runtime decision-making at all. Every system this engine has shipped, across both Wave 2 and Wave 3, remains deterministic and rule-based by construction -- the verdict stays exactly what it was, re-affirmed rather than assumed.
- **`games/textured-adventure.json`** -- the Texture stress game: three distinctly-textured Relic pickups (each a separate imported checkerboard PNG, spin + Increment Counter + Set Visibility + Play Sound on Enter), a textured Hazard (damage-over-time, Lose on death), and a textured Shrine gated on `Counter Reaches(relics, 3)` for Win. Verified end-to-end both ways (collecting all 3 relics and touching the Shrine = Win, standing in the Hazard = Lose), including after a fresh `fetch()` + `loadProjectFull()` round-trip of the exact shipped file. Zero console errors; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (no engine-layer changes, entirely existing Wave 2/3 primitives composed together).
- **`games/survival-arena-3.json`** -- the Spawner-based rebuild of the Wave 2 Survival Arena stress game: a real `Spawner` object (`prefabName: "Hunter"`) replacing the old hand-wired Delay-chain wave workaround, plus a hidden timer cube (Delay 20s → Win) as the survival clock. **Found and fixed a real, previously-undiscovered bundling bug** while exporting this file: `usedPrefabNames()` (decides which locally-saved prefabs get embedded into a project export) only ever recognized a prefab referenced via the `spawnPrefab` action on a cube's Behaviors or Node Graph -- it never checked a `Spawner` object's own `options.prefabName`. A level depending on a Spawner alone (no `spawnPrefab` action anywhere) would silently export with `prefabs: []` -- a file that opens fine, plays fine in the authoring session (prefabs already live in the browser's local library), and then spawns *nothing* on a fresh machine with no error at all, since the Spawner simply finds no matching prefab name. Fixed by adding a `record.kind === 'spawner'` check alongside the existing behavior scan. See API-FRICTION.md finding #56. Verified the fix directly: cleared every local prefab, fresh-loaded the exported file, and confirmed the Spawner still successfully spawns Hunters from the now-correctly-bundled prefab. Zero console errors; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23.
- **`games/npc-puzzle-adventure.json`** -- the Dialogue+Interact+Inventory+Flags stress game (deliberately no Custom Script): a 2-level "Village" → "Inner Sanctum" story. An NPC (Sailor) carries both a branching `dialogueTree` (offers a key, flag-gated so the offer doesn't repeat meaninglessly) and a separate Node Graph gated on that same flag (`isFlagTrue('talkedToSailor')`) that actually grants the Give Item + Set Flag -- the two systems composing through shared state rather than one calling into the other. A DoorSwitch's Node Graph (`Has Item('key') → Move To (Tween) → Change Camera → Show Message`) opens a solid Door and cuts to a dedicated Camera object for the reveal; a trigger past the door fires Next Level; the second level's trigger fires Win. Verified the complete path end-to-end, live in Play mode against the actual exported file (not just the authoring session): first Interact shows exactly one dialogue choice with zero starting inventory; choosing it sets the flag and advances to the closing entry; a second Interact (flag now true) grants the key via the graph while the dialogue's own re-offer of the same choice is harmless (still gated the same way); the DoorSwitch's condition correctly blocks with no key and passes with one; the door tween lands exactly on its configured target; the level trigger correctly advances `currentLevelIndex` and respawns the player in "Inner Sanctum"; the Sanctum trigger correctly fires Win with its configured title. Zero console errors (aside from the sandbox's known headless-AudioContext warning, present on every game this session, not a product issue); `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23.
- **Node Graph pan/zoom** -- real click-drag panning (drag empty canvas background; a node/port drag still wins since those have their own listeners), cursor-anchored scroll-wheel zoom (35%-250%, clamped), a **Fit** button (fits every node into view with padding, centered) and a **Reset View** button, plus a live zoom-% readout. Pan/zoom is purely a CSS transform on the rendering layers -- node coordinates on disk are completely unaffected, and view state itself is explicitly UI-only (never serialized into the saved graph, matching the rearchitecture pass's "editor layout preferences are not project data" principle) -- confirmed directly: re-opening a graph always starts at the same default view regardless of how the previous session left it panned/zoomed.
  - The pre-existing node-drag and connection-line-drawing code both did screen-pixel math directly against graph-space coordinates, which only happened to work by coincidence at the fixed 100% zoom that was the only zoom level that ever existed before. Both needed a real fix, not just a wrapper: node dragging now converts the cursor position through a shared `graphScreenToSpace()` helper (divides by the current scale) before computing the grab offset and the live position; the SVG connection lines divide their computed screen-space port positions by the same scale before writing them as line coordinates, since the `<svg>` element carries the identical CSS transform its lines are drawn inside of.
  - Dogfooded live and verified with exact coordinate arithmetic, not just "it looks right": zoomed via a real wheel event and confirmed the graph-space point under the cursor was bit-for-bit identical before and after (cursor-anchoring math, not approximate); dragged a node at 35% zoom and confirmed a 35-screen-pixel move produced exactly a 100-graph-space-pixel move (screen delta ÷ scale); connected two ports at 51.75% zoom and confirmed the resulting SVG line's screen-space endpoint was pixel-exact (0px diff) against the actual port's rendered center; confirmed panning tracks the cursor 1:1 in screen space; confirmed a real Fit run against 3 widely-scattered nodes correctly brought every node's rendered box inside the viewport at a real (non-zero, explicitly-set) window size, after first catching that the default browser-pane state reports a zero-height viewport rect -- not a product bug, a known artifact of this tool's rendering-pane compositing state (see earlier findings), worked around the same way earlier phases did: `resize_window` to a real size before measuring layout-dependent geometry. Built and saved a real 22-action/condition-node gameplay graph (counter tracking, a dialogue-gated boss encounter, checkpoint/respawn, tween/emitter effects, win/lose) with 4 real wired connections -- Fit correctly brought all 23 rendered nodes (22 + the fixed Start node) inside the viewport with zero out-of-bounds, 20 rapid zoom events processed in 16ms (no perceptible lag at this node count), and the full graph round-tripped intact through `serializeProject()` → JSON → `loadProjectFull()`. Zero console errors throughout; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (Node Graph is entirely editor-side, no engine-layer surface).
- **Export Tiers** -- three deliberate tiers instead of one, sharing ONE export builder (`buildExportHTML`) rather than a second maintained pipeline per tier:
  - **Export Project (.json)** -- the existing raw project export, unchanged, now labeled to make clear it's data (re-importable) not a playable file on its own.
  - **Export Editable Game (.html)** -- the existing standalone export (was the only tier), now explicitly named as one of three: a single-file playable game with the full editor one click away via "Edit This Game".
  - **Export Playable Game (.html), new** -- the exact same file (same engine bundle, same editor/runtime script, same embedded project -- genuinely the SAME code, not a second cut-down player runtime that could drift from what the real editor does), plus one embedded boolean (`window.__OPUS_PLAYABLE_ONLY__`) the script reads at its own boot time to permanently disable the ONE entry point back into editing. `setEditorVisible` itself refuses to reveal the chrome while `playableOnlyMode` is set (not just a hidden button -- a direct call to reveal it is refused too), and the toggle button is hidden outright. For sharing the finished game without also shipping "here's the editor for it."
  - **Export metadata** -- a new Title/Author/Version/Description panel (Project tab). Title becomes the exported HTML's `<title>` (verified via regex extraction against the real generated file, not assumed); Author/Version/Description are carried through Save/Load/Export for the author's own record-keeping, deliberately not forced into any in-game UI (Start Screen/HUD text are already separately authorable). Round-trips through `serializeProject()`/`loadProjectFull()` like any other project field.
  - **Export size safety warning** -- any export over 15MB (both HTML tiers embed the same assets either way, so this isn't tier-specific) shows a status warning with the actual size, without blocking the export -- still a valid file, just worth knowing about before emailing or hitting a host's upload cap.
  - **Asset embedding** was already solved by the existing mesh/texture/audio asset libraries (all embed as base64 in project JSON) -- both HTML tiers get it for free by embedding the full serialized project either way; no separate embedding step needed per tier.
  - Dogfooded live: set real metadata through the actual Project tab fields; built both HTML tiers via the real `buildExportHTML()` and confirmed via precise string extraction (isolating the actual embedded-flags `<script>` block, not a substring match against the whole file -- the first attempt at this check was a false positive, since the literal string `window.__OPUS_PLAYABLE_ONLY__ = true;` also appears as source text inside `buildExportHTML`'s own inlined code regardless of which tier is built) that the Playable tier's block ends with the flag assignment and the Editable tier's doesn't. Verified the actual lockout behavior live (not just the embedded flag) by exercising the real `setEditorVisible`/`chromeToggleBtn` code path with the flag set: clicking the toggle while hidden stayed hidden, and a direct `setEditorVisible(true)` call was refused too. Full metadata round-trip (`serializeProject()` → JSON → `loadProjectFull()`) verified restoring every field into the real UI inputs. Zero console errors throughout; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (export tiers are entirely editor-side, no new engine-layer surface).
- **Dialogue system upgrade: branching conversations** -- the Wave 2 `showDialogue` action (speaker/text + up to 2 flag-setting choices, always closes) is untouched and still fully works, in both the Behaviors dropdown and Node Graph. Additive on top: a new **Show Dialogue (Branching)** action (`dialogueTree`, outside `ACTION_TYPES` -- same "opens its own editor instead of flat fields" pattern `graph` already established, so it's Behaviors-only by the same reasoning Node Graph itself doesn't nest inside Node Graph) with a real dedicated **Dialogue Editor** modal: named entries (speaker/text), up to 3 choices per entry, each choice optionally **jumping to another entry** instead of always closing, optionally **setting a flag**, and optionally **gated by a condition** -- Is Flag True/False, Has Item, Health At Least/Most, Counter At Least/Most, reusing the exact same `evaluateConditionNode` the Node Graph already evaluates its own conditions with (leaf types only, no AND/OR/NOT nesting) rather than a second condition-evaluation system.
  - **Found and fixed a real Interact double-trigger bug** while confirming Dialogue can't fire twice: the engine gates every `app.onUpdate` callback behind `!app.paused` (so the keyboard Interact path is automatically blocked while a Dialogue has the game paused), but the touch Interact button's `pointerdown` listener calls `fireInteract()` directly, with no such gate -- tapping it while a Dialogue was already open re-ran the same `interact` behavior on top of the first, e.g. opening a second Dialogue instance. Fixed with a one-line `if (app.paused) return;` guard at the top of `fireInteract()`, which is a no-op on the already-correct keyboard path and closes the gap on the touch path. See API-FRICTION.md finding #54.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end through the real UI (Add Behavior → Show Dialogue (Branching) → the actual modal's form fields, not raw data): authored a 2-entry conversation (a guard asking for a key) with one choice gated on `Has Item "key"` jumping to a second entry, and a second unconditional choice closing immediately. Verified: without the item, only the unconditional choice renders; giving the item and re-triggering correctly reveals both; choosing the gated one correctly navigates to entry 2 (not closing); the touch button no longer re-fires while paused; Duplicate deep-clones the tree (editing the copy's text left the original's untouched -- confirmed by reference inequality, not just content); a full `serializeProject()` → JSON → `loadProjectFull()` round-trip preserves every entry/choice/condition and the reloaded scene reproduces the exact same conditional filtering. Zero console errors throughout; Crate Run reboot regression-clean; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (no new engine-layer surface -- this is entirely an editor-side feature built on existing condition/flag/inventory/health primitives).
- **Texture support** -- a bounded, deliberately-NOT-PBR texture system: real per-face UVs (cube gets a proper box-unwrap; `Opus.Mesh.fromArrays` takes an optional `uvs` array; `Opus.Mesh.toArrays` returns them), a new `App.createTexture(image)`/`destroyTexture(handle)` engine API, and `entity.texture` -- one optional base-color texture per entity, sampled in the existing lit shader and simply multiplied into the same ambient+diffuse+spec lighting every untextured entity already used (`u_useTexture` gates the sample, so the overwhelming majority of existing untextured content is provably unaffected -- verified by re-booting Crate Run, the regression anchor, with zero console errors). No metallic/roughness/normal/emissive maps, no multi-texture layering.
  - **Editor: Texture Asset Library** -- Import Texture (any image file) or automatically from a glTF/GLB's embedded base-color texture (glTF import now extracts `TEXCOORD_0` and one base-color texture -- see 21-gltf.js; still geometry-first, still no animations/skinning). New Material Inspector field (Texture dropdown, alongside the existing Color field which now tints the texture rather than replacing it) on any cube object. Mirrors the Wave 3 Mesh Asset Library's shape (id/name/usage-count/Place-Rename-Delete, usage-count-gated deletion) with one necessary difference: image decode is genuinely asynchronous, bridged with a small pending-callback queue so a textured object loaded from a fresh project briefly renders untextured for at most one decode's worth of time instead of blocking the (synchronous) scene-load path.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end: generated a real checkerboard PNG in-browser, imported it through the actual Texture Asset Library code path (not a shortcut), applied it to a cube through the real inspector `<select>`, confirmed `entity.texture` is a genuine `WebGLTexture` (not a mock/stub), round-tripped the whole project through `serializeProject()` → JSON → `loadProjectFull()` and confirmed the texture re-attached correctly via the async pending-callback path, verified usage-count-gated delete correctly refuses while in use and succeeds once cleared, and reconfirmed zero console errors and a clean Crate Run boot throughout. New engine unit test (`node tests/run-tests.js`, 23/23) covers `fromArrays` uv validation/defaulting and a real glTF file with `TEXCOORD_0` plus an embedded single-pixel PNG.
- **Audio asset pipeline** -- browser-native decode confirmed as the right approach (no bundled codec needed): `AudioEngine.decodeAudio(arrayBuffer)` wraps the standard `AudioContext.decodeAudioData`, so mp3/wav/ogg/whatever the browser itself supports just works. New `AudioEngine.playBuffer(buffer, {volume, loop, position, category})` plays a decoded `AudioBuffer` through the exact same gain/panning routing `playBlip` already used, one-shot or looping, returning a `{stop()}` handle.
  - **Editor: Audio Asset Library** (Assets tab) -- Import Audio (any sound file), Preview, Rename, usage-count-gated Delete. Mirrors the Texture Asset Library's async-decode-bridged-with-pending-callbacks shape exactly (see that entry) since decode is genuinely async there too.
  - **Play Sound 2.0** -- the existing Play Sound action gained a Mode field: **Synth** (the original procedural blip, still the default -- every pre-2.0 saved behavior has no `.mode` at all and falls through to it unchanged) or **Asset** (plays a real imported sound at an authored volume). Both field groups render together (the fixed-dropdown Behaviors UI has no per-field conditional visibility), `exec()` just reads whichever Mode selects.
  - **Per-level Ambient Audio** -- a new collapsible section in the Level tab: pick an Audio Asset + volume, loops automatically on Play (and on every mid-play level switch, replacing whatever was playing -- never stacks), stops cleanly on Stop.
  - Dogfooded live end-to-end: synthesized a real WAV file in-browser (not a stub) and decoded it through the actual `AudioEngine.decodeAudio` path; imported it into the Audio Library and confirmed it renders correctly (duration, usage count); applied it as both a Play Sound behavior (Asset mode, fired with zero exceptions) and as a level's Ambient Audio (confirmed a real handle exists after Play, confirmed it's torn down to `null` after Stop); round-tripped the whole project through `serializeProject()` → JSON → `loadProjectFull()` and confirmed the Audio Library, Ambient Audio config, and the behavior's `audioAssetId` all survive and correctly re-decode via the async pending-callback bridge; confirmed usage-count-gated deletion correctly refuses while referenced by 2 places (the behavior and the ambient config) at once. Zero console errors throughout; `node tests/run-tests.js` stayed at 23/23 (audio has no dedicated unit tests -- `AudioContext` isn't available in the Node sandbox, same pre-existing limitation the rest of `AudioEngine` already had before this pass).
- **Fixed inspector panel overflow** (reported live by the user: "ui is cutoff needs work like on right side cant read some stuff") -- two real layout bugs, not one: (1) `.field-triple` rows (the trigger/action behavior dropdowns, Duplicate/Delete button pairs) only gave `flex:1` to `<input>` elements in its CSS rule, never `<select>`/`<button>`, so a `<select>` defaulted to its longest `<option>` text as intrinsic width and a `<button>` (`#delete-btn` additionally had its own unconditional `width:100%` rule) simply refused to share the row, pushing content past the panel's right edge with nothing to indicate it was cut off. (2) Separately, a Behaviors-list row summarizing a Custom Script behavior inlines the raw script source with no width constraint or truncation at all. Fixed by making every `.field-triple` child shrink together (`flex:1; min-width:0; box-sizing:border-box`), removing the conflicting fixed-width button rule, and truncating the behavior-summary label with an ellipsis plus a native tooltip (`title`) carrying the full text instead of losing it entirely. Also widened the inspector panel itself (220px -> 260px) for margin. Verified directly: measured real `getBoundingClientRect()`s across cube/terrain/AI-dropdown/Custom-Script inspector states pre- and post-fix -- `inspector.scrollWidth > inspector.clientWidth` (true, overflowing, before) is `false` (no overflow) after, with zero console errors.
- **VERTICAL_CHARACTER_PHYSICS_GATE: PASSED** — bounded gravity/jump/grounded-state, Third-Person controller only, **opt-in per level** (a new Jump checkbox in Player Settings, off by default so every existing Third-Person level's exact prior flat-ground behavior is unchanged). Gravity accelerates a real Y velocity every frame; landing on TERRAIN reuses the existing (already Y-aware) `resolveTerrainCollision` unmodified. Landing on/bonking a **solid object acting as a platform** needed a genuinely new engine primitive — `Opus.Physics.resolveVerticalBoxCollision` — since the existing `resolveBoxCollision` is deliberately XZ-only (documented, tested, and relied on by every other mover). Jump is edge-triggered (Space, or the existing Boost touch button relabeled JUMP when active) so holding the key doesn't repeat-fire mid-air. New public API + a dedicated regression test (`node tests/run-tests.js`, 22/22).
  - **Found and fixed two real interaction bugs live dogfooding a platform-landing scene** (not written into the plan up front — surfaced only by actually jumping onto a platform in the browser): (1) the existing XZ-only wall collision was firing on ANY solid object whose footprint the player was merely *above or below*, shoving a player standing on a platform sideways every frame; gated it to only apply when jump is disabled OR the player's own vertical extent genuinely overlaps the box, matching how a real wall would behave. (2) even with that gate, the exact landing frame still triggered the sideways push (the landing tolerance band reads as "overlapping" too), flinging the player to the box's edge via the horizontal collision's degenerate dead-center fallback and dropping them off the platform on the very frame they landed — fixed by resolving vertical (top/bottom face) contact FIRST and skipping the horizontal wall push entirely for any object that already resolved vertically that frame. See API-FRICTION.md finding #49 for the full diagnosis.
  - Dogfooded live: a player spawned dead-center above a floating solid platform now falls under gravity, lands exactly on the surface (`y = platformTop + halfHeight`) with zero horizontal drift, jumps to the exact configured `jumpSpeed` on a single edge-triggered press (verified no repeat-jump while the key is held, `vy` decaying smoothly under gravity across 15 consecutive frames), arcs back down, and re-lands cleanly. Verified non-jump Third-Person (and every other controller) is byte-for-byte unaffected -- confirmed by re-running the identical scene with Jump disabled and observing the exact original flat-ground/always-a-wall behavior.
  - Known bound, same class already documented for terrain: a fast enough fall combined with a large enough `dt` in one frame can still tunnel through a thin platform without triggering the landing tolerance band -- no sweep/CCD, consistent with every other collision primitive in this engine.
- **`games/skyway-trial.json`** -- the platformer stress game the Vertical Physics Gate unlocks: a Third-Person + Jump level with a 5-platform staircase, a Win Trigger over the final platform, and a Fall Zone Trigger catching missed jumps into Lose. Verified end-to-end both ways (falling = Lose, clearing every platform = Win), including after a fresh round-trip of the exact shipped file. See games/README.md for the full writeup and two more findings surfaced building it (trigger volumes are spheres sized off scale, not boxes; `computeSpawnPoint` doesn't know about solid-cube "ground").
- **Waypoint object + AI Patrol Route** — a new palette item (📍 Waypoint, a green diamond marker, hidden during Play) reusing the existing cube+flag pattern (`isWaypoint: true`, mirroring `isTrigger`) rather than a whole new item kind, so it inherits free drag, the Move gizmo, Duplicate, stable ids, and Save/Load for free. A new **Patrol Route** AI type moves an entity through a comma-separated list of Waypoint *names* (authored in the inspector), resolved once into cached object ids and invalidated whenever the route or Loop/Ping-Pong settings change; supports both **Loop** (wrap to the first point) and **Ping-Pong** (reverse direction at either end) advancement, direct point-to-point (no pathfinding/navmesh, same bounded spirit as the existing single-axis Patrol). Dogfooded live: 4 waypoints placed, a Patrol Route enemy authored entirely through the real inspector (dropdown + fields, not raw data), verified it actually walks leg-to-leg and loops back around (`currentWaypointIndex` cycling 0→1→2→3→0 with real position movement each leg), and reverified movement resumes correctly after a full Save→JSON→Load round-trip.
  - **Fixed a real, pre-existing serialization bug found while building this**: `serializeCubeRecord`'s `ai:` field was a fixed whitelist (`type`/`speed`/`radius`/`base` only) that had always silently dropped `targetId` — meaning a Follow Object/Face Target enemy retargeted onto something other than the Player would silently revert to Follow-the-Player on the very next Save/Load, with no error, since Follow Object shipped in Wave 2. Fixed by adding `targetId`, `waypointNames`, `loop`, `pingpong` to the same whitelist. Verified via direct round-trip: a Patrol Route entity's `targetId`/`waypointNames`/`loop` all survive `serializeProject()` → JSON → `loadProjectFull()` intact.
- **Spawner polish** — **Initial Delay** (separate from the repeat Interval — the first spawn can happen sooner/later than subsequent ones), an optional **Tag** applied to every spawned instance (composes directly with the existing Object Tags system — Count/Hide/Destroy Tagged now work on spawned waves for free), and a **Reset Spawner** action (destroys every currently-active instance and rewinds all counters to zero, for "restart this wave from scratch" rather than just pause/resume). Dogfooded: spawned enemies correctly carried the configured tag; Reset Spawner correctly removed all 3 active instances and zeroed `spawnedTotal`/`activeIds`/the first-spawn flag.
- **Level Transitions** — an optional **Instant** or **Fade** mode (project-wide, with a configurable duration) for every level switch during Play (Switch/Next/Previous/Restart Level). Fade out → switch → fade in, using real wall-clock time rather than the simulation clock, so it works identically whether or not the game happens to be paused at the moment it fires — verified directly (paused the engine, fired Previous Level, confirmed the fade still completed and landed on the right level). **Switch Level** (and the underlying `'level'` field type) now references levels by their new stable **id** instead of by name, with a name-based fallback for behaviors saved before ids existed — fixes the exact rename-fragility the stable-id work above was meant to close. New optional per-level **description** field.
- **Stable Level IDs** — every level now has a permanent `id` (`level1`, `level2`, ...), assigned on creation and reconciled/backfilled on load for every historical project shape. `Duplicate Level` now regenerates a fresh id (and fresh ids for every one of its objects, with every internal reference — `targetId`/`aiTargetId`/`lookAtTargetId`/etc. — remapped to match) rather than risking two levels' data aliasing each other. Collision-safe duplicate naming (`"X Copy"`, `"X Copy 2"`, ...). Dogfooded with real cross-object references (a lever targeting a door by id): duplicating the level produced a lever correctly retargeted at the DUPLICATE's own door, with the original level's reference completely untouched.
- **Project format versioning + single migration path** — exports now stamp `{format: "digital-opus-project", version: 3}`. All project loading (local save, JSON import, embedded standalone project) now funnels through one `normalizeProjectData()` — replacing the three separately-inlined shape checks `loadProjectFull` used to do — which still accepts every historical shape (raw array, `{objects, prefabs}`, unversioned `{levels, ...}`) and never mutates the caller's original parsed object. An unrecognized *newer* version fails with a clear error instead of guessing. Re-verified all 3 existing Wave 2 stress games (`relic-island.json`, `dungeon-descent.json`, `survival-arena-2.json`) still load correctly and get backfilled stable level ids.
- **Project Asset Library (mesh assets)** — Import Model now creates a reusable **asset** (one immutable geometry payload, cached as a real GPU-uploadable mesh built once) instead of embedding a full independent geometry copy into every placed instance. New Assets panel: import, list (type/vertex count/usage count), Place (adds another instance sharing the same mesh), Rename, Delete (refuses with a clear message while any object still references it). `Opus.Mesh.toArrays`/`fromArrays` (already public) do the round-trip; nothing new needed at the engine layer. Dogfooded with a real imported mesh: placed 20 instances, confirmed all 20 share the literal same mesh object (not 20 separate GPU buffers), and measured the actual export-size win — **71.6% smaller** than the naive per-instance-embedding equivalent for just 20 instances of a 48-vertex mesh (savings scale up further with instance count and mesh complexity).
- **Fixed a real regression in Standalone Export**, caught by this batch's own dogfooding: the dev-time cache-busting fix applied to `editor/index.html`'s script tag earlier this session (see API-FRICTION #46) silently broke the export's own `<script>`-tag string-replace, which was still matching the old plain-tag pattern — Standalone Export would have shipped a broken `document.write` call pointing at a relative path that doesn't exist in a single exported file. Fixed with a regex anchored on a unique comment string (not a generic `<script>` tag, which the first replace's own large inlined blob could otherwise cause the second replace to latch onto instead of the intended one — an actual failure mode hit and diagnosed live during this fix). Reverified: exported HTML boots as a real page (not just an assertion) — Crate Run renders and plays, and "Edit This Game" reveals a fully working embedded editor.

### Added
- Formal release roadmap with reliability, developer-experience, content-pipeline, rendering, and 1.0 milestones.
- Dependency-free public API regression suite covering math, procedural sampling, physics, scene/entity ownership, particles, input cleanup, settings, and save slots.
- Real-browser WebGL2 smoke harness covering App rendering, pause/resume, scene ownership, and terminal cleanup.
- One-command build-and-test runner at `node tests/run-tests.js`.
- **Editor: Custom Script trust gate** — loading or importing a project containing Custom Script behaviors now requires an explicit "Enable Scripts" opt-in before any script runs (AUDIT.md finding #1).
- **Editor: Import Project JSON** — a file-picker import alongside the existing localStorage Load Project, both routed through the same trust-gated `loadProjectData`.
- **glTF import allocation bound** — `Opus.Mesh.fromGLTF` now rejects unreasonably large accessors before allocating, instead of after (AUDIT.md finding #2).
- **Editor: Object References** — every placed item now has a stable id and an optional user-editable name, surviving rename/duplicate/Save-Load/Undo-Redo; `findItemById` resolves references gracefully when a target has been deleted.
- **Editor: shared action-execution core** — the fixed trigger/action dropdown and the Node Graph now dispatch through one `ACTION_TYPES` table and `executeAction`, instead of two separate implementations. Expanded the action vocabulary (available in both the dropdown and the graph): Move Object, Set Position, Rotate Object, Show/Hide Object, Enable/Disable Object, Destroy Object, Change Color, Set Counter, Increment Counter — all support targeting another placed object via the new target-reference dropdown, not just the object whose behavior fired.
- **Editor: Trigger Volumes** — a new palette item placing an invisible-in-play trigger region, independent of any gameplay-visible object; supports On Enter and the new On Exit trigger (now available on any object, not just Trigger Volumes), plus an optional one-shot ("Repeat" off) mode.
- **Editor: Prefabs** — save any placed object as a reusable, named prefab (persisted independently of any one project) from the inspector, and place instances of it from a new Prefabs section in the palette, alongside the built-in object types.
- **Editor: AI Behavior** — a new inspector field on any object: Follow (chases the craft), Orbit (circles its base point), or Wander (random drift within a radius). Also added Decrement Counter, completing a generic counter-based primitive usable for health/lives/score.
- **Editor: Grouping** — a Group/Ungroup control in the multi-select inspector; clicking any one grouped member reselects the whole group, so it can be moved/duplicated/deleted together without reselecting by hand each time. Persists through Save/Load.
- **Editor: Spawn Prefab action** — instantiate a saved prefab at runtime (relative to the firing object's position), fully wired with live behaviors/triggers immediately — enemy waves, respawning pickups, and similar dynamic content are now possible without restarting Play.
- **Editor: On Start trigger** — fires once per Play session before the craft spawns, for initializing counters (starting health/lives/score). Named counters and runtime-spawned items now also reset properly between Play/Stop cycles instead of silently persisting.
- **Editor: Counter At Most condition** — a Node Graph condition (`value <= target`) alongside the existing Counter Reaches (`value >= target`), for a counter that counts down to a floor (health, lives) rather than up to a threshold.
- **`games/survival-arena.json`** — a second stress-test game (import via Project → Import Project JSON): AI "Hunter" enemies chase the player, colliding costs health, health reaching 0 loses; collecting all 4 orbs wins.
- **Editor: Checkpoints/Respawn** — Set Checkpoint and Respawn At Checkpoint actions, for hazards that knock the player back instead of ending the game outright.
- **`games/temple-switch-puzzle.json`** — a third stress-test game: two one-shot switches must both be activated (stress-testing object references and cross-object logic) before a gate trigger accepts Win.
- **`games/crate-run-expanded.patch.js`** — Game A: a real evolution of the shipped Crate Run (not just more islands) — a 3-life system with checkpoint respawn replaces instant Lose on hazard touch, applied as a patch over the existing starter game.
- **Portable prefabs** — Export Project now bundles any prefab referenced via Spawn Prefab into the exported file; importing merges bundled prefabs into the recipient's local library (additive only — never overwrites an existing local prefab of the same name). Closes the gap recorded in API-FRICTION.md finding #33.
- **Export Standalone Game** — produces a single self-contained, double-clickable HTML file (engine + editor inlined, no adjacent `dist/`/`src/` folders needed) that boots straight into the current project, with the same "Edit This Game" toggle Crate Run itself ships with.
- **Solid objects** — a new `Physics.resolveBoxCollision` API plus a per-object "Solid" inspector checkbox: any cube can now physically block the craft (horizontally). An invisible solid object doesn't block, so Show/Hide Object doubles as a real door mechanic. Game B's Temple Switch Puzzle now has a genuine blocking door instead of a logic-only gate.
- **Rotation and non-uniform scale fields** — the inspector gained a Rotation Y field (degrees) and independent Scale X/Y/Z fields (was uniform-only), for shapes like a wide thin wall that weren't reachable through the UI before.
- **Multiple named local projects** — a new `SaveSystem.list()` API plus a Project Name field, Saved Projects dropdown, and Load/Delete Selected buttons, replacing the single hardcoded `'editor-project'` save slot.
- **On-screen Move gizmo** — click-and-drag X/Y/Z axis handles for the selected object, in addition to the existing free XZ-plane drag (which had no Y control at all). Undo-able as a single step.
- **Multiple levels within one project** — a Levels panel (Add/Rename/Switch/Delete) plus a Switch Level action/Node Graph node for gameplay-driven transitions (carries score/lives across the switch, fires the new level's On Start behaviors). Export/Save/Load/Import/Standalone-Export all now save and restore every level, while still loading every older single-level project/save unchanged.
- **Pause** — a Pause/Resume button and overlay during Play, surfacing the engine's existing (but previously unreachable, since the editor runs with `hud: false`) `App.pause()`/`resume()`.
- **Environment settings** — new `App.setEnvironment()`/`getEnvironment()` API (sky/fog/sun colors, fog density, light direction — previously hardcoded, though already fully rendered) plus an editor panel for Sky/Fog color, Sun color, and Fog density, kept per level.
- **Particle Emitter authoring** — a new placeable object type surfacing `App.createEmitter` (already existed, already used for the craft's own trail effects) as a no-code decoration: Position, Color, Rate/Spread, Life range, Rise Speed range, and Gravity, editable live in the inspector.

- **Rotate gizmo handle** — a fourth on-screen handle (cube-only) for click-and-drag rotation around Y, alongside the existing Move gizmo.

### Capability Expansion Wave 2 (in progress)
- **Generalized Player Controller system** — the editor no longer has "the craft" hardcoded as the only possible player; a `PLAYER_CONTROLLERS` registry plus a generic `player` accessor (entity/velocity/radius/controllerType) now sits between gameplay code (camera, AI Follow, checkpoints, triggers, the Boost action) and whichever controller is actually spawned. The existing hovercraft was ported into this registry with its proven movement/collision/hover math completely unchanged (verified via `node tests/run-tests.js` and live dogfooding in Crate Run — same 21-crate map, same terrain/solid-object/boundary collision, same chase camera). Two new controllers were added on top of the same registry: **Top-Down** (direct WASD/touch movement on the XZ plane, optional rotate-to-movement, terrain-following, solid collision) and **Third-Person** (camera-relative move/strafe, grounded, chase camera, solid collision) — both reuse the exact same `Opus.Physics` primitives (`resolveTerrainCollision`, `resolveBoxCollision`, `applyMapBoundary`) the hovercraft already used, rather than reimplementing collision. A new per-level **Player panel** (controller type + Move Speed/Acceleration for the two new controllers) lets a level's controller be authored and saved/exported/imported alongside its existing Environment settings, with old single-controller projects defaulting to Hovercraft unchanged.
- **Camera authoring** — `Camera` (engine) gained two new modes alongside the existing Orbit/Chase: **Fixed** (a static eye/look-at point, e.g. a cinematic shot) and **Follow Look-At** (a static eye that smoothly tracks a moving target, camera itself doesn't move). Both reuse the existing `chaseOptions.lerpSpeed` for target smoothing rather than adding a near-duplicate option. On the editor side, a new placeable **Camera object** (editor-only marker, hidden in Play like a Trigger Volume) authors a named camera preset — mode, FOV, chase distance/height/smoothing, and an optional Look-At object reference — and a new **Change Camera** action (available in both the fixed dropdown and Node Graph, via a new `cameraPreset` field type) applies it to the live `app.camera` at runtime. Dogfooded end-to-end: a Trigger Volume wired to Change Camera correctly swaps `app.camera.mode`/eye/target/FOV when the player crosses it, and Follow Look-At correctly keeps the eye fixed while the target smoothly tracks the live player position.
- **Transform Gizmos 2.0** — the single always-on Move+Rotate gizmo became three explicit **Move / Rotate / Scale modes**, switched via a new on-canvas toolbar (with W/E/R shortcuts, disabled while typing in a field) so only one tool's handles are ever visible/pickable at once. Scale adds three new per-axis handles (X/Y/Z, cube-only — reuses the exact same closest-point-on-ray-to-segment pick math the Move axes already used) plus a fourth **uniform-scale handle** that scales all three axes proportionally from one drag. All three modes now support optional **snapping** (position/rotation-degrees/scale increments; off by default, the on/off flag persisted to `localStorage`) via a Snap checkbox in the same toolbar. Verified live: real synthetic pointer drags on each new handle (dispatched at the handle's actual projected screen position, the same way the earlier Move-gizmo fix was verified) correctly scaled a single axis, correctly scaled all three axes proportionally from the uniform handle, and correctly snapped a Rotate drag to a 15° increment.
- **Player Health/Damage system** — `window.__gameState.health`/`.maxHealth` graduated from a bare counter-naming convention to first-class actions: **Damage Player** (amount, optional Instant Kill, optional Cooldown for repeat-on-stay use), **Heal Player**, and **Set Player Health** (with an optional Max Health field), all clamped to `[0, maxHealth]` and auto-initialized on first use via `ensureHealthInit()` — still just the same `__gameState` keys underneath, so the existing generic Counter Reaches/At Most conditions keep working on `'health'` unmodified. Added **Health At Least/At Most** Node Graph conditions (fixed key, no free-typed counter name to typo). A new **Max Health** field on the per-level Player panel sets the starting value. **Damage Zones** are now buildable from existing primitives: `Physics.TriggerVolume` gained an `onStay` callback (fires every frame the point is inside, engine-level addition) and the editor gained a matching **On Stay** trigger, so a Trigger Volume + On Stay + Damage Player (with its own Cooldown field) is a full damage-over-time zone with no new object type needed. Dogfooded end-to-end: a real Damage Zone correctly ticked damage on a cooldown (not every frame), Heal/Set Health clamped correctly at both ends, Instant Kill zeroed health in one hit, and both new conditions evaluated correctly.
- **Named Timers** — `window.__gameTimers[name]` (elapsed/duration/countDown/running/finished), ticked once per frame from inside the same pause-gated update loop Player physics already uses (simulation-time and pause-safe for free). New **Start/Stop/Reset/Set Timer** actions and **Timer Finished / At Least / At Most** Node Graph conditions. Dogfooded: a 2-second count-down timer correctly reached 0, stopped itself, and both conditions read it correctly.
- **Node Graph Delay/Wait** — a graph-only **Delay** node that pauses just that one sequence (not the whole game) for N simulation seconds before continuing to the next node. `runGraph` is now resumable (`runGraph(graph, item, startId)`); a Delay node registers `{resumeAt, graph, item, fromId}` in `pendingDelays` and returns, and the main update loop's new `processPendingDelays()` resumes any that are due. Simulation-time (pause-safe), cleared on both Play start and Stop (no leak across sessions), and skipped if its item was destroyed mid-delay. Dogfooded: a 3-node graph (set counter → delay 1s → set counter again) ran the first node immediately, correctly held at the delay with the third node NOT yet run, then completed automatically once the delay elapsed; a fresh Delay registered then immediately Stopped confirmed the pending queue is actually cleared, not just left to expire.
- **Node Graph Wave 2** — five new conditions: **NOT** (a combinator like AND/OR, reads one operand via a `cond` edge), **Object Visible**, and **Counter Equals/Less Than/Greater Than** (joining the existing Reaches `>=`/At Most `<=`). Graph editor QoL: connections can now be **deleted directly** (click the line — a wide invisible hit-line sits over the thin visible one, since a 2px stroke was too thin to click reliably), nodes can be **duplicated** (same field values, fresh id, no connections carried over) via a new per-node button, and the Add-node toolbar gained a **search/filter** box (39 node types was becoming a lot to scan). Dogfooded all five end-to-end: a `Counter Reaches` → `NOT` chain correctly flipped as the underlying counter crossed its threshold, `Object Visible` correctly tracked a live visibility toggle, duplicate produced an independent copy with the same field values, and a real click on a connection's hit-line removed exactly that edge.
- **Deferred, with reason**: pan/zoom, a visible grid with node-snapping, multi-select, and hover-based connection-highlighting were in the Wave 2 QoL list but not built this pass — the graph canvas is plain DOM-absolute-positioned nodes with no viewport/coordinate-transform layer yet, so pan/zoom specifically would need that transform system built first, not just a new control; not attempted at low confidence rather than shipped half-working.
- **Named boolean Flags** — `window.__gameFlags[name]` plus **Set Flag True/False**, **Toggle Flag** actions and **Is Flag True/False** Node Graph conditions, for pure on/off state (a switch thrown, a door unlocked) that would be an odd fit for a numeric counter.
- **Object Tags** — a new per-cube **Tags** inspector field (comma-separated, distinct from the stable per-object `id` Object References already use), round-tripped through serialize/duplicate/prefab-instantiate. New **Hide Tagged**, **Destroy Tagged**, and **Count Tagged Into Counter** actions operate on every object sharing a tag at once. Dogfooded: tagged two crates `breakable` (one also `enemy`), Count Tagged correctly counted 2, Hide Tagged correctly hid only the `enemy`-tagged one, Destroy Tagged correctly removed both `breakable`-tagged objects from the scene.
- **Particle actions and presets** — **Start/Stop/Burst Emitter** actions (Stop saves the emitter's own rate per-item before zeroing it, so multiple stopped emitters don't clobber each other's remembered rate; Burst is a direct pass-through to the engine's existing `ParticleEmitter.emit(count)`, which was already public but unreachable from the editor). A new **Preset** dropdown on the Particle Emitter inspector (Smoke/Sparks/Fire/Magic/Dust/Water Spray/Burst) one-click-applies tuned rate/life/riseSpeed/spread/size/color/gravity values. Dogfooded: applying Fire set the expected color/gravity/rate on both the live handle and the saved options; Stop correctly zeroed the rate, Start correctly restored it, and Burst added exactly the requested particle count.
- **Tween actions** — **Move To**, **Rotate To**, and **Scale To** (all "(Tween)" — duration + easing: linear/easeIn/easeOut/easeInOut), animating a position/rotationY/scale change over time instead of snapping instantly. A second tween on the same object+property replaces the first outright rather than blending (predictable: "the door was already closing, now open it instead" just works). Simulation-time and pause-safe (same `app.onUpdate`-gated `tickTweens()` pattern as Timers/Delay), cleared on both Play start and Stop, and dropped (not resumed) if the target entity was destroyed mid-tween. Dogfooded as a real door: a solid cube tweened from its closed position up to an open one with easeOut, landed exactly on target, and a concurrent Rotate To + Scale To on the same object both completed correctly at their own independent target values.
- **Runtime Save Game / Player Progress** — distinct from the editor's own project save (a different `Opus.SaveSystem` namespace, `doengine:playersave` vs the editor's project-save namespaces): **Save Game**, **Load Game**, and **Clear Save** actions capture/restore level index, checkpoint, all counters/flags, and last known player position under a named slot. Reuses the existing public `Opus.SaveSystem` (already used for editor project saves) rather than a second storage mechanism. Dogfooded: saved mid-session state (a counter, a flag, a specific player position), overwrote all three in memory, then Load Game restored all three exactly; Clear Save confirmed the slot was actually gone (`SaveSystem.has()` false afterward), not just overwritten with empty data.
- **Enemy AI Wave 2** — **Flee** (runs away from the Player, only while within Radius — stands still once safely clear, since Flee is a reaction, not a full-time behavior swap), **Face Target** (rotation-only tracking, no movement, for a turret/statue), and **Follow Object** (the existing Follow behavior gained an optional Target field — any placed object, not just the Player — defaulting to the Player when unset, so every pre-existing Follow-the-Player enemy keeps working unmodified). No pathfinding/navmesh added, per the explicit "don't turn this into Unity" constraint — these are direct-steering reactions, same spirit as the existing Orbit/Wander. Dogfooded: a Flee enemy correctly ran from a nearby player and stopped once past its radius (distance settled at exactly the radius, 10 units); a Face Target turret's rotation matched the exact geometric angle to an off-axis player position (45°) while its own position stayed completely fixed.
- **Deferred, with reason**: Patrol Points via ordered Waypoint objects were in the Wave 2 AI list but not built — Patrol (single-axis back-and-forth) already exists and covers the common case; ordered multi-point patrol routes need a new placeable Waypoint object type plus per-enemy ordered-list authoring UI, which is a larger, separate feature rather than an extension of the existing AI dropdown.
- **Generic Interact** — a new **On Interact** trigger, fired by pressing F (desktop) or a new auto-appearing touch button, against whichever interactable object is nearest the Player within range (closest wins if several overlap). A DOM interaction prompt ("Press F to interact") and the touch button both appear only while something is actually in range. Reuses the existing behavior/action dispatch (`runBehaviors(item, 'interact')`) — no new action vocabulary, any existing action can be an Interact response. Dogfooded: walked into range of an NPC cube with an On Interact → Show Message behavior, confirmed the prompt appeared, then confirmed BOTH the real F key and a real touch-button pointerdown independently fired the same message.
- **Inventory-lite** — `window.__gameInventory[itemName]`, the same plain-named-state shape as counters/flags. New **Give Item**/**Remove Item** actions and a **Has Item** condition (minimum count, default 1) — e.g. gating a door's Open action on holding a key, combinable with the existing Interact trigger and Tween/Show-Hide actions with no new authoring concepts. Dogfooded: gave 1 key, confirmed Has Item(1) true / Has Item(2) false, removed it, confirmed Has Item(1) correctly flipped back to false.
- **Level Management 2.0** — **Duplicate Level** (deep clone, not shared references — objects/environment/player settings/HUD all copied independently), **Move Up/Move Down** (reorders levels, correctly remapping both the currently-open-for-editing level and the Start Level through the swap), and a **Start Level** designation (★ in the level list) distinct from "which level is currently open for editing" — a fresh Play session now always boots from the Start Level, not whichever level a project author happened to have open. New **Next Level**/**Previous Level**/**Restart Level** actions alongside the existing Switch Level. Dogfooded end-to-end: duplicated a level and confirmed independent object arrays; moved the Start Level and confirmed the ★ and `startLevelIndex` correctly followed it, not the slot; entered Play with a *different* level open for editing and confirmed it correctly booted the Start Level instead; fired Next/Previous Level and confirmed the level actually changed; hid an object, fired Restart Level, and confirmed it came back (a level restart re-reads the level's saved objects, and visibility was never part of what gets saved, so "restart" and "undo pickups" come for free from the same mechanism).
- **Dialogue system** — a bounded, deterministic speaker/text modal (**Show Dialogue** action) with up to two optional choice buttons; each choice sets its own named **Flag** rather than introducing a second action-execution path, so downstream Node Graph logic (Is Flag True/False, already built) decides what a choice actually does — no new branching vocabulary. Reuses `app.pause()`/`resume()` (same proven mechanism as the Start Screen) so nothing moves, damages, or spawns while dialogue is up. Omitting both choice labels falls back to a single "Continue" button. Dogfooded: a two-choice dialogue paused the game and showed exactly the two configured buttons; clicking one closed the box, resumed the game, and set exactly that choice's flag (not the other); a no-choice dialogue correctly showed a single Continue button and resumed on click.
- **Gamepad support** — a small generic mapping layer (`pollGamepad`, called every play-mode frame) onto the exact same key codes every Player Controller/Boost/Interact already reads via `Input.simulateKeyDown/Up` — left stick + D-pad map to WASD, face buttons to Space (boost) and F (interact) — so no controller or action has any gamepad-specific code. Edge-triggered against its own remembered held-key set, not polled-and-blindly-released every frame, specifically so an idle/disconnected gamepad can never fight a real keyboard press on the same mapped key (both share one underlying key-state Set). Dogfooded with a mocked `navigator.getGamepads`: stick-up + button 0 correctly simulated KeyW+Space down, releasing the pad correctly released both, and — the one that would have been a real bug — polling an *idle* gamepad while a real keyboard key was independently held confirmed the real key stayed down, untouched.
- **Scene List improvements** — a **search/filter** box (filters by the same display name shown in the list) and a per-row **visibility toggle** (👁/🚫, entity-backed kinds only — terrain/water/emitter have no on/off visual state to toggle) that doesn't also trigger row selection. Dogfooded: filtering to "boost" correctly showed only the 5 Boost Pad rows out of the full scene; the toggle correctly flipped a real object's visibility and left selection behavior on the rest of the row untouched.
- **Spawner object** — a new placeable (editor-only marker, hidden in Play) that spawns a chosen prefab on its own interval at runtime: Prefab, Interval, Radius (random scatter), Max Active, Total Count (0 = unlimited), and Enabled-at-start. Generalizes the "wave" pattern (previously a hand-wired Node Graph Delay chain, see Survival Arena 2.0) into a reusable object. New **Start/Stop Spawner** and **Spawn Once** actions. Reuses `instantiateCubeRecord`/`wireItemTrigger` — a spawner-produced instance is indistinguishable from one placed by hand. Dogfooded with a real 2-wave-style scenario: Max Active correctly capped concurrent spawns at 3; destroying one correctly triggered a same-tick-detected refill (dead ids pruned from the active set before each tick); Total Count correctly stopped spawning at exactly 5 total even with only 1 left alive; Stop Spawner correctly halted auto-spawning while Spawn Once still fired a real manual spawn on top.
- **HUD/UI authoring** — a per-level HUD Elements list (Text Label, Counter Display, Timer Display, Health Bar, Objective Text, Button, Panel), each with a 9-way anchor (top/middle/bottom × left/center/right) and pixel offset, rendered fresh every play-mode frame from live `window.__gameState`/`__gameTimers`/health bindings (`{value}`/`{counterName}` placeholders) — the same state every other Wave 2 system already reads, so a HUD label and a Node Graph condition can reference the same counter with no separate wiring. A Button element fires any existing action (reuses `ACTION_TYPES` directly, no new action vocabulary). New **Show/Hide/Set HUD Text** actions target elements via a new `hudElementRef` field type. A configurable **Start Screen** (title/subtitle/button text, off by default) reuses the existing proven Pause plumbing (`app.pause()`/`resume()`) rather than inventing a second "input blocked" concept, so the player genuinely can't act before dismissing it, not just visually can't see the game. **Win/Lose screens** gained optional per-level title/message overrides (falling back to the original hardcoded "🏆 You Win!"/"💀 Game Over" when unset, so no existing project changes behavior). Dogfooded end-to-end: added Counter/Health/Button elements through the real Add-element UI, confirmed live rendering and binding resolution in Play, confirmed the Start Screen genuinely paused the engine and resumed correctly on dismiss, confirmed a configured Win title actually displayed, and confirmed a HUD Button click fired its wired action for real.

### Fixed
- **Play Sound's Frequency field** — silently non-functional since the shared action-execution core refactor (every fixed-dropdown Play Sound behavior played a hardcoded 660Hz regardless of the field's value). Now works, and gained Duration and Waveform fields alongside it.

### Changed
- Bundle size remains reported but no longer has a hard 81,920-byte build limit.
- Node Graph node ids now reconcile against the graph being opened, preventing an id collision after a fresh page load (AUDIT.md finding #3).

## [0.1.0] - 2026-07-30

### Added
- **Public API Surface (`window.Opus`)**: Complete documented entry point (`Opus.create`, `App`, `Mesh`, `Terrain`, `Water`, `Physics`, `AudioEngine`, `HUD`, `Camera`).
- **Playable Demo (`dist/delivery-run.html`)**: 90-second Hovercraft Delivery Run demo game built 100% through the public engine API.
- **Chase Camera System**: Added target entity following with smooth position/rotation lerping and lookahead to `Camera`.
- **Physics Enhancements**: Added `Physics.resolveTerrainCollision` (steep slope cliff sliding & terrain height locking) and `Physics.applyMapBoundary` (soft pushback).
- **Procedural Audio Vehicle Hum**: Added node-recycling `AudioEngine.startEngineHum()`, `updateEngineHum()`, and game stingers (`playPickup`, `playDelivery`, `playWin`, `playLose`, `playBoost`).
- **HUD Primitives & Overlays**: Added `hud.addLabel`, `addPanel`, `showTitleCard`, `showControlsOverlay`, `showWinModal`, `showLoseModal`, `showWarning`.
- **Starter Template**: Added `template/index.html` and `template/game.js`.
- **Tutorial Examples**: Added 5 self-contained HTML examples in `examples/` (`01-hello-scene` to `05-mini-game`).
- **Documentation**: Added `getting-started.md`, `api-reference.md`, `architecture.md`, `API-FRICTION.md`, and `LICENSE`.

### Changed
- Mesh factories now return renderer-independent descriptions; raw WebGL state is private.
- Input, resize, animation, HUD timer, audio timer, particle, scene, and GPU cleanup are App-owned.
- Water sampling returns world-space height and offers surface normals/intensity/foam.
- Pause keeps rendering active while freezing simulation so UI resume remains available.
- Simulation elapsed time and App-created water freeze together while paused.
- Trigger volumes support swept high-speed crossings through an optional previous position.
- Scene removal destroys entities, chase targets clear safely, and post-destroy App calls report useful errors.

### Known limitations
- WebGL2-only; no fallback renderer.
- Developer-preview API may change after v0.1.0.
- Primitive procedural geometry only; no asset importers, editor, mobile/touch controls, or gamepad support.
- One directional light and compact material model.
